is a Nisnevich distinguished square of usual schemes since the morphisms
f
and
g
in (
3.1.2
) are strict.
We also note that
Q
is a pullback of
\underline{Q}
,
i.e.,

Y\simeq\underline{Y}\times_{\underline{X}}X,\;Y^{\prime}\simeq\underline{Y^{\prime}}\times_{\underline{X}}X,\;X^{\prime}\simeq\underline{X^{\prime}}\times_{\underline{X}}X.

Remark 3.1.7
.

(1)

Suppose
f:Y\rightarrow X
is a surjective proper log étale monomorphism.
Theorem
A.6.2
shows that Zariski locally on
X
and
Y
,
the restriction

f^{-1}(X-\partial X)\rightarrow X-\partial X

has a chart
\theta:P\rightarrow Q
,
where
P=0
is trivial and
Q
is finite.
Thus
f^{-1}(X-\partial X)
has a trivial log structure, since
\overline{Q}=0
,
so that
Y-\partial Y=f^{-1}(X-\partial X)
and the induced morphism
Y-\partial Y\rightarrow X-\partial X
is surjective.
The latter morphism is an isomorphism since surjective proper étale monomorphisms of schemes are isomorphisms according to
[
30
, Théorème IV.17.9.1]
.

(2)

For cdh-distinguished squares of schemes
[
92
, Section 2]
,
the schemes
Y^{\prime}
and
X^{\prime}
in (
3.1.2
) can be nonempty.
Thus the dNis-structure is not a direct analog of the cdh-structure
[
91
]
.

(3)

We choose the terminology “dividing” because morphisms of fs log schemes induced by subdivisions of fans in toric geometry are surjective proper log étale monomorphisms.
For a proof we refer to Proposition
A.11.9
and Example
A.11.8
.

(4)

A presheaf
\mathcal{F}
is a dividing sheaf if and only if for every log modification
Y\rightarrow X
the induced homomorphism
\mathcal{F}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(Y)
is an isomorphism.

3.2.
Cohomology on big and small sites

If the base scheme
S
has trivial log structure and
\mathcal{F}
is an étale sheaf of
\Lambda
-modules on
Sm/S
,
then there are two equivalent ways of defining the cohomology group
H^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})
for any integer
i\geq 0
, namely

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{\acute{e}t}(X_{\acute{e}t},\Lambda))}(\Lambda(X),\mathcal{F}|_{X_{\acute{e}t}}[i])\text{ and }\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{\acute{e}t}(Sm/S,\Lambda))}(\Lambda(X),\mathcal{F}[i]).

In this section, we generalize the above comparison to the topologies we consider on
lSm/k
.
As an application, we shall discuss compact objects in the corresponding categories of sheaves.

Definition 3.2.1
.

A morphism of fs log schemes
Y\rightarrow X
is a
dividing Nisnevich

(resp.
dividing étale
) morphism if it is contained in the smallest class of morphisms containing log modifications and strict Nisnevich (resp. strict étale)
morphisms and closed under compositions.

Definition 3.2.2
.

If
X
is an fs log scheme, let
X_{sNis}
(resp.
X_{s\acute{e}t}
,
X_{k\acute{e}t}
,
X_{dNis}
,
X_{d\acute{e}t}
,
X_{l\acute{e}t}
) denote the full subcategory of
lSch/X
consisting
of fs log schemes strict Nisnevich (resp. strict étale, Kummer étale, dividing Nisnevich, dividing étale, log étale) over
X
.

Note that these classes of morphisms are closed under compositions and pullbacks.
Due to Lemma
A.11.2
for every log étale morphism
Z\rightarrow Y
of fs log schemes, the diagonal morphism
Z\rightarrow Z\times_{Y}Z
is an open immersion.
Thus all these categories have finite limits.

Lemma 3.2.3
.

If
X
is an fs log scheme, every morphism in
X_{sNis}
(resp.
X_{s\acute{e}t}
,
X_{k\acute{e}t}
,
X_{dNis}
,
X_{d\acute{e}t}
,
X_{l\acute{e}t}
) is strict Nisnevich
(resp. strict étale, Kummer étale, dividing Nisnevich, dividing étale, log étale).

Démonstration.

We focus on the dividing Nisnevich case since the proofs are similar.
Let
\mathscr{P}
denote the class of dividing Nisnevich morphisms.
Suppose that
g:Z\rightarrow Y
and
f:Y\rightarrow X
be morphisms of fs log schemes such that
f,fg\in\mathscr{P}
.
We claim that
g\in\mathscr{P}
.
There exists a commutative diagram :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\times_{X}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times_{X}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\times{\rm id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rm id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

Here
p
and
q
are the second projections,
\Delta
is the diagonal morphism, and the left square is cartesian.
Since
f
is log étale, as noted above
\Delta\in\mathscr{P}
.
It follows that
\Gamma,q\in\mathscr{P}
because
\mathscr{P}
is closed under pullbacks.
This shows that
g=q\circ\Gamma\in\mathscr{P}
.
∎

Let
t
be one of the following topologies on
lSm/S
:

t=sNis,\;s\acute{e}t,\;dNis,\;d\acute{e}t,\;k\acute{e}t,\textrm{ and }\;l\acute{e}t.

We have an inclusion functor

\eta\colon X_{t}\rightarrow lSm/S

sending
Y\in X_{t}
to
Y
.
We regard
lSm/S
as a site for the topology
t
.
Since
X_{t}
has fiber products and the functor
\eta
commutes with fiber products,

[
2
, Proposition III.1.6]
shows that
\eta
is a continuous functor of sites.
If
Y\in X_{t}
, then every
t
-covering
\{U_{i}\rightarrow Y\}_{i\in I}
in
lSm/S
has a refinement in
X_{t}
, so
\eta
is a cocontinuous functor of sites.
Then
[
2
, Sections IV.4.7, IV.4.9]
gives adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(X_{t},\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(lSm/S,\Lambda),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{*}}

(3.2.1)

where
\eta^{*}
is the restriction functor,
\eta_{\sharp}
is left adjoint to
\eta^{*}
, and
\eta_{*}
is right adjoint to
\eta^{*}
.
Note that
\eta^{*}
is exact.

There are naturally induced adjoint functors

\eta_{\sharp}:\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(X_{t},\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(lSm/S,\Lambda)):\eta^{*}.

(3.2.2)

We impose the descent model structures on both categories, see Proposition
B.1.4
.
By virtue of Example
B.1.7
an object
\mathcal{F}
in
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(X_{t},\Lambda))
(resp.
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(lSm/S,\Lambda))
) is fibrant if for every
t
-hypercover
\mathscr{Y}\rightarrow Y
in
X_{t}
(resp.
lSm/S
) there is a natural quasi-isomorphism

\mathcal{F}(Y)\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\rm Tot}^{\pi}\mathcal{F}(\mathscr{Y}).

(3.2.3)

Moreover,
a morphism
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
in
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(X_{t},\Lambda))
(resp.
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(lSm/S,\Lambda))
) is a fibration if it is a degreewise epimorphism and its kernel is fibrant.
If
Y\in X_{t}
, then every
t
-hypercovering of
Y
in
lSm/S
is again a
t
-hypercovering in
X_{t}
.
Moreover, since
\eta^{*}
is exact,
\eta^{*}
commutes with
{\rm Tot}^{\pi}
.
Hence from the description of fibrant objects (
3.2.3
) we deduce that
\eta^{*}
preserves fibrant objects.
Use again the fact that
\eta^{*}
is exact to deduce that
\eta^{*}
preserves fibrations and trivial fibrations.
Thus (
3.2.2
) is a Quillen adjunction.
By passing to homotopy categories we obtain the adjoint functors

L\eta_{\sharp}:\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(X_{t},\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(lSm/S,\Lambda)):R\eta^{*}.

(3.2.4)

Since
\eta^{*}
is exact,
R\eta^{*}\simeq\eta^{*}
.

Proposition 3.2.4
.

With
t
is as above, for every complex of
t
-sheaves
\mathcal{F}
, there is a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(lSm/S,\Lambda))}(a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X),\eta^{*}\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(X_{t},\Lambda))}(a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X),\mathcal{F}).

Démonstration.

Since
a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X)
is cofibrant, there is an isomorphism

L\eta_{\sharp}a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X)\simeq a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X).

To conclude, we use the adjunction (
3.2.4
).
∎

Proposition 3.2.5
.

With
t
is as above, suppose that
X
is a noetherian fs log scheme.
Then the topos
\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(X_{t})
is coherent.

Démonstration.

Since
X_{t}
has fiber products, owing to
[
2
, Proposition VI.2.1]
, it suffices to show that every object of
X_{t}
is quasi-compact.
The log étale topology is finer than
t
, and
X_{t}
is a full subcategory of
X_{l\acute{e}t}
.
Hence it remains to show that every object of
X_{l\acute{e}t}
is quasi-compact.
For this we refer to
[
70
, Proposition 3.14(1)]
.
∎

Definition 3.2.6
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a category with a topology
\tau
.
We say that
\mathcal{C}
has
finite
\tau
-cohomological dimension
for
\Lambda
-linear coefficients if for every fixed
X\in\mathcal{C}
,
there exists an integer
N
such that for every
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{Shv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C},\Lambda)
and integer
i>N
we have the vanishing

H_{\tau}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})=0.

Proposition 3.2.7
.

With
t
as above, suppose that
S
is noetherian and
lSm/S
has finite
t
-cohomological dimension for
\Lambda
-linear coefficients.
Then for every
X\in lSm/S
, the functor

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(lSm/S,\Lambda))}(a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X),-)\colon\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(lSm/S,\Lambda))\rightarrow\Lambda\text{-}\mathbf{Mod}

commutes with small sums.
In particular, the category

\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(lSm/S,\Lambda)

is compactly generated.

Démonstration.

By virtue of Proposition
3.2.4
, it suffices to show that the functor

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(X_{t},\Lambda))}(a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X),-)\colon\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(X_{t},\Lambda))\rightarrow\Lambda\text{-}\mathbf{Mod}

commutes with small sums.
By assumption
\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(X_{t},\Lambda)
has finite
t
-cohomological dimension for
\Lambda
-linear coefficients, and
X_{t}
is coherent due to Proposition
3.2.5
.
Then apply
[
20
, Proposition 1.9]
to conclude.
∎

3.3.
The dividing density structure

In addition to completeness and regularity,
Voevodsky
[
91
]
also introduced the notion of a bounded cd-structure.
In tandem,
these properties ensure the associated topology has a finite cohomological dimension.
The standard density structure,
introduced in
[
92
]
,
is used to demonstrate that certain cd-structures are bounded, including the Nisnevich cd-structure.

We need to deal with certain non-bounded cd-structures for fs log schemes due to the abundance of proper log étale monomorphisms (such morphisms are typically not isomorphisms).
In effect,
we introduce the notion of a quasi-bounded cd-structure,
see Definition
3.3.22
,
and modify the standard density structure so that it fits our situation,
see Definition
3.3.4
.
Moreover,
we extend B.G. functors to simplicial objects in order to deduce a finite cohomological dimension result.

Definition 3.3.1

(
[
91
, Definition 2.20]
)
.

A density structure on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object is the data of
D_{d}(X)
for
d\in\mathbb{N}
and
X\in\mathcal{C}
,
where
D_{d}(X)
is a family of morphisms with codomain
X
satisfying the following properties.

(i)

(\emptyset\rightarrow X)\in D_{0}(X)
.

(ii)

D_{d}(X)
contains all isomorphisms.

(iii)

D_{d+1}(X)\subset D_{d}(X)
.

(iv)

If
v:V\rightarrow U
is in
D_{d}(U)
and
u:U\rightarrow X
is in
D_{d}(X)
, then
u\circ v
is in
D_{d}(X)
.

We often write
U\in D_{d}(X)
instead of
(U\rightarrow X)\in D_{d}(X)
for short.

Definition 3.3.2
.

A density structure
D_{*}
on
\mathcal{C}
is
locally of finite dimension with respect to a topology
t
on
\mathcal{C}
if for any
X\in\mathcal{C}
,
there exists a natural number
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that for any
f:Y\rightarrow X
in
D_{n+1}(X)
, there exists a finite
t
-covering sieve
\{f_{i}:Y_{i}\rightarrow X\}_{i\in I}
such that
f=\amalg_{i\in I}f_{i}
.
The smallest such
n
is called the
dimension
of
X
with respect to
D_{*}
.

Remark 3.3.3
.

A density structure is locally of finite dimension with respect to the trivial topology if and only if it is locally of finite dimension in the sense of
[
91
, Definition 2.20]
.
Indeed, according to
[
91
, Definition 2.20]
, a density structure is locally of finite dimension if for any
X
, there exists
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that any element of
D_{n+1}(X)
is an isomorphism.

In the following definition, we slightly modify the standard density structure on a finite-dimensional scheme introduced by Voevodsky
[
92
, p. 1401]
.
Recall that distinct points
x_{0},\ldots,x_{d}
in
X
form an
increasing sequence
if
x_{i+1}
is a generalization of
x_{i}
for every
i
,
i.e.,
the point
x_{i}
is in the closure of
{\{x_{i+1}\}}
.

Definition 3.3.4
.

Let
D_{d}(X)
denote the family of finite disjoint unions

U=\amalg_{i}U_{i}\rightarrow X

of open immersions such that for any point
x\in X
that is not in the image, there is an increasing sequence
x=x_{0},\ldots,x_{d}
of length
d
in
X
.
Note that
D_{*}
is a density structure.

For a finite-dimensional fs log scheme
X
, as in
[
78
]
, we set

D_{d}(X):=D_{d}(\underline{X}).

Remark 3.3.5
.

In
[
92
, Section 2, p. 1401]
, only open immersions (and not finite disjoint unions of open immersions) are considered in the standard density structure.
Our slight weakening of the original conditions allows us to relate the dividing density structure defined in Definition
3.3.18
to the standard density structure.

Lemma 3.3.6
.

Let
X
be a finite dimensional scheme, and let
d\in\mathbb{N}
.

(1)

If
U\in D_{d}(X)
and
V
is an open subscheme of
X
, then
U\times_{X}V\in D_{d}(V)
.

(2)

Let
U\rightarrow X
be a finite disjoint union of open immersions mapping to
X
, and let
\{V_{i}\rightarrow X\}_{i\in I}
be an open cover of
X
.
Then
U\times_{X}V_{i}\in D_{d}(V_{i})
for every
i
if and only if
U\in D_{d}(X)
.

(3)

If
U,V\in D_{d}(X)
, then
U\times_{X}V\in D_{d}(X)
.

Démonstration.

Parts (1) and (3) are shown in
[
92
, Lemmas 2.4, 2.5]
.
Let us prove (2).
If
U\in D_{d}(X)
, then
U\times_{X}V_{i}\in D_{d}(V_{i})
for any
i
by (1).
Conversely, assume that
U\times_{X}V_{i}\in D_{d}(V_{i})
for every
i
.
Let
x_{0}
be a point of
X
that is not in the image of
U\rightarrow X
.
Choose
i
such that
x_{0}\in V_{i}
.
Then there exists an increasing sequence
x_{0},\ldots,x_{d}
of
V_{i}
, which is also an increasing sequence of
X
.
It follows that
U\in D_{d}(X)
.
∎

Definition 3.3.7
.

Let
X
be a finite dimensional noetherian fs log scheme.
For
d\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote by
D_{d}^{div}(X)
the family
U=\amalg_{i}U_{i}\rightarrow X
of finite disjoint unions of log étale monomorphisms to
X
with the following property :
For any log étale monomorphism
V\rightarrow X
such that the projection
U\times_{X}V\rightarrow V
is a finite disjoint union of log schemes
\{V_{i}\}
with open immersions
V_{i}\rightarrow V
,
then
U\times_{X}V\in D_{d}(V)
.

Remark 3.3.8
.

Any finite disjoint union
U\rightarrow X
of log étale monomorphisms is in
D_{0}^{div}(X)
.

In the following series of lemmas, we study fundamental properties of
D_{d}^{div}
.
In particular, we will show that
D_{d}^{div}
is a density structure.

Lemma 3.3.9
.

Let
f:Y\rightarrow X
be a surjective proper log étale monomorphism of finite-dimensional noetherian fs log schemes.
Then
Y\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
for all
d\in\mathbb{N}
.

Démonstration.

Let
V\rightarrow X
be a log étale monomorphism such that the projection
p:Y\times_{X}V\rightarrow V
is a finite disjoint union of open immersions to
V
.
By Proposition
A.11.6
,
p
is surjective.
Thus
Y\times_{X}V\in D_{d}(V)
and
Y\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
for all
d\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

Remark 3.3.10
.

If
X
has a trivial log structure, then every proper log étale monomorphism is an isomorphism,
see Remark
3.1.7
(1).
Thus, in this case,
the density structure
D_{d}^{div}(X)
agrees with the standard one.

Let us review the structure of affine toric varieties used in the proof of Lemma
3.3.11
.
Suppose
P
is an fs monoid.
Recall from
[
76
, §I.3.3]
that for every face
F
of
P
, there is a naturally induced closed immersion

\underline{\mathbb{A}_{F}}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[F])\simeq\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[P]/\mathbb{Z}[P-F])\rightarrow\underline{\mathbb{A}_{P}}.

There is also a naturally induced open immersion

\underline{\mathbb{A}_{F}^{*}}:=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[F^{\rm gp}])\rightarrow\underline{\mathbb{A}_{F}}.

The family
\{\underline{\mathbb{A}_{F}^{*}}\}
indexed by all the faces
F
of
P
forms a stratification of
\mathbb{A}_{P}
,
see
[
76
, Proposition I.3.3.4]
.
Moreover,
since
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[P]/\mathbb{Z}[P-F])
is naturally identified with
\underline{\mathbb{A}_{F}}
,
the points of
\underline{\mathbb{A}_{F}^{*}}
are exactly identified with the points
x
of
\underline{\mathbb{A}_{P}}
such that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbb{A}_{P},x}
is naturally given by
P/F
.
Suppose that
\theta:P\rightarrow Q
is a homomorphism of fs monoids.
This induces a natural morphism

\underline{\mathbb{A}_{\theta}}:\underline{\mathbb{A}_{Q}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathbb{A}_{P}}.

For every face
G
of
Q
, we have that

\underline{\mathbb{A}_{\theta}}(\underline{\mathbb{A}_{G}})\simeq\underline{\mathbb{A}_{\theta^{-1}(G)}}.

(3.3.1)

Lemma 3.3.11
.

Suppose
f:Y\rightarrow X
be a log étale monomorphism of finite dimensional noetherian fs log schemes.
For
x\in X
, let
y
be a generic point of
f^{-1}(x)
, and let
z\in Y
be a generalization of
y
.
Then
z
is a generic point of
f^{-1}(f(z))
.

Démonstration.

The question is Zariski local on
Y
and
X
.
Thus by Remark
A.5.8
(2), we may assume that
X
has a neat chart
P
at
x
.
This means that
P
is sharp and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X,x}\simeq P
.
Moreover,
by Lemma
A.11.3
,
we may assume that
f
has an fs chart
\theta:P\rightarrow Q
such that
\theta^{\rm gp}
is an isomorphism and the induced morphism

Y\rightarrow X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}

is an open immersion.
By Remark
A.5.8
(1), we may assume that
Q
is an exact chart at the point
y
.
This means that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,y}\simeq Q
, but we do not require that
Q
is sharp.
We may assume
Y\simeq X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}
since the question is Zariski local on
Y
.

Next, we claim that the induced homomorphism

\overline{\theta}:P\rightarrow\overline{Q}

is
\mathbb{Q}
-surjective, i.e.,
\overline{\theta}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
is surjective. Arguing by contradiction, assume that it is not.
Let
G_{1},G_{2},\ldots,G_{r}
be all the faces of
Q
such that
\theta^{-1}(G_{i})=0
.
Then the corresponding faces
\overline{G_{1}},\ldots,\overline{G_{r}}
in
\overline{Q}
are all the faces of
\overline{Q}
such that
\overline{\theta^{-1}}(\overline{G_{i}})=0
.
The implication (6)
\Rightarrow
(1) in
[
76
, Proposition I.4.3.9]
means that
r\geq 2
.
From (
3.3.1
) we have that

\{0\}\times_{\underline{\mathbb{A}_{P}}}\underline{\mathbb{A}_{Q}}\simeq\underline{\mathbb{A}_{G_{1}}}\cup\cdots\cup\underline{\mathbb{A}_{G_{r}}}.

(3.3.2)

Let
\kappa(-)
denote the residue field at some point, and set
D_{i}:=\underline{\mathbb{A}_{G_{i}}}\times\operatorname{Spec}(\kappa(x))
for simplicity.
Then from (
3.3.2
), we have

f^{-1}(x)\simeq D_{1}\cup\cdots\cup D_{r}.

Since
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,y}\simeq Q
, it follows that
y
is contained in
D_{1}
.
The isomorphism

D_{2}\simeq\operatorname{Spec}(\kappa(x)[G_{2}]),

shows that
D_{2}
is irreducible.
We have reached a contradiction since
D_{1}\subsetneq D_{2}
and
y
is a generic point of
f^{-1}(x)
.

Let
z^{\prime}
be a generalization of
z
in
f^{-1}(f(z))
.
Consider the projection

p:X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{Q},

and let
\overline{Q}/G
(resp.
\overline{Q}/G^{\prime}
) be the log structure on
z
(resp.
z^{\prime}
) where
G
(resp.
G^{\prime}
) is a face of
\overline{Q}
.
Note that
G^{\prime}\subset G
.
Since
f(z)=f(z^{\prime})
we have

\overline{\theta}^{-1}(G)=\overline{\theta}^{-1}(G^{\prime}).

By the implication (1)
\Rightarrow
(2) in
[
76
, Proposition I.4.3.9]
it follows that
G=G^{\prime}
.
Set
u:=f(z)=f(z^{\prime})
, and let
w
be the generic point of
\underline{\mathbb{A}_{G}}
with image
t
in
\underline{\mathbb{A}_{P}}
.
Then

z,z^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Spec}(\kappa(u)\otimes_{\kappa(t)}\kappa(w)).

Thus
z=z^{\prime}
,
so any generalization of
z
in
f^{-1}(f(z))
is equal to
z
.
This implies that
z
is a generic point of
f^{-1}(f(z))
.
∎

Lemma 3.3.12
.

Let
f:Y\rightarrow X
be a surjective log étale monomorphism of finite-dimensional noetherian fs log schemes, and let
j:U\rightarrow X
be a finite disjoint union of open immersions to
X
.
If
U\times_{X}Y\in D_{d}(Y)
, then
U\in D_{d}(X)
.

Démonstration.

Suppose
x\in X
is not in the image of
j
.
Since
f
is surjective, we can choose a generic point
y_{0}
of
f^{-1}(x_{0})
and an increasing sequence
y_{0},\ldots,y_{d}
of
Y
.
Then
f(y_{i})
is a generic point of
f^{-1}(f(y_{i}))
according to Lemma
3.3.11
.
Thus
f(y_{0}),\ldots,f(y_{d})
is an increasing sequence of
X
.
It follows that
U\in D_{d}(X)
.
∎

Lemma 3.3.13
.

Let
X
be a finite dimensional noetherian fs log scheme, and let
d\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then for every
U\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
and log étale monomorphism
V\rightarrow X
, we have
U\times_{X}V\in D_{d}^{div}(V)
.

Démonstration.

Let
W\rightarrow V
be a log étale monomorphism such that the projection
U\times_{X}W\rightarrow W
is a finite disjoint union of open immersions.
Since
U\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
and the composition
W\rightarrow X
is a log étale monomorphism, we have

(U\times_{X}V)\times_{V}W=U\times_{X}W\in D_{d}(W).

This shows that
U\times_{X}V\in D_{d}^{div}(V)
.
∎

Lemma 3.3.14
.

Let
Y\rightarrow X
be a surjective proper log étale monomorphism of finite-dimensional noetherian fs log schemes, and let
U\rightarrow X
be a finite disjoint union of log étale monomorphisms to
X
.
Then
U\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
if and only if
U\times_{X}Y\in D_{d}^{div}(Y)
.

Démonstration.

If
U\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
, then
U\times_{X}Y\in D_{d}^{div}(Y)
by Lemma
3.3.13
.
Conversely, assume that
U\times_{X}Y\in D_{d}^{div}(Y)
.
Let
V\rightarrow X
be a log étale monomorphism such that the projection
U\times_{X}V\rightarrow V
is a finite disjoint union of open immersions to
V
.
Then

U\times_{X}V\times_{X}Y\in D_{d}(V\times_{X}Y),

and the projection
V\times_{X}Y\rightarrow V
is a surjective proper log étale monomorphism by Proposition
A.11.6
.
Thus
U\times_{X}V\in D_{d}(V)
by Lemma
3.3.12
, so
U\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
.
∎

Lemma 3.3.15
.

Let
X
be a finite dimensional noetherian fs log scheme, and let
d\in\mathbb{N}
.

(1)

Let
U\rightarrow X
be a finite disjoint union of log étale monomorphisms to
X
,
and let
\{V_{i}\rightarrow X\}_{i\in I}
be an open cover of
X
.
Then
U\times_{X}V_{i}\in D_{d}^{div}(V_{i})
for any
i
if and only if
U\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
.

(2)

If
U,V\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
, then
U\times_{X}V\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
.

Démonstration.

(1) If
U\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
, then
U\times_{X}V_{i}\in D_{d}^{div}(V_{i})
for any
i
.
Conversely, assume that
U\times_{X}V_{i}\in D_{d}^{div}(V_{i})
for any
i
.
Let
W\rightarrow X
be a log étale monomorphism such that the projection
U\times_{X}W\rightarrow W
is a finite disjoint union of open immersions to
W
.
Then

V_{i}\times_{X}U\times_{X}W\in D_{d}(V_{i}\times_{X}W),

and hence
U\times_{X}W\in D_{d}(W)
by Lemma
3.3.6
(3).
This shows that
U\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
.

(2) The question is Zariski local on
X
by (1), so we may assume that
X
has an fs chart
P
.
By Proposition
A.11.5
there is a subdivision of fans
\Sigma\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
such that the pullbacks

U\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\rightarrow X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma},\,\,V\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\rightarrow X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}

are disjoint unions of open immersions to
X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}
.
By Lemma
3.3.14
,

U\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma},V\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\in D_{d}^{div}(X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}),

and we are reduced to showing that

U\times_{S}V\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\in D_{d}^{div}(X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}).

We may replace
X
by
X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}
, and assume that
U
and
V
are a disjoint union of open subschemes of
X
.
Let
W\rightarrow X
be a log étale monomorphism.
Then

U\times_{X}W,V\times_{X}W\in D_{d}(W)

and hence
U\times_{X}V\times_{X}W\in D_{d}(W)
by Lemma
3.3.6
(3).
This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.3.16
.

If
v:V\rightarrow U
is in
D_{d}^{div}(U)
and
u:U\rightarrow X
is in
D_{d}^{div}(X)
, then the composite
v\circ u:V\rightarrow X
is in
D_{d}^{div}(X)
.

Démonstration.

The question is Zariski local on
X
by Lemma
3.3.15
(1),
so we may assume that
X
has an fs chart
P
.
By Proposition
A.11.5
,
there is a surjective proper log étale monomorphism
Y\rightarrow X
such that the projections
U\times_{X}Y\rightarrow Y
and
V\times_{X}Y\rightarrow Y
are finite disjoint unions of open immersions to
Y
.
Then the pullback
V\times_{X}Y\rightarrow U\times_{X}Y
is also a finite disjoint union of open immersions to
U\times_{X}Y
.
By Lemma
3.3.14
,

U\times_{X}Y\in D_{d}^{div}(Y)\text{ and }V\times_{X}Y\in D_{d}^{div}(U\times_{X}Y).

We are reduced to showing that
V\times_{X}Y\in D_{d}^{div}(Y)
again by Lemma
3.3.14
.
To that end, we may replace
(X,U,V)
by
(Y,U\times_{X}Y,V\times_{X}Y)
and assume that
u
and
v
are disjoint unions of open immersions to
X
.

Let
W\rightarrow X
be a log étale monomorphism such that the projection
U\times_{X}W\rightarrow W
is a finite disjoint union of open immersions.
Then
U\times_{X}W\in D_{d}(W)
and
V\times_{X}W\in D_{d}(U\times_{X}W)
, so
V\times_{X}W\in D_{d}(W)
since
D_{*}
is a density structure.
Thus we have
V\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
.
∎

Here is a summary of the previous lemmas.

Theorem 3.3.17
.

Let
X
be a finite dimensional noetherian fs log scheme.
Then we have the following properties.

(1)

If
U\rightarrow X
is a surjective proper log étale monomorphism, then
U\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
for all
d\in\mathbb{N}
.

(2)

Let
V\rightarrow X
be a log étale monomorphism.
If
U\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
, then
U\times_{X}V\in D_{d}^{div}(V)
.

(3)

Let
Y\rightarrow X
be a dividing Zariski cover.
Then
U\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
if and only if
U\times_{X}Y\in D_{d}^{div}(Y)
.

(4)

If
U,V\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
, then
U\times_{X}V\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
.

(5)

If
v:V\rightarrow U
is in
D_{d}^{div}(U)
and
u:U\rightarrow X
is in
D_{d}^{div}(X)
, then
v\circ u
is in
D_{d}^{div}(X)
.

Definition 3.3.18
.

The density structure
D_{\ast}^{div}
on
lSm/S
and
lSch/S
established in Lemma
3.3.16
is called the
dividing density structure
.

Lemma 3.3.19
.

Let
X
be a finite-dimensional noetherian fs log scheme with an fs chart
P
, and let
U\rightarrow X
be a finite disjoint union of log étale monomorphisms to
X
.
If
U\times_{X}V\in D_{d}(V)
for any surjective proper log étale monomorphism
V\rightarrow X
such that the projection
U\times_{X}V\rightarrow V
is a finite disjoint union of open immersions to
V
,
then
U\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
.

Démonstration.

Let
W\rightarrow X
be a log étale monomorphism such that the projection

U\times_{X}W\rightarrow W

is a finite disjoint union of open immersions to
W
.
By Proposition
A.11.5
, there is a surjective proper log étale monomorphism
V\rightarrow X
such that

W\times_{X}V\rightarrow V\text{ and }U\times_{X}V\rightarrow V

is an open immersion and a finite disjoint union of open immersions to
V
,
respectively.

Consider the commutative diagram with the evident projection morphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times_{X}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times_{X}W\times_{X}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times_{X}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\times_{X}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V.}

By assumption we have
U\times_{X}V\in D_{d}(V)
.
Lemma
3.3.6
(1) implies that

U\times_{X}W\times_{X}V\in D_{d}(W\times_{X}V)

since
W\times_{X}V
is an open subscheme of
V
.
Thus by Lemma
3.3.12
,

U\times_{X}W\in D_{d}(W)

since the projection
W\times_{X}V\rightarrow W
is a surjective proper log étale monomorphism by Proposition
A.11.6
.
This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.3.20
.

Let
f:Y\rightarrow X
be a strict Nisnevich morphism of finite-dimensional noetherian fs log schemes.
Assume that
X
has an fs chart
P
.
Then for every
d\in\mathbb{N}
and
Y_{0}\in D_{d}^{div}(Y)
, there exists
X_{0}\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
such that the projection
X_{0}\times_{X}Y\rightarrow Y
factors through
Y_{0}
.

Démonstration.

By definition,
Y_{0}\rightarrow Y
is a disjoint union of log étale monomorphisms.
Proposition
A.11.5
says that there exists a subdivision of fans
\Sigma\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
such that the induced morphism

Y_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\rightarrow Y\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}

is a finite disjoint union of open immersions to
Y\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}
.
Now by Lemma
3.3.14
,
Y_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\in D_{d}^{div}(Y\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma})
.
Hence replacing
Y_{0}
by
Y_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}
, we may assume

Y_{0}\simeq Y_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}.

Then the morphism
Y_{0}\rightarrow Y\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}
is a finite disjoint union of open immersions.
It suffices to construct
X_{0}\in D_{d}^{div}(X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma})
since then
X_{0}\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
by Lemma
3.3.14
.
We may replace
X
by
X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}
and assume
Y_{0}
is a finite disjoint union of open subschemes of
Y
.

Set

X_{0}:=X-cl(f(Y-\operatorname{Im}(Y_{0}\rightarrow Y)),

where
cl(-)
denotes the closure.
The construction in the proof of
[
92
, Lemma 2.9]
shows that
X_{0}\in D_{d}(X)
.
Let
V\rightarrow X
be a surjective proper log étale monomorphism.
Since
V
is proper over
X
, we have

X_{0}\times_{X}V=V-cl(f_{V}(Y\times_{X}V-\operatorname{Im}((Y_{0}\times_{X}V)\rightarrow(Y\times_{X}V)))),

where
f_{V}:Y\times_{X}V\rightarrow V
is the projection.
Thus again using the proof of
[
92
, Lemma 2.9]
,
X_{0}\times_{X}V\in D_{d}(V)
, and
X_{0}\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
by Lemma
3.3.19
.
∎

Definition 3.3.21

(
[
91
, Definition 2.21]
)
.

Let
P
be a cd-structure on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object, and let
D_{*}
be a density structure on
\mathcal{C}
.
A distinguished square
Q
of the form (
3.1.1
) is called
reducing
with respect to
D_{*}
if for all
d\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
,
Y_{0}\in D_{d}(Y)
,
X_{0}^{\prime}\in D_{d}(X^{\prime})
,
and
Y_{0}^{\prime}\in D_{d-1}(Y^{\prime})
there is a distinguished square

together a morphism
Q_{1}\rightarrow Q
satisfying the following properties.

(i)

The morphism
X_{1}\rightarrow X\in D_{d}(X)
.

(ii)

The morphism
Y_{1}\rightarrow Y
(resp.
X_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
, resp.
Y_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
) factors through
Y_{0}
(resp.
X_{0}^{\prime}
, resp.
Y_{0}^{\prime}
).

We say that a distinguished square
Q^{\prime}
is a
refinement
of
Q
if there is a morphism
Q^{\prime}\rightarrow Q
that is an isomorphism between the lower right corners.

Definition 3.3.22
.

Let
P
be a cd-structure on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object, and let
D_{*}
be a density structure on
\mathcal{C}
. Consider the following conditions.

(i)

Any distinguished square in
P
has a refinement that is reducing with respect to
D_{*}
.

(ii)

D_{*}
is locally of finite dimension with respect to the topology associated with
P
.

If
P
satisfies (i) (resp. (i) and (ii)), we say that
P
is
reducing
(
[
91
, Definition 2.22]
) (resp.
quasi-bounded
) with respect to
D_{*}
.

Remark 3.3.23
.

A cd-structure
P
is bounded with respect to
D_{*}
in the sense of
[
91
, Definition 2.22]
if and only if
P
is reducing with respect to
D_{*}
and

D_{*}
is locally of finite dimension with respect to the trivial topology.
The reason why we consider quasi-bounded cd-structures is that
D_{*}^{div}
is not bounded.
Indeed, if
Y\rightarrow X
is a surjective proper log étale monomorphism, then
Y\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
for any
d\in\mathbb{N}
by Lemma
3.3.13
.
Thus for any
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
D_{d}^{div}(X)
can contain morphisms other than isomorphisms in general, so
D_{*}^{div}
is not bounded.

Lemma 3.3.24
.

Let
Y\rightarrow X
be a log étale morphism in
lSm/k
.
Then
\dim Y\leq\dim X
.

Démonstration.

Since
X
and
Y
are in
lSm/k
,
X-\partial X
and
Y-\partial Y
are dense in
X
and
Y
.
To conclude observe that
\dim(Y-\partial X)\leq\dim(X-\partial X)
.
∎

Example 3.3.25
.

Let
\Sigma
be the star subdivision of the dual of
\mathbb{N}^{2}
.
If

X:=\{(0,0)\}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}^{2}}\text{ and }Y=\{(0,0)\}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma},

then the naturally induced morphism
Y\rightarrow X
is log étale, but
\dim Y=2
and
\dim X=1
.

Proposition 3.3.26
.

The dividing density structures on
lSm/S
and
lSch/S
are locally of finite dimension with respect to the dividing Nisnevich topology.

Démonstration.

Suppose
X
is an
n
-dimensional noetherian fs log scheme.
Let
f:U\rightarrow X
be any finite disjoint union of log étale monomorphisms to
X
.
We need to show that there exists an integer
m\geq 0
such that
U\in D_{m}^{div}(X)
implies that
f
is a dividing Nisnevich cover.
Let
r
be the maximum rank of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X,x}^{\rm gp}
,
where
x
ranges over all points of
X
.
Since
X
is an fs log scheme, the rank of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X,x}^{\rm gp}
is a locally finite function on
X
.
Thus
r
is a finite number since
X
is quasi-compact.
In order to conclude,
it suffices to show that
U\in D_{r+d+1}^{div}(X)
implies that
f
is a dividing Zariski cover.
The problem is a Zariski local on
X
,
so that we may assume
X
has an fs chart
P
that is exact at a point of
X
,
see Remark
A.5.8
(1).
By definition we have
\operatorname{rank}P^{\rm gp}\leq r
.

Proposition
A.11.5
shows there exists a subdivision of fans
\Sigma\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
such that the pullback

f^{\prime}:U\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\rightarrow X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}

is a finite disjoint union of open immersions to
X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}
.
For every point
x\in\underline{\mathbb{A}_{P}}
, the fiber of
x
under
\underline{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathbb{A}_{P}}
has dimension
\leq r
.
Thus we get that

\dim(X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma})\leq r+n.

It follows that
f^{\prime}
is surjective because

U\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\in D_{r+n+1}(X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}).

In
[
92
, p. 1401]
, it is shown that the dimension with respect to the standard density structure is equal to the Krull dimension.
Thus
f^{\prime}
is a Zariski cover.
The projection
X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\rightarrow X
is a surjective proper log étale monomorphism.
In conclusion,

f
admits a refinement by a dividing Zariski cover,
i.e.,

f
is a dividing Zariski cover.
∎

Remark 3.3.27
.

The above proof shows that the dimension of
X
with respect to
D_{*}^{div}
is less than or equal to the maximum possible dimension of
X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}
.
If
X
is log smooth over
k
,
we see that the dimension of
X
with respect to
D_{*}^{div}
is
\leq n
since
\dim X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\leq n
due to Lemma
3.3.24
.

Proposition 3.3.28
.

The strict Nisnevich cd-structures on
lSm/S
and
lSch/S
are quasi-bounded with respect to the dividing density structure.

Démonstration.

By Proposition
3.3.26
it remains to show the strict Nisnevich cd-structure is reducing with respect to the dividing density structure.

be a strict Nisnevich distinguished square of finite dimensional noetherian fs log schemes,

together with a morphism
Q_{1}\rightarrow Q
that coincides with
X_{1}\rightarrow X
on the lower right corner such that

Y_{1}\rightarrow Y
(resp.
X_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
, resp.
Y_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
) factors through
Y_{0}
(resp.
X_{0}
, resp.
Y_{0}
).
By appealing to Lemma
3.3.15
(1), it suffices to construct
X_{1}
Zariski locally on
X
. Hence we may assume that
X
has an fs chart
P
.

Owing to Lemma
3.3.20
we can choose
V,W\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
such that the projections

V\times_{X}Y\rightarrow Y\text{ and }W\times_{X}X^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}

factors through
Y_{0}
and
X_{0}^{\prime}
, respectively.
Take
X_{0}:=V\times_{X}W
, which is again in
D_{d}^{div}(X)
by Lemma
3.3.15
(2).
Then the projections

X_{0}\times_{X}Y\rightarrow Y\text{ and }X_{0}\times_{X}X^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}

again factors through
Y_{0}
and
X_{0}^{\prime}
, respectively.
It suffices to construct
X_{1}\in D_{d}^{div}(X_{0})
with the required property since then
X_{1}\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
by Lemma
3.3.16
.
Hence we may replace
X
by
X_{0}
and assume that
X^{\prime}=X_{0}^{\prime}
and
Y=Y_{0}
.

By Proposition
A.11.5
, there exists a subdivision of fans
\Sigma\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
such that the pullback

Y_{0}^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\rightarrow Y^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}

is a finite disjoint union of open immersions to
Y^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}
.
By Lemma
3.3.14
we have

Y_{0}^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\in D_{d-1}^{div}(Y^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}).

This reduces the claim to constructing
X_{1}\in D_{d}^{div}(X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma})
with the required property since then
X_{1}\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
again by Lemma
3.3.14
.
To that end, we may replace
X
by
X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}
, and hence assume that
Y_{0}^{\prime}
is a finite disjoint union of open subschemes of
Y_{0}
.

The construction in the proof of
[
92
, Proposition 2.10]
shows that

X_{1}:=X-((X-X^{\prime})\cap cl(gf^{\prime}(Y^{\prime}-Y_{0}^{\prime})))\in D_{d}(X),

and the induced square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{0}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}(Y_{0}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}}

is a strict Nisnevich distinguished square.

Now let
V\rightarrow X
be a surjective proper log étale monomorphism.
Then

Y_{0}^{\prime}\times_{X}V\in D_{d-1}(V),

and

X_{1}\times_{X}V=V-(V-X^{\prime}\times_{X}V)\cap cl(g_{V}f_{V}^{\prime}(Y^{\prime}\times_{X}V-Y_{0}^{\prime}\times_{X}V)),

where
f_{v}^{\prime}:Y^{\prime}\times_{X}V\rightarrow X^{\prime}\times_{X}V
and
g_{v}:X^{\prime}\times_{X}V\rightarrow V
are the pullbacks since
V
is proper over
X
.
Thus
X_{1}\times_{X}V\in D_{d}(X)
as in the above paragraph, and therefore
X_{1}\in D_{d}^{div}(X)
by Lemma
3.3.19
.
∎

Proposition 3.3.30
.

The strict Nisnevich, dividing, and dividing Nisnevich cd-structures on
lSm/S
and
lSch/S
are complete,
regular, and quasi-bounded with respect to the dividing density structure.

Démonstration.

These cd-structures are complete by
[
91
, Lemma 2.5]
.
By Propositions
3.3.28
and
3.3.29
, the strict Nisnevich cd-structure and the dividing cd-structure are quasi-bounded with respect to the dividing density structure.
The union of two cd-structures that are quasi-bounded with respect to the same density structure is again a quasi-bounded cd-structure as in
[
91
, Lemma 2.24]
.
Thus the dividing Nisnevich cd-structure is also quasi-bounded with respect to the dividing density structure.

A square is a strict Nisnevich distinguished square if and only if every morphism in the square is strict and the square of underlying schemes is a Nisnevich distinguished square.
Therefore the strict Nisnevich cd-structure is regular since the Nisnevich cd-structure is regular by
[
92
, Lemma 2.14]
.
The dividing cd-structure is regular since if
f:Y\rightarrow X
is a log modification, then
f
is a monomorphism, so the diagonal morphism
Y\rightarrow Y\times_{X}Y
is an isomorphism.
Thus the dividing Nisnevich cd-structure is regular by
[
91
, Lemma 2.12]

∎

3.4.
Flasque simplicial presheaves

In this section, we generalize Voevodsky’s
[
91
, Lemma 3.5]
to complete, quasi-bounded, and regular cd-structures.
As an application, we discuss a bounded descent structure on sheaves of abelian groups.

Definition 3.4.1
.

A morphism
f:Y\rightarrow X
in a category
\mathcal{C}
is
squareable
2
2

2

Some authors opted for the French expression
quarrable
from SGA 4.
if for every morphism
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
the fiber product
Y\times_{X}X^{\prime}
is representable.
If
f
is squareable, then let
\check{C}(Y/X)
(or
\check{C}(Y)
when no confusion seems likely to arise) denote the corresponding Čech nerve

\check{C}(Y/X):=\left(\cdots\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\longrightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\longrightarrow\end{subarray}Y\times_{X}Y\rightrightarrows Y\right).

Let
\check{C}(f)
denote the morphism
\check{C}(Y)\rightarrow X
induced by the structure morphisms
Y\times_{X}\cdots\times_{X}Y\rightarrow Y
.

Definition 3.4.2
.

A cd-structure
P
in a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object is
squareable
if the following conditions are satisfied.

(i)

Every morphism to the initial object is an isomorphism.

(ii)

For every distinguished square
Q
of the form (
3.1.2
) and morphism
V\rightarrow X
, the square
Q\times_{X}V
is representable and belongs to
P
.

Note that any squareable cd-structure is complete by
[
91
, Lemma 2.5]

In the following, we consider a variant of the notion of a B.G. functor introduced in
[
91
, Definition 3.1]

Here B.G. is shorthand for Brown-Gersten.

Definition 3.4.4
.

Let
P
be a squareable cd-structure on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object.
An
extended B.G. functor
on
\mathcal{C}
with respect to
P
is a family of contravariant functors
\{T_{q}\}_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}

from the category of simplicial objects in
\mathcal{C}
to the category of pointed sets together with pointed maps

\partial_{\mathscr{Q}}:T_{q+1}(\mathscr{Y}^{\prime})\rightarrow T_{q}(\mathscr{X})

for any level-wise distinguished square
\mathscr{Q}
of the form (
3.4.1
) subject to the following conditions.

(i)

The pointed maps
\partial_{\mathscr{Q}}
are natural with respect to the morphisms
\mathscr{Q}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathscr{Q}
of level-wise distinguished squares.

(ii)

For any
q\in\mathbb{Z}
and any level-wise distinguished square
\mathscr{Q}
of the form (
3.4.1
), the induced sequence of pointed sets

T_{q+1}(\mathscr{Y}^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial_{\mathscr{Q}}}}{{\rightarrow}}T_{q}(\mathscr{X})\rightarrow T_{q}(\mathscr{Y})\times T_{q}(\mathscr{X}^{\prime})

is exact.

(iii)

For any
t_{P}
-covering sieve
\{U_{i}\rightarrow X\}_{i\in I}
, the induced morphism

T_{q}(X)\rightarrow T_{q}(\check{C}(U))

is an isomorphism, where
U=\amalg_{i\in I}U_{i}
.

In
[
91
, Definition 3.1]
only the conditions (i) and (ii) are needed in the definition of B.G. functor.
The reason why we introduce the condition (iii) is to take advantage of extending
\{T_{q}\}_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}
to simplicial objects.

For any morphism
f:Y\rightarrow X
, let
f^{*}:T_{q}(X)\rightarrow T_{q}(Y)
denote the induced pullback morphism.
Let
D
be a density structure on
\mathcal{C}
.
For
d\in\mathbb{N}
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, we consider the following condition.

(
{\rm Cov}_{d,q}
)

For any
X\in\mathcal{C}
and
a\in T_{q}(X)
, there exists
u:U\rightarrow X
in
D_{d}(Y)
such that the pullback of
a
to
T_{q}(\check{C}(U))
is trivial.

For every
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, let
a_{t_{P}}T_{q}
be the
t_{P}
-sheaf on
\mathcal{C}
associated with the presheaf

(X\in\mathcal{C})\mapsto T_{q}(X).

Lemma 3.4.5
.

Let
P
be a squareable and regular cd-structure reducing with respect to a density structure
D
on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object, let
\{T_{q}\}_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}
be an extended B.G. functor, and let
d\in\mathbb{N}
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
.
If
a_{t_{P}}T_{q}
is trivial, then
({\rm Cov}_{d,q+1})
implies
({\rm Cov}_{d+1,q})
.

Démonstration.

Subject to a few changes, we follow the proof of
[
91
, Theorem 3.2]
.
By forming refinements,
we may assume that every distinguished square is reducing with respect to a density structure
D
.
Let
a\in T_{q}(X)
,
where
X
is an object of
\mathcal{C}
.

Consider a distinguished square

(3.4.2)

First, let us assume there exist morphisms
v:Y_{0}\rightarrow Y
in
D_{d+1}(Y)
and
u^{\prime}:X_{0}^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
in
D_{d+1}(X^{\prime})
such that
a
restricts trivially on
\check{C}(Y_{0})
and
\check{C}(X_{0}^{\prime})
(i.e. becomes trivial after such pullback).
Since
Q
is reducing, there exists a morphism
Q_{1}\rightarrow Q
of distinguished triangles such that
Y_{1}\rightarrow Y
(resp.
X_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
) in the upper right (resp. lower left) corner factors through

Y_{0}
(resp.
X_{0}^{\prime}
), and the morphism
X_{1}\rightarrow X
in the lower right corner belongs to
D_{d+1}(X)
.
Then
a
restricts trivially on
\check{C}(Y_{1})
and
\check{C}(X_{1}^{\prime})
.
According to condition (ii) in Definition
3.4.4
, there exists an element
b\in T_{q+1}(\check{C}(Y_{1}^{\prime}))
such that
\partial_{\mathscr{Q}}(b)=a
.
By
({\rm Cov}_{d,q+1})
, there exists an object
Y_{2}^{\prime}\in D_{d}(Y_{1}^{\prime})
such that
b
restricts trivially on
\check{C}(Y_{1}^{\prime})
.
Now
Q_{1}
is reducing, so we have a morphism
Q_{3}\rightarrow Q_{1}
of distinguished squares such that
Y_{3}^{\prime}\rightarrow Y_{1}^{\prime}
factors through
Y_{2}^{\prime}
and
X_{3}\in D_{d+1}(X_{1})
.
Note that
b
restricts trivially on
\check{C}(Y_{3}^{\prime})
,
and hence likewise for
a
and
\check{C}(X_{3})
.
Since
X_{1}\in D_{d+1}(X)
and
X_{3}\in D_{d+1}(X_{1})
, we deduce that
X_{3}\in D_{d+1}(X)
, i.e.,
(\mathrm{Cov}_{d+1,q})
is satisfied, which concludes the proof in this special case.

In the general case, since
a_{t_{P}}T_{q}
is trivial, there exists a
t_{P}
-covering sieve

\mathcal{F}=\{V_{j}\rightarrow X\}_{j\in J}

such that
a
restricts trivially on
V_{j}
for all
j\in J
.
Since
P
is complete, we may assume the covering is a simple covering.
By excluding the trivial cover, we may assume that
\mathcal{F}
is formed by simple coverings of
Y
and
X^{\prime}
for a distinguished square of the form (
3.4.2
).
If there exists morphisms
v:Y_{0}\rightarrow Y
in
D_{d+1}(Y)
and
u^{\prime}:X_{0}^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
in
D_{d+1}(X^{\prime})
such that
a
restricts trivially on
\check{C}(Y_{0})
and
\check{C}(X_{0}^{\prime})
,
using the above paragraph,
there exists a morphism
u:U\rightarrow X
in
D_{d}(Y)
such that
a
restricts trivially on
\check{C}(U)
.
Repeating this argument, we reduce to the case when
\mathcal{F}
is the trivial cover.
Then clearly
a
is trivial, and we are done.
∎

Theorem 3.4.6
.

Let
P
be a squareable and regular cd-structure that is quasi-bounded with respect to a density structure
D_{\ast}
on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object,
and suppose
\mathcal{F}
is a
t_{P}
-sheaf of abelian groups.
Then for any
X\in\mathcal{C}
and
n>\dim_{D}X
, the
n
th
t_{P}
-cohomology group vanishes

H_{t_{P}}^{n}(X,\mathcal{F})=0.

Démonstration.

For any simplicial object
\mathscr{X}
in
\mathcal{C}
, set
T_{q}(\mathscr{X}):=\mathbf{H}_{t_{P}}^{-q}(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{F})
.
There is a canonical hypercohomology spectral sequence

E_{1}^{pq}:=H_{t_{P}}^{q}(\mathscr{X}_{p},\mathcal{F})\Rightarrow\mathbf{H}_{t_{P}}^{p+q}(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{F}).

Together with
[
91
, Lemma 2.19]
we deduce that
\{T_{q}\}_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}
satisfies the conditions (i) and (ii) in Definition
3.4.4
.
For any finite
t_{P}
-covering sieve
\{U_{i}\rightarrow X\}_{i\in I}
, the induced morphism
U\rightarrow X
forms a
t_{P}
-covering sieve,
where
X:=\amalg_{i\in I}U_{i}
.
Let
\mathscr{U}
denote the Čech nerve associated with
U\rightarrow X
.
By cohomological descent, we obtain an isomorphism

H_{t_{P}}^{-q}(X,\mathcal{F})\rightarrow H_{t_{P}}^{-q}(\mathscr{U},\mathcal{F}).

That is,

\{T_{q}\}_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}
satisfies condition (iii) in Definition
3.4.4
.
Therefore
\{T_{q}\}_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}
is an extended B.G. functor.
Moreover,
for every
q<0
we have

a_{t_{P}}T_{q}=a_{t_{P}}\mathbf{H}_{t_{P}}^{-q}=0.

Since
\emptyset\in D_{0}(X)
, we see that
{(\rm Cov}_{0,0})
holds.
Then by Lemma
3.4.5
,
{(\rm Cov}_{n,-n})
holds for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
i.e.,
there exists
j:U\rightarrow X
in
D_{n}(Y)
such that
a\in H^{n}(X,\mathcal{F})
restricts trivially on
\check{C}(U)
.
In the range
n>\dim_{D}X
, the morphism
j
is a
t_{P}
-covering sieve.
Thus
a
is trivial owing to condition (iii) in Definition
3.4.4
.
∎

Theorem 3.4.7
.

Let
P
be a squareable, quasi-bounded, and regular cd-structure on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object, and let
\{T_{q}\}_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}
be an extended B.G. functor.
If
a_{t_{P}}T_{q}
and
T_{q}(\emptyset)
are trivial for any
q
, then
T_{q}
is trivial for any
q
.

Démonstration.

Since
T_{q}(\emptyset)
is trivial for any
q
, we see that
({\rm Cov}_{0,q})
holds for all
q
.
Applying Lemma
3.4.5
we deduce that
({\rm Cov}_{d,q})
holds for all
d
and
q
.
Thus for all
d
,
q
,
X\in\mathcal{C}
, and
a\in T_{q}(X)
, there exists a morphism
u:U\rightarrow X
in
D_{d}(Y)
such that
a
restricts trivially on
\check{C}(U)
.
In the range
d>\dim_{D}(X)
, we have
u=\amalg_{i\in I}u_{i}
for some finite
t_{P}
-covering sieve
\{u_{i}:U_{i}\rightarrow X\}_{i\in I}
.
It follows that
a
is trivial owing to condition (iii) in Definition
3.4.4
.
∎

Definition 3.4.8

(
[
91
, Definition 3.3]
)
.

Let
P
be a cd-structure on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object.
A simplicial presheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathcal{C}
is called
flasque
(with respect to
P
) if
\mathcal{F}(\emptyset)
is contractible and for any distinguished square
Q
,
the square
\mathcal{F}(Q)
is homotopy cartesian.

Definition 3.4.9
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a category equipped with a topology
t
.
A morphism of simplicial presheaves
f:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is called a
t
-local equivalence
if the following conditions are satisfied.

(i)

The map
a_{t}\pi_{0}(\mathcal{F})\rightarrow a_{t}\pi_{0}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
induced by
f
is an isomorphism.

(ii)

The map
a_{t}\pi_{n}(\mathcal{F},x)\rightarrow a_{t}\pi_{n}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},f(x))
induced by
f
is an isomorphism for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
,
X\in\mathcal{C}
, and
x\in F(X)
.

Note that pointwise weak equivalences are exactly local equivalences for the trivial topology.

Lemma 3.4.10
.

Let
P
be a squareable, quasi-bounded, and regular cd-structure on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object.
Then a morphism
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
of flasque simplicial presheaves is a
t_{P}
-local equivalence if and only if it is a pointwise weak equivalence.

Démonstration.

The if part is trivial. Let us assume that
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
is a
t_{P}
-local equivalence.
By the first two paragraphs of the proof of
[
91
, Lemma 3.5]
, we reduce to the case when
G={\rm pt}
and
\mathcal{F}
is a Kan simplicial presheaf.
The fourth paragraph of the proof of
[
91
, Lemma 3.5]
shows that
\mathcal{F}({\rm pt})
is nonempty.
Choose an element
x\in\mathcal{F}({\rm pt})
and consider the family of functors

T_{q}(\mathscr{X}):=\pi_{q}(\mathcal{F}(\mathscr{X}),x|_{\mathscr{X}}),

where
q\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathscr{X}
is a simplicial object in
\mathcal{C}
(by convention,
\pi_{0}(T,x):=\pi_{0}(T)
and
\pi_{q}(T,x):=0
for any simplicial set
T
and
x\in\pi_{0}(T)
if
q<0
).
Since
\mathcal{F}
is flasque,
T_{q}
satisfies the conditions (i) and (ii) of Definition
3.4.4
.
By
[
91
, Corollary 5.10]
,
T_{q}
satisfies condition (iii) of Definition
3.4.4
,
i.e.,

T_{q}
is an extended B.G. functor.
Since
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow{\rm pt}
is a
t_{P}
-local equivalence,
a_{t_{P}}T_{q}
is trivial for any
q
.
By Theorem
3.4.7
we deduce
T_{q}
is trivial for all
q
since
T_{q}(\emptyset)
is trivial for all
q
.
∎

Let
P
be a squareable, quasi-bounded, and regular cd-structure on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object, and let
t_{P}
be the topology associated with
P
.
For an object
T\in\mathcal{C}
let
\Lambda(T)
be the representable presheaf of
\Lambda
-modules given by

Z\mapsto\Lambda(T)(Z):=\Lambda\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(Z,T).

Let
\mathcal{H}_{pre}^{\prime}
be the family of complexes of the form

\Lambda(Y^{\prime})\rightarrow\Lambda(Y)\oplus\Lambda(X^{\prime})\rightarrow\Lambda(X)

(3.4.3)

induced by the distinguished squares of
P
, as in (
3.1.1
).

Corollary 3.4.11
.

Let
P
be a squareable, quasi-bounded, and regular cd-structure on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object, and let
t_{P}
be the topology associated with
P
.
Then a complex of presheaves of
\Lambda
-modules
\mathcal{F}
is
\mathcal{H}_{pre}^{\prime}
-flasque in the sense of
Definition
B.1.2
(iv)
if and only if for every
X\in\mathcal{C}
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{K}(\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{C},\Lambda))}(\Lambda(X)[i],\mathcal{F})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(\mathcal{C},\Lambda))}(a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X)[i],a_{t}^{*}\mathcal{F}).

Démonstration.

This follows by replacing
[
91
, Lemma 3.5]
with Lemma
3.4.10
and adapting
[
91
, Lemma 4.3]
in the proof of
[
24
, Theorem 3.7]
.
∎

Lemma 3.4.12
.

Let
P
be a squareable, quasi-bounded, and regular cd-structure on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object.
Then a morphism
f:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{H}_{pre}^{\prime}
-flasque complexes of presheaves of
\Lambda
-modules is a quasi-isomorphism if and only if

a_{t}^{*}f:a_{t}^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Démonstration.

If
f
is a quasi-isomorphism, then
a_{t}^{*}f
is a quasi-isomorphism since the sheafification functor
a_{t}^{*}
is exact.
Conversely,
if
a_{t}^{*}f
is a quasi-isomorphism,
then owing to Corollary
3.4.11
we have an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{K}(\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{C},\Lambda))}(\Lambda(X)[i],\mathcal{F})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{K}(\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{C},\Lambda))}(\Lambda(X)[i],\mathcal{F}^{\prime})

for every
X\in\mathcal{C}
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
This implies that
f
is a quasi-isomorphism.
∎

Example 3.4.13
.

Let
P
be a squareable, quasi-bounded, and regular cd-structure on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object, and let
t_{P}
be the topology associated with
P
.
Recall the collection
\mathcal{G}
in Example
B.1.7
and
\mathcal{H}_{pre}^{\prime}
in (
3.4.3
).
Let
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
be the family of complexes of the form

a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(Y^{\prime})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(Y)\oplus a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X^{\prime})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X)

induced by the distinguishes squares of
P
, as in (
3.1.1
).
Suppose that the complex of
t_{P}
-sheaves
\mathcal{F}
is
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
-flasque.
By adjunction this implies that
a_{t*}\mathcal{F}
is
\mathcal{H}_{pre}^{\prime}
-flasque.
Owing to Corollary
3.4.11
we see that
\mathcal{F}
is
\mathcal{G}
-local.
Thus
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H}^{\prime})
is a bounded descent structure because
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
consists of bounded complexes of objects that are finite sums of objects of
\mathcal{G}
.
This is genuinely different from the descent structure
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
discussed in Example
B.1.7
.

4.
Sheaves with logarithmic transfers

The following three fundamental results are due to Voevodsky
[
64
, §6]
.

(1)

The Nisnevich sheafification of a presheaf with transfers has a unique transfer structure.

(2)

The category of Nisnevich sheaves with transfers
\mathbf{Shv}_{Nis}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda)
is a Grothendieck abelian category.

(3)

For
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{Shv}_{Nis}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda)
and
X\in Sm/k
there is a canonical isomorphism (see below for the definition of
\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}
)

{\rm Ext}_{\mathbf{Shv}_{Nis}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda)}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X),F)\simeq H_{Nis}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F}).

In this section, we develop the log versions of (1)–(3) and show that the following topologies are compatible with log transfers :

sNis,\;dNis,\;s\acute{e}t,\;d\acute{e}t,\;k\acute{e}t,\;\text{and }l\acute{e}t.

4.1.
Category of presheaves with log transfers

Recall
\Lambda
is a commutative unital ring.
The next step after introducing
lCor/k
is to study presheaves with log transfers in the following sense.

Definition 4.1.1
.

A
presheaf of
\Lambda
-modules with log transfers
is an additive presheaf of
\Lambda
-modules on the category of finite log correspondences
lCor/k
.
We let
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
denote the category of presheaves of
\Lambda
-modules with log transfers.
For
X\in lSm/k
, let
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)
denote the representable presheaf of
\Lambda
-modules with log transfers given by

Y\mapsto\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y):=\operatorname{lCor}(Y,X)\otimes\Lambda.

Remark 4.1.2
.

Let
F
be a presheaf of
\Lambda
-modules with log transfers.
For every
X,Y\in lSm/k
, by additivity, there exists an induced paring

\operatorname{lCor}(X,Y)\otimes F(Y)\rightarrow F(X).

This produces the ”wrong way” maps parametrized by finite log correspondences.

Example 4.1.3
.

Every constant presheaf of
\Lambda
-modules on
lSm/k
has a canonical log transfer structure.

Example 4.1.4
.

The presheaves
\mathcal{O}
and
\mathcal{O}^{*}
are two examples of presheaves with transfers.
Let us extend these to presheaves with log transfers.
For
X\in lSm/k
, we define

\mathcal{O}(X):=\mathcal{O}(\underline{X}),\;\mathcal{O}^{*}(X):=\mathcal{O}^{*}(\underline{X}).

Owing to Lemma
2.2.8
,
X
is normal.
For any strict finite surjective morphism
V\rightarrow X
, as observed in
[
64
, Example 2.4]
, there is a canonical trace map and a norm map

{\rm Tr}:\mathcal{O}(V)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(X),\;N:\mathcal{O}^{*}(V)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{*}(X).

If
V
is a finite log correspondence from
X
to
Y\in lSm/k
, we can define transfer maps via the composite

V^{*}:\mathcal{O}(Y)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(V)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm Tr}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}(X),\;V^{*}:\mathcal{O}^{*}(Y)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{*}(V)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle N}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}^{*}(X)

Let us check that these transfer maps are compatible with compositions of finite log correspondences.
Since
X
is reduced, we have the inclusions

\mathcal{O}(X)\subset\mathcal{O}(X-\partial X),\;\mathcal{O}^{*}(X)\subset\mathcal{O}^{*}(X-\partial X).

Thus for
Z\in lSm/k
and
W\in\operatorname{lCor}(Y,Z)
to check that
V^{*}\circ W^{*}=(W\circ V)^{*}
, owing to Lemma
2.3.2
, it suffices to check that

(V-\partial V)^{*}\circ(W-\partial W)^{*}=((W-\partial W)\circ(V-\partial V))^{*}.

This is also observed in
[
64
, Example 2.4]
.
Thus
\mathcal{O}
and
\mathcal{O}^{*}
are both examples of presheaves with log transfers.

Example 4.1.5
.

Let
\mathcal{M}^{\rm gp}
denote the presheaf of abelian groups on
lSm/k
given by

\mathcal{M}^{\rm gp}(X):=\mathcal{M}_{X}^{\rm gp}(X).

By
[
76
, Theorem IV.3.5.1]
,
X\in lSm/k
is log regular.
By
[
76
, Theorem III.1.11.12]
, the log structure on
X
is the compactifying log structure associated with the inclusion
X-\partial X\rightarrow X
.
In particular, there is a canonical isomorphism

\mathcal{M}^{\rm gp}(X)\simeq\mathcal{O}^{*}(X-\partial X).

For
Y\in lSm/k
and
V\in\operatorname{lCor}(X,Y)
, we can now define a transfer map

V^{*}\colon\mathcal{M}^{\rm gp}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\rm gp}(Y)

because there is a transfer map

(V-\partial V)^{*}\colon\mathcal{O}^{*}(X-\partial X)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{*}(Y-\partial Y).

As in the above paragraph,
V^{*}
is compatible with compositions of correspondences.
Thus
\mathcal{M}^{\rm gp}
is an example of a presheaf with log transfers.

Next, we give
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
the structure of a closed symmetric monoidal category following the work of Day
[
26
]
in a general setting, and
[
80
]
for presheaves with transfers.

Definition 4.1.6
.

The tensor product of two representable presheaves with log transfers on
lSm/k
is defined by

\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\otimes\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y):=\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X\times_{k}Y).

More generally,
we define the tensor product of
F,G\in\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
by

F\otimes G:=\varinjlim_{X,Y}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\otimes\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y).

Here we write
F
and
G
as colimits of representable presheaves

F\simeq\varinjlim_{X}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\text{ and }G\simeq\varinjlim_{Y}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y),

This gives a symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
.

The internal hom object
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)}(F,G)
is the presheaf with log transfers on
lSm/k
defined by

X\mapsto\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)}(F,G)(X):=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)}(F\otimes\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),G).

The functor
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)}(F,-)
is right adjoint to
(-)\otimes F
.

Let
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
be a morphism of fs log schemes log smooth over
k
.
Then the graph
\gamma_{f}
is a finite log correspondence from
X
to
Y
.
To show this we may assume
X
and
Y
are irreducible.
Note that
\gamma_{f}
is a closed subscheme (see Definition
A.5.11
) of
X\times Y
.
The morphism
\gamma_{f}\rightarrow X
is an isomorphism.
Thus
\gamma_{f}
is an elementary log correspondence from
X
to
Y
by Example
2.1.3
(4).
If
g\colon Y\rightarrow Z
is another morphism of fs log schemes log smooth over
k
,
then by construction
\gamma_{g}\circ\gamma_{f}=\gamma_{g\circ f}
.
Thus there is a faithful functor

\gamma\colon lSm/k\rightarrow lCor/k

(4.1.1)

given by

X\mapsto X,\,(f\colon X\rightarrow Y)\mapsto\gamma_{f}.

Definition 4.1.7
.

Let
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
be the category of presheaves of
\Lambda
-modules on
lSm/k
.
For an fs log scheme
X
of finite type over
k
,
we let
\Lambda(X)
be the presheaf of
\Lambda
-modules on
lSm/k
given by

Y\mapsto\Lambda(X)(Y):=\Lambda\operatorname{Hom}_{lSm/k}(Y,X).

The restriction (forgetting log transfer) functor

\gamma^{*}\colon\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)

is defined by
\gamma^{*}F(X):=F(\gamma(X))
for
F\in\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
and
X\in lSm/k
.

According to
[
2
, Proposition I.5.1]
there exist adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{*}}

By convention
\gamma_{\sharp}
is left adjoint to
\gamma^{*}
and
\gamma^{*}
is left adjoint to
\gamma_{*}
.
This sequence of adjoint functors induces a similar sequence of adjoint functors for the categories of chain complexes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{*}}

We shall use similar notations for the category
Cor/k
of finite correspondences over the field
k
.
Note that the functor
\hat{\gamma}_{*}
in
[
21
, Definition 10.1.1]
corresponds to our
\gamma^{*}
.
Let
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda)
denote the category of presheaves of
\Lambda
-modules with transfers.
For any smooth scheme
X
of finite type over
k
,
the representable presheaf of
\Lambda
-modules with transfers
\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)
on
Sm/k
is given by

Y\mapsto\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)(Y):=\operatorname{Cor}(Y,X)\otimes\Lambda.

We write
\mathbf{Psh}(k,\Lambda)
for the category of presheaves of
\Lambda
-modules on
Sm/k
.
For any smooth scheme
X
of finite type over
k
,
we denote by
\Lambda(X)
the presheaf of
\Lambda
-modules on
Sm/k
defined by

Y\mapsto\Lambda(X)(Y):=\Lambda\operatorname{Hom}_{Sm/k}(Y,X).

In this setting, there is a faithful functor

\gamma\colon Sm/k\rightarrow Cor/k,

and corresponding sequences of adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{*}}

(4.1.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Psh}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}({\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda)}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{*}}

Next, we define functors between the categories of finite log correspondences and finite correspondences.

Definition 4.1.8
.

Let

\omega\colon lCor/k\rightarrow Cor/k

be the functor that sends an object
X
to
X-\partial X
and a morphism
V\in\operatorname{lCor}(X,Y)
to
V-\partial V\in\operatorname{Cor}(X-\partial X,Y-\partial Y)
for all
X,Y\in lSm/k
.

Conversely, let

\lambda\colon Cor/k\rightarrow lCor/k

be the functor sending an object
X
to
X\in lSm/k
equipped with the trivial log structure and a morphism
V\in\operatorname{Cor}(X,Y)
to
V\in l\operatorname{Cor}(X,Y)
for all
X,Y\in Sm/k
,
see Example
2.1.3
.

There are induced functors

\omega^{*}\colon\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)\text{ and }\lambda^{*}\colon\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda)

given by
\omega^{*}F(X):=F(\omega(X))
for
F\in\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda)
and
X\in lSm/k
and
\lambda^{*}G(Y):=G(\lambda(Y))
for
G\in\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
and
Y\in Sm/k
.
Example
2.1.3
(1) shows that
\lambda^{*}
is left adjoint to
\omega^{*}
.

Again by
[
2
, Proposition I.5.1]
there exist adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{*}\simeq\omega_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{*}\simeq\omega^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{*}}

(4.1.3)

Our convention for such diagrams is that
\lambda_{\sharp}
is left adjoint to
\lambda^{*}\simeq\omega_{\sharp}
, which is left adjoint to
\lambda_{*}\simeq\omega^{*}
,
which is left adjoint to
\omega_{*}
.
On chain complexes, we use the same notation for the induced adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{*}\simeq\omega_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{*}\simeq\omega^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{*}}

Definition 4.1.9
.

Let

\omega\colon lSm/k\rightarrow Sm/k

be the functor that sends an object
X
to
X-\partial X
and a morphism
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
to the naturally induced morphism
X-\partial X\rightarrow Y-\partial Y
for all
X,Y\in lSm/k
.

Conversely, let

\lambda\colon Sm/k\rightarrow lSm/k

be the functor sending
X\in Sm/k
to
X
equipped with the trivial log structure and a morphism
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
to itself for all
X,Y\in Sm/k
.

With these definitions, there are associated adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{*}\simeq\omega_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{*}\simeq\omega^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{*}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Psh}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{*}\simeq\omega_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{*}\simeq\omega^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{*}}

and a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces lSm/k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces lCor/k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Sm/k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Cor/k.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}

Definition 4.1.10
.

Suppose
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
is a morphism of fs log schemes log smooth over
k
.
Let
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y\rightarrow X)
(resp.
\Lambda(Y\rightarrow X)
) denote the complex

\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(f)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\;\;(\text{resp.}\ \Lambda(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Lambda(f)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda(X)).

Suppose
\mathscr{X}
is a simplicial fs log scheme over
k
.
Let
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{X})
(resp.
\Lambda(\mathscr{X})
) denote the complex associated with the simplicial object

i\mapsto\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{X}_{i})\;\;(\text{resp.}\ i\mapsto\Lambda(\mathscr{X}_{i}))

under the Dold-Kan correspondence.

Similarly,
when
Y\rightarrow X
is a morphism of schemes smooth over
k
and
\mathscr{X}
is a simplicial scheme over
k
,
we use the notations
\Lambda(Y\rightarrow X)
,
\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(Y\rightarrow X)
,
\Lambda(\mathscr{X})
, and
\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathscr{X})
.

4.2.
Compatibility with log transfers

This section discusses several notions of compatibility with log transfers for topologies on log schemes.
We proceed by adapting to the log setting the analogous notions introduced by Cisinski-Deglise in
[
21
, Definition 10.3.2]
.

Definition 4.2.1
.

Let
t
be a topology on
lSm/k
and
\Lambda
a commutative unital ring.
A presheaf
\mathcal{F}
with log transfers is called a
t
-sheaf with log transfers
if
\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
is a
t
-sheaf.
Here,
\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}(X):=\mathcal{F}(\gamma(X))
for
X\in lSm/k
.
Let
\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
denote the category of
t
-sheaves with log transfers.

Let
\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
be the category of
t
-sheaves on
lSm/k
(without log transfers).
The
t
-sheafification functor
a_{t}^{*}
and the forgetful functor
a_{t*}
form an adjoint functor pair

a_{t}^{*}:\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda):a_{t*}.

By abuse of notation,
let

a_{t*}:\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)

be the inclusion functor, and let

\gamma^{*}\colon\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)

be the restriction of the functor
\gamma^{*}\colon\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
.
We note that

\gamma^{*}a_{t*}\simeq a_{t*}\gamma^{*}.

(4.2.1)

For a topology
t
on
Sm/k
,
let
\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
be the category of
t
-sheaves on
Sm/k
with transfers.

The notion of a topology on
Sm/k
being compatible with transfers was introduced in
[
21
, Definition 10.3.2]
.
We adopt this notion to our log setting.

Definition 4.2.2
.

A topology
t
on
lSm/k
is
compatible with log transfers

if for any
t
-hypercover
\mathscr{X}\rightarrow X
,
the induced morphism

a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{X})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)

of complexes of presheaves is a quasi-isomorphism (see also Definition
4.1.10
).

Following
[
21
, Definition 10.3.5]
, we say that
t
is
mildly compatible with log transfers
if
\gamma_{*}a_{t*}\mathcal{F}
is a
t
-sheaf with log transfers for any
t
-sheaf
\mathcal{F}
.
Recall that
\gamma_{*}
is a right adjoint of
\gamma^{*}
.
There is a description of
\gamma_{*}
: For every presheaf
\mathcal{G}
and
X\in lSm/k
,

\gamma_{*}\mathcal{G}(X)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}(\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),\mathcal{G}).

Remark 4.2.3
.

These notions are equivalent to each other if
t
is a topology associated with a cd-structure, see Proposition
4.3.5
.

Remark 4.2.4
.

According to
[
21
, 10.4.1]
, the Nisnevich topology on
Sm/k
is compatible with transfers.
One goal in this section is to generalize this to our log setting,
i.e., we will show that the strict Nisnevich topology on
lSm/k
is compatible with log transfers.

Proposition 4.2.5
.

Let
t
be a topology on
lSm/k
compatible with log transfers. Then
t
is mildly compatible with log transfers.

Démonstration.

Let
\mathscr{X}\rightarrow X
be a Čech
t
-hypercover, and let
\mathcal{F}
be a
t
-sheaf.
To show that
\gamma_{*}a_{t*}\mathcal{F}
is a
t
-sheaf, we need to show that the induced sequence of
\Lambda
-modules

\begin{split}0\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),\gamma_{*}a_{t*}\mathcal{F})\rightarrow&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{X}_{0}),\gamma_{*}a_{t*}\mathcal{F})\\
\rightarrow&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{X}_{1}),\gamma_{*}a_{t*}\mathcal{F})\end{split}

is exact.
This is equivalent to showing exactness of the sequence

\begin{split}0\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),\mathcal{F})\rightarrow&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{X}_{0}),\mathcal{F})\\
\rightarrow&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{X}_{1}),\mathcal{F}).\end{split}

(4.2.2)

This follows since by assumption we have a quasi-isomorphism

a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{X})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X).

∎

Proposition 4.2.6
.

Let
t
be a topology on
lSm/k
mildly compatible with log transfers.
Then for any
t
-cover
Y\rightarrow X
, the induced morphism of
t
-sheaves
a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)
is an epimorphism.

Démonstration.

As in the proof of Proposition
4.2.5
, this follows because the sequence (
4.2.2
) is exact.
∎

Proposition 4.2.7
.

Let
t
be a topology on
lSm/k
mildly compatible with log transfers.
Then the functor

\gamma^{*}\colon\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)

admits a right adjoint
\gamma_{*}
that commutes with
a_{t*}
.

Démonstration.

Due to the assumption that
t
is mildly compatible with log transfers,
by abuse of notation, we have the functor

\gamma_{*}\colon\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda).

That is, the restriction of the functor
\gamma_{*}\colon\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
.
We note that the functors
a_{t*}
and
\gamma_{*}
commute.

For
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{G}\in\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
it remains to note that there are natural isomorphisms of
\Lambda
-modules

\begin{split}&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}(\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\\
\simeq&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F},a_{t*}\mathcal{G})\quad\text{(since }a_{t*}\text{ is fully faithful)}\\
\simeq&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}(\gamma^{*}a_{t*}\mathcal{F},a_{t*}\mathcal{G})\quad\text{(since }\gamma^{*}\text{ commutes with }a_{t*},\text{ see }\eqref{A.8.1.1})\\
\simeq&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t*}\mathcal{F},\gamma_{*}a_{t*}\mathcal{G})\\
\simeq&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t*}\mathcal{F},a_{t*}\gamma_{*}\mathcal{G})\quad\text{(since }\gamma_{*}\text{ commutes with }a_{t*})\\
\simeq&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)}(\mathcal{F},\gamma_{*}\mathcal{G})\quad\text{(since }a_{t*}\text{ is fully faithful)}.\end{split}

∎

Let us prove an analog of
[
64
, Lemma 6.16]
.

Lemma 4.2.8
.

Let
t
be a topology on
lSm/k
that is mildly compatible with log transfers.
Suppose that
p\colon Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
is a
t
-cover and
f\in\operatorname{lCor}(X,Y)
is a finite log correspondence, where
X,Y,Y^{\prime}\in lSm/k
.
Then there exists a
t
-cover
p^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
and a commutative diagram in
lCor/k

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

(4.2.3)

Démonstration.

By Proposition
4.2.6
, the induced morphism

a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y^{\prime})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)

in
\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
is an epimorphism.
The implication (ii)
\Rightarrow
(i) in
[
2
, Proposition II.5.1]
shows that
X\times_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y^{\prime})\rightarrow X
is a covering sieve.
From this, we immediately deduce the existence of
p^{\prime}
along with (
4.2.3
).
∎

Using this lemma, we prove an analog of
[
64
, Theorem 6.17]
as follows.

Lemma 4.2.9
.

Let
t
be a topology on
lSm/k
that is mildly compatible with log transfers.
Then for every
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
there exists an object
\mathcal{G}\in\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
together with a morphism
u\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{t*}\mathcal{G}
in
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
such that

\gamma^{*}u
is isomorphic to the sheafification
\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}
.
The pair
(\mathcal{G},u)
is unique up to isomorphism.

Démonstration.

Let us first construct
\mathcal{G}\in\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
.
The unit
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\gamma_{*}a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}
induces a morphism

\eta\colon\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\gamma^{*}\gamma_{*}a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}

Here
\gamma^{*}\gamma_{*}a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}
is a
t
-sheaf because
t
is mildly compatible with log transfers.
Hence according to the universal property of the sheafification functor, the latter morphism admits a factorization

\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi}}{{\rightarrow}}\gamma^{*}\gamma_{*}a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}

that is unique up to isomorphism.
Next, we consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}\gamma_{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}\gamma_{*}a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}

Here
ad
(resp.
ad^{\prime}
) is induced by a unit (resp. counit).
Let
\psi^{\prime}
be the composition of the lower horizontal morphisms.
Since the composition of the upper horizontal morphisms is the identity by the unit-counit identity, there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{\prime}}

The diagram still commutes if we replace
\psi^{\prime}
by
{\rm id}
.
By the universal property of sheafification, the morphism

a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}

making the above diagram commutative is unique,
and we deduce
\psi^{\prime}={\rm id}
.
It follows that
\psi
is a monomorphism.

Let
f\in\operatorname{lCor}(X,Y)
be a finite log correspondence, where
X,Y\in lSm/k
, and set
\mathcal{G}=a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}
.
Then
f
and
\psi
induce a diagram of
\Lambda
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}(Y)}
