{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y),\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),\mathcal{G}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi(Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi(X)}

Since
\psi
is a monomorphism, to show the log transfer structure on
\gamma_{*}\alpha_{t*}\mathcal{G}
induces a log transfer structure on
\mathcal{G}
it suffices to show that

\operatorname{Im}(\alpha_{f}\circ\psi(Y))\subset\operatorname{Im}\psi(X).

(4.2.4)

For every
a\in\mathcal{G}(Y)
there exists an element
b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}(Y^{\prime})
for some
t
-cover
q\colon Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
such that the images of
a
and
b^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{G}(Y^{\prime})
coincide with some
a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}(Y^{\prime})
.
According to Lemma
4.2.8
there exists a
t
-cover
p^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
with a commutative diagram in
lCor/k

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

(4.2.5)

Then there is a diagram of
\Lambda
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}(Y^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y^{\prime}),\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}(X^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X^{\prime}),\mathcal{G}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi(Y^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{f^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi(X^{\prime})}

Suppose that
(\alpha_{f^{\prime}}\circ\psi(Y^{\prime}))(a^{\prime})=\psi(X^{\prime})(d^{\prime})
for some
d^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}(X^{\prime})
.
The two images of
a^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{G}(Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime})
are equal since
\mathcal{G}
is a presheaf.
Thus the two images of
\psi(X^{\prime})(d^{\prime})
in
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X^{\prime}\times_{X}X^{\prime}),\mathcal{G})
are equal.
Since
\psi
is a monomorphism, this implies that the two images of
d^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{G}(X^{\prime}\times_{X}X^{\prime})
are equal.
Thus there exists
d\in\mathcal{G}(X)
whose image in
\mathcal{G}(X^{\prime})
is equal to
d^{\prime}
since
\mathcal{G}
is a sheaf.
Using the exactness of (
4.2.2
), we see that

(\alpha_{f}\circ\psi(Y))(a)=\psi(X)(d).

Hence let us show that
(\alpha_{f^{\prime}}\circ\psi(Y^{\prime}))(a^{\prime})=\psi(X^{\prime})(d^{\prime})
for some
d^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}(X^{\prime})
.

The morphism
\eta\colon\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\gamma^{*}\gamma_{*}a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}
induces a commutative diagram of
\Lambda
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(Y^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y^{\prime}),\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(X^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X^{\prime}),\mathcal{G}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta(Y^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(f^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{f^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta(X^{\prime})}

(4.2.6)

This implies that
(\alpha_{f^{\prime}}\circ\psi(Y^{\prime}))(a^{\prime})=\psi(X^{\prime})(d^{\prime})
for some
d^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}(X^{\prime})
.
We deduce the claimed log transfer structure on
\mathcal{G}
.

Since
\psi
is a monomorphism,
combining the diagram (
) for
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
together with (
4.2.4
) we obtain a canonical commutative diagram of
\Lambda
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}(X).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}(f)}

Thus we obtain a morphism
u\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{t*}\mathcal{G}
of presheaves with log transfers such that
\gamma^{*}u
is isomorphic to the sheafification

\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}
.

For uniqueness,
suppose
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is a
t
-sheaf with log transfers that is equipped with a morphism
u^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{t*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
of presheaves with log transfers such that
\gamma^{*}u^{\prime}

is isomorphic to the sheafification
\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}
.
Since
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
agree on objects,
it suffices to check that
\mathcal{G}(f)=\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(f)
for any
f\in\operatorname{lCor}(X,Y)
where
X,Y\in lSm/k
.
Using the commutative diagram (
4.2.5
), we see this question is
t
-local on
X
and
Y
.
Hence after shrinking
Y
we may assume that for any
a\in\mathcal{G}(Y)
, there is an element
b\in\mathcal{F}(Y)
whose image in
\mathcal{G}(Y)
is equal to
a
.
The morphisms
u\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{t*}\mathcal{G}
and
u^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{t*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
induce a commutative diagram of
\Lambda
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}(X).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u(X)}

With the identification of
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
on objects, the homomorphisms
u(X)
and
u^{\prime}(X)
become equal.
This allows us to conclude

\mathcal{G}(f)(a)=u(X)(\mathcal{F}(f)(b))=u^{\prime}(X)(\mathcal{F}(f)(b))=\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(f)(a).

∎

Proposition 4.2.10
.

Let
t
be a topology on
lSm/k
mildly compatible with log transfers.
Then the functor

a_{t*}\colon\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)

admits a left adjoint functor
a_{t}^{*}
.
Moreover, the functor
a_{t}^{*}
is exact.

Démonstration.

For
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
we define
a_{t}^{*}\mathcal{F}\colon=\mathcal{H}
,
where
\mathcal{H}\in\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
is constructed as in Lemma
4.2.9
.
The uniqueness part in Lemma
4.2.9
ensures that we obtain a functor

a_{t}^{*}\colon\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda).

It remains to show
a_{t}^{*}
is a left adjoint of
a_{t*}
.
Again, by uniqueness, the morphism
u\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{t*}\mathcal{H}
in
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
constructed in Lemma
4.2.9
gives rise to a natural transformation

{\rm id}\rightarrow a_{t*}a_{t}^{*}.

For
\mathcal{G}\in\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
, the uniqueness part also implies that there is an isomorphism

a_{t}^{*}a_{t*}\mathcal{G}\simeq\mathcal{G}.

By functoriality of this isomorphism, we obtain a natural transformation

a_{t}^{*}a_{t*}\rightarrow{\rm id}.

Owing to the uniqueness part in Lemma
4.2.9
,
the unit and counit triangle identities follow from the corresponding identities for
a_{t}^{*}\colon\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
and
a_{t*}\colon\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
.

Since
a_{t}^{*}
is a left adjoint,
a_{t}^{*}
is right exact.
It remains to show that
a_{t}^{*}
is left exact.
Let
f\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
be a monomorphism of presheaves with log transfers.
The functor
\gamma^{*}
is exact since
\gamma^{*}
has both left and right adjoints, and the functor

a_{t}^{*}\colon\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)

is known to be exact.
It follows that

a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}f\colon a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}

is a monomorphism of
t
-sheaves.
Since
a_{t}^{*}
commutes with
\gamma^{*}
by Proposition
4.2.7
,

\gamma^{*}a_{t}^{*}f\colon\gamma^{*}a_{t}^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\gamma^{*}a_{t}^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}

is a monomorphism of
t
-sheaves.
Let
\mathcal{G}
be a kernel of
a_{t}^{*}f\colon a_{t}^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
.
It follows that
\gamma^{*}\mathcal{G}=0
.
This means that
\mathcal{G}(X)=0
for all
X\in lSm/k
, so
\mathcal{G}=0
.
Thus
a_{t}^{*}f
is a monomorphism.
∎

Proposition 4.2.11
.

Let
t
be a topology on
lSm/k
mildly compatible with log transfers.
Then the functor

\gamma^{*}\colon\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)

admits a left adjoint functor
\gamma_{\sharp}
.

Démonstration.

For
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{G}\in\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
, we have isomorphisms

\begin{split}&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t}^{*}\gamma_{\sharp}a_{t*}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\\
\simeq&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t*}\mathcal{F},\gamma^{*}a_{t*}\mathcal{G})\\
\simeq&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t*}\mathcal{F},a_{t*}\gamma^{*}\mathcal{G})\quad\text{(since }\gamma^{*}\text{ and }a_{t*}\text{ commute)}\\
\simeq&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)}(\mathcal{F},\gamma^{*}\mathcal{G})\quad\text{(since }a_{t*}\text{ is fully faithful}).\end{split}

By functoriality in
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
, it follows that
a_{t}^{*}\gamma_{\sharp}a_{t*}
is a left adjoint of
\gamma^{*}
.
∎

We recall the definition of Grothendieck abelian category (see e.g.,
[
85
, Chapter V]
for details).
An abelian category
\mathcal{A}
is called a
Grothendieck abelian category
with the following conditions.

(i)

\mathcal{A}
admits small sums.

(ii)

Filtered colimits of exact sequences in
\mathcal{A}
is exact.

(iii)

There is a generator of
\mathcal{A}
, i.e., there is an element of
G\in\mathcal{A}
such that the functor
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(G,-)\colon\mathcal{A}\rightarrow{\bf Set}
is faithful.

Proposition 4.2.12
.

Let
t
be a topology on
lSm/k
mildly compatible with log transfers. Then
\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
is a Grothendieck abelian category.

Démonstration.

Let us check the above axioms of a Grothendieck abelian category.
The categories
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
and
\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
are Grothendieck abelian categories, and
\gamma^{*}\colon\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
is exact since
\gamma^{*}
has both left and right adjoints.
Thus
\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
is an abelian category, and we have (i) and (ii).
Since by the Yoneda lemma

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),\mathcal{F})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),\mathcal{F})\simeq\mathcal{F}(X),

the object
\bigoplus_{X\in lSm/k}a_{t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)
is a generator of
\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
. Thus we have (iii).
∎

In conclusion,
if
t
is a topology on
lSm/k
mildly compatible with log transfers,
there exist adjunctions between Grothendieck abelian categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{*}}

(4.2.7)

such that the following properties hold :

(i)

a_{t}^{*}
is left adjoint to the corresponding
a_{t*}

(ii)

\gamma^{*}
is left (resp. right) adjoint to the corresponding
\gamma_{*}
(resp.
\gamma_{\sharp}
)

(iii)

a_{t*}\gamma_{*}\simeq\gamma_{*}a_{t*}

(iv)

a_{t*}\gamma^{*}\simeq\gamma^{*}a_{t*}

Proposition 4.2.13
.

Let
t
be a topology on
lSm/k
.
Suppose that for every
t
-cover
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
the complex

\cdots\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y\times_{X}Y)\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\rightarrow 0

(4.2.8)

associated with the Čech nerve of
f
is acyclic.
Then
t
is compatible with log transfers.

Démonstration.

Let
p\colon\mathscr{X}\rightarrow X
be a
t
-hypercover.
To check that

a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{X})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)

is a quasi-isomorphism it suffices to check that

a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{X}_{i})\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{X}_{0})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\rightarrow 0

is exact for all integer
i\geq 0
.
Hence we may assume that
\mathscr{X}
is a bounded
t
-hypercover of height
n
.

To proceed, we use the technique in the proof of
[
32
, Proposition A4]
.
If
n=0
, then
\mathscr{X}
is a Čech nerve, so we are done.
Suppose that
n>0
, and consider the bounded
t
-hypercover

\mathscr{Y}:={\rm cosk}_{n-1}\mathscr{X}

of height at most
n-1
.
By induction we have that
a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{Y})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)
is a quasi-isomorphism.
The Čech nerve of the canonical morphism
\mathscr{X}\rightarrow\mathscr{Y}
is a bisimplicial complex ;
let
\mathscr{D}
denote its diagonal.
Since (
4.2.8
) is acyclic for every
t
-cover, there is a canonically induced quasi-isomorphism

a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{D})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{Y}).

In particular,

a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{D})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)
is a quasi-isomorphism.
Since
\mathscr{X}
is a retract of
\mathscr{D}
,
see the proof of
[
32
, Proposition A4]
,
we deduce that

a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{X})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)

is a quasi-isomorphism.
∎

4.3.
Derived categories of sheaves with log transfers

For miscellaneous facts about model structures on chain complexes we refer the reader to Appendix
B
.
Let
t
be a topology on
lSm/k
mildly compatible with log transfers.
Passing to chain complexes in (
4.2.7
) we obtain the diagram of adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}

(4.3.1)

We refer to Example
B.1.7
for the descent structures on
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
and
\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
.
The functors
\gamma^{*}
in (
4.2.7
) are exact since they have both left and right adjoints.
If the topology
t
is compatible with log transfers,
then for any
t
-hypercover
\mathscr{X}\rightarrow X
,
the cone of the morphism
a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{X})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)
is quasi-isomorphic to
0
.
Thus by Proposition
B.1.10
, we also obtain descent structures on
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
and
\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
, and the
(\gamma_{\sharp},\gamma^{*})
’s are Quillen pairs with respect to the said descent model structures.
Note also that the
a_{t}^{*}
’s satisfy the conditions of Proposition
B.1.9
due to exactness.
Owing to Propositions
B.1.9
and
B.1.10
, the
(a_{t}^{*},a_{t*})
’s are Quillen pairs with respect to the descent model structures.

Since
\gamma^{*}
preserves quasi-isomorphisms and monomorphisms,
the adjunctions

\gamma^{*}\colon\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))\colon\gamma_{*},

\gamma^{*}\colon\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))\colon\gamma_{*}

are Quillen pairs with respect to the injective model structures ; see Definition
B.1.3
for injective model structures.
Thus we have an induced diagram of adjunctions between triangulated categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ra_{t*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\gamma_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\gamma_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ra_{t*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\gamma_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\gamma_{*}}

(4.3.2)

where

(i)

L\gamma^{*}\simeq\gamma^{*}\simeq R\gamma^{*}
since
\gamma^{*}
is exact,

(ii)

a_{t}^{*}\simeq La_{t}^{*}
since
a_{t}^{*}
is exact, see Proposition
4.2.10
for the log transfer case.

Suppose that
\mathcal{W}
is an essentially small class of morphisms in
lSm/k
that is stable by isomorphisms, products, and compositions.
We consider
\mathcal{W}
as classes of morphisms of representable objects in
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
,
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
,
\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
, and
\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
.
Note that
a_{t}^{*}
preserves
\mathcal{W}
because the object of
\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
(resp.
\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)
) representable by
X\in lSm/k
is
a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X)
(resp.
a_{t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)
).
The four adjunctions in (
4.3.1
) are Quillen adjunctions with respect to the
\mathcal{W}
-local descent model structures furnished by Propositions
B.1.11
and
B.1.12
.
Thus we have the following induced diagram of adjunctions between triangulated categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ra_{t*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\gamma_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\gamma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ra_{t*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{t}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\gamma_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\gamma^{*}}

(4.3.3)

Here
a_{t}^{*}\simeq La_{t}^{*}
since
a_{t}^{*}
is exact by Proposition
4.2.10
and
a_{t}^{*}
preserves
\mathcal{W}
.

Definition 4.3.1
.

Let
t
be a topology on
lSm/k
, and let
\mathcal{F}
be a complex of
t
-sheaves with log transfers.
For
X\in lSm/k
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
we define hypercohomology groups by setting

\mathbf{H}_{t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F}):=\mathbf{H}_{t}^{i}(X,\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}).

Proposition 4.3.2
.

Let
t
be a topology on
lSm/k
compatible with log transfers.
Then for all
X\in lSm/k
and every complex
\mathcal{F}
of
t
-sheaves with log transfers, there is a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))}(a_{t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),\mathcal{F}[i])\simeq\mathbf{H}_{t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F}).

Démonstration.

The functor

L\gamma_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))\rightarrow\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))

in (
4.3.2
) is left adjoint to

\gamma^{*}\colon\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))\rightarrow\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)).

Thus the group
\mathbf{H}_{t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})
can be identified with

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}(a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X),\gamma^{*}\mathcal{F}[i])\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))}(L\gamma_{\sharp}a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X),\mathcal{F}[i]).

The object
a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X)
is cofibrant, see Definition
B.1.2
.
The functor
a_{t}^{*}
commutes with
\gamma_{\sharp}
due to (
4.2.1
).
Thus we deduce

L\gamma_{\sharp}a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X)\simeq\gamma_{\sharp}a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X)\simeq a_{t}^{*}\gamma_{\sharp}\Lambda(X)\simeq a_{t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X).

∎

We will need the following lemma in our discussion of adjunctions.

Lemma 4.3.3
.

Suppose
F\colon C\rightarrow D
is a left and a right Quillen functor between model categories.
Then
F
preserves cofibrations, fibrations, and weak equivalences.

Démonstration.

For the convenience of the reader, we shall outline a proof ; see also the discussion in
[
21
, Remark 1.3.23]
.
By assumption,
F
preserves cofibrations, fibrations, trivial cofibrations, and trivial fibrations.
It remains to show that
F
preserves weak equivalences.
If
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
be a weak equivalence in
C
, we can choose a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where
g
and
g^{\prime}
are trivial fibrations and
f^{\prime}
is a weak equivalence between cofibrant objects.
Then
F(g)
and
F(g^{\prime})
are weak equivalences.
Ken Brown’s lemma implies that
F(f^{\prime})
is a weak equivalence.
It follows that
F(f)
is also a weak equivalence.
∎

Let
t
be a topology on
lSm/k
, and let
t^{\prime}
be the restriction of
t
to
Sm/k
.
Then the functor
\lambda
from
Sm/k
with the topology
t^{\prime}
to
lSm/k
with the topology
t
is a continuous functor of sites.
Thus we have an induced adjunction

\lambda_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{Shv}_{t^{\prime}}(k,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)\colon\lambda^{*}.

This induces an adjunction

\lambda_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{t^{\prime}}(k,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)\colon\lambda^{*}.

Assume
t
is compatible with log transfers and
t^{\prime}
is compatible with transfers.
By appealing to Proposition
B.1.9
, we obtain a Quillen pair for the descent model structures

\lambda_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{t^{\prime}}(k,\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))\colon\lambda^{*}

since
t^{\prime}
is the restriction of
t
, for every
t^{\prime}
-hypercover
\mathscr{X}\rightarrow X
we obtain a
t
-hypercover

\lambda(\mathscr{X})\rightarrow\lambda(X).

On the level of homotopy categories, there is an induced adjunction

L\lambda_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{t^{\prime}}(k,\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))\colon R\lambda^{*}.

Assume that for any
t
-cover
Y\rightarrow X
, the naturally induced morphism

\omega(Y)=Y-\partial Y\rightarrow X-\partial X=\omega(X)

is also a
t
-cover.
Then
\omega
is a continuous functor of sites from
lSm/k
with the topology
t
to
Sm/k
with the topology
t^{\prime}
.
There are induced adjunctions

\omega_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(k,\Lambda)\colon\omega^{*}

and

\omega_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{t^{\prime}}(k,\Lambda)\colon\omega^{*}.

(4.3.4)

By assumption
\omega
maps any
t
-covers to
t^{\prime}
-covers,
and for any
t
-hypercover
\mathscr{X}\rightarrow X
,
\omega(\mathscr{X})\rightarrow\omega(X)
is a
t^{\prime}
-hypercover.
According to Proposition
B.1.9
there is a Quillen pair for the descent model structures

\omega_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{t^{\prime}}(k,\Lambda))\colon\omega^{*}.

We also note the induced adjunction

L\omega_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{t^{\prime}}(k,\Lambda))\colon R\omega^{*}.

Since
\lambda
is left adjoint to
\omega
, by Lemma
4.3.3
we deduce

R\lambda^{*}\simeq\lambda^{*}\simeq\omega_{\sharp}\simeq L\omega_{\sharp}.

In conclusion, there is a sequence of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{t^{\prime}}(k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\lambda_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{*}\simeq\omega_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}}

(4.3.5)

As above,
suppose
\mathcal{W}
is an essentially small class of morphisms in
lSm/k
that is stable by isomorphisms, products, and compositions.
Then, by Proposition
B.1.11
, we obtain a Quillen pair for the
\mathcal{W}
-local and
\omega(\mathcal{W})
-local descent model structures

\omega_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{t^{\prime}}(k,\Lambda))\colon\omega^{*}.

Thus we have the induced adjunction

L\omega_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{D}_{\omega(\mathcal{W})}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{t^{\prime}}(k,\Lambda))\colon R\omega^{*},

(4.3.6)

and similarly for

L\omega_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{D}_{\omega(\mathcal{W})}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(k,\Lambda))\colon R\omega^{*}.

Lemma 4.3.4
.

Let
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
be a monomorphism of fs log schemes.
Then the naturally induced morphism of presheaves

\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(f)\colon\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\rightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)

is a monomorphism.

Démonstration.

For
T\in lSm/k
, there is a naturally induced commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{lCor}(T,X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{lCor}(T,Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Cor}(T-\partial T,X-\partial X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Cor}(T-\partial T,Y-\partial Y).}

We need to show that the upper horizontal homomorphism is injective.
The induced morphism
X-\partial X\rightarrow Y-\partial Y
is also a monomorphism.
Thus the lower horizontal homomorphism is injective, see
[
64
, Exercise 12.22]
.
The vertical homomorphisms are injective due to Lemma
2.3.2
, which finishes the proof.
∎

Next we specialize to the setting of cd-structures.

Proposition 4.3.5
.

Let
P
be a complete, quasi-bounded, and regular cd-structure on
lSm/k
, and let
t
be the associated topology.
Then the following conditions are equivalent.

(i)

t
is compatible with log transfers.

(ii)

t
is mildly compatible with log transfers.

(iii)

For any
P
-distinguished square in
lSm/k
of the form
(
3.1.1
)
, the sequence

0\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y^{\prime})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)\oplus a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X^{\prime})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\rightarrow 0

(4.3.7)

of
t
-sheaves with log transfers is exact.

Démonstration.

The implication (i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) is Proposition
4.2.5
.
Assuming condition (ii) the induced sequence of
t
-sheaves

0\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(Y^{\prime})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(Y)\oplus a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X^{\prime})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X)\rightarrow 0

(4.3.8)

is exact by
[
91
, Lemma 2.18]
.
Let us show that the following naturally induced sequence of
t
-sheaves with log transfers is exact :

0\rightarrow\gamma_{\sharp}a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(Y^{\prime})\rightarrow\gamma_{\sharp}a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(Y)\oplus\gamma_{\sharp}a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X^{\prime})\rightarrow\gamma_{\sharp}a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X)\rightarrow 0.

(4.3.9)

Since
\gamma_{\sharp}
commutes with
a_{t}^{*}
, this is equivalent to showing that the sequence

0\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y^{\prime})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)\oplus a_{t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X^{\prime})\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\rightarrow 0

(4.3.10)

of
t
-sheaves with log transfers is exact.
Since
\gamma_{\sharp}
is a left adjoint,
\gamma_{\sharp}
is right exact.
Thus
(
4.3.9
) is exact at
\gamma_{\sharp}a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(Y)\oplus\gamma_{\sharp}a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X^{\prime})
and
\gamma_{\sharp}a_{t}^{*}\Lambda(X)
.
Since
P
is regular, the morphism
Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
is a monomorphism.
Thus
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y^{\prime})\rightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)
is a monomorphism owing to Lemma
4.3.4
.
This gives the exactness of (
4.3.10
) at
a_{t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y^{\prime})
,
i.e.,
(
4.3.9
) and (
4.3.10
) are exact.
We deduce that (
4.3.7
) is exact since
\gamma^{*}
commutes with
a_{t}^{*}
and
\gamma^{*}
is exact.
This implies condition (iii).

Let us assume (iii) holds.
To conclude (ii), we need to show that (
4.2.2
) is exact when
\mathscr{X}\rightarrow X
is a Čech nerve associated with a
t
-cover
T\rightarrow X
.
Every
t
-cover has a simple refinement because
P
is complete, see
[
91
, Definition 2.3]
.
Thus since
t
is the topology associated with
P
, it suffices to consider the case when
T\rightarrow X
is of the form
Y\amalg X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
.
In this case, our claim follows from the exactness of (
4.3.7
).

Now assume (ii) and (iii) hold.
It remains to show (i).
We work with the descent structure
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
(resp.
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H}^{\prime})
) of
\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
given in Example
B.1.7
(resp. Example
3.4.13
).
Let
G\in\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))
be an injective fibrant complex.
Since the sequence (
4.3.9
) is exact, we have

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf K}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}(F^{\prime},\gamma^{*}G)=0

for all
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.
Thus
\gamma^{*}G
is
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
-flasque,
i.e.,

\gamma^{*}G
is
\mathcal{G}
-fibrant.
By Propositions
B.1.4
(2) we see that
\gamma^{*}G
is
\mathcal{H}
-flasque.
Thus for all
F\in\mathcal{H}
, we have the vanishing

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf K}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}(F,\gamma^{*}G)=0.

Since
G
is an injective fibrant complex, we also have

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf K}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}(F,\gamma^{*}G)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf K}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))}(\gamma_{\sharp}F,G)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{t}(k,\Lambda))}(\gamma_{\sharp}F,G).

Here
G
is an arbitrary injective fibrant complex.
Hence
\gamma_{\sharp}F
is quasi-isomorphic to
0
for all
F\in\mathcal{H}
.
This implies that condition (i) holds.
∎

4.4.
Structure of dividing Nisnevich covers

In this section, we shall study dividing Nisnevich covers and the dividing Nisnevich sheafification functor.
We also discuss their étale versions.

Suppose that
X
is an excellent reduced noetherian scheme.
In
[
87
, Theorem 3.1.9]
it is shown that every
h
-cover
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
of schemes admits a refinement of the form

Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Y_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Y_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g_{3}}}{{\longrightarrow}}X,

where
g_{1}
is a Zariski cover,
g_{2}
is a finite surjective morphism, and
g_{3}
is a proper surjective birational morphism.
This is one of the fundamental tools in the theory of
h
-coverings.

We provide a result with a similar flavor for factorizations of dividing Nisnevich, dividing étale, and log étale covers.

Proposition 4.4.1
.

Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
be a dividing Nisnevich (resp. dividing étale, resp. log étale) cover in
lSm/k
.
Then there exists a log modification
h\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
such that the pullback

g\colon Y\times_{X}X^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}

is a strict Nisnevich (resp. strict étale, resp. integral Kummer étale) cover.

In particular,
f
has a refinement of the form

Y\times_{X}X^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\rightarrow}}X^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\rightarrow}}X,

(4.4.1)

where
g
is a strict Nisnevich (resp. strict étale, resp. integral log étale) cover and
h
is a log modification.

Démonstration.

If
f
is log étale, see Theorem
A.14.13
.
We focus the attention on the dividing Nisnevich topology since the proofs are similar for the dividing Nisnevich and dividing étale cases.
First, we reduce to the case when
f
has a refinement in the form

Y_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{0}}}{{\rightarrow}}Y_{0}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{0}^{\prime}}}{{\rightarrow}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{n-2}^{\prime}}}{{\rightarrow}}Y_{n-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{n-1}}}{{\rightarrow}}Y_{n-1}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{n-1}^{\prime}}}{{\rightarrow}}Y_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{n}}}{{\rightarrow}}X,

where each
f_{i}
is a strict Nisnevich cover and each
f_{i}^{\prime}
is a log modification.
Owing to Proposition
A.14.14
, there exists a log modification
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
such that the pullback

Y_{n-1}^{\prime}\times_{X}X^{\prime}\rightarrow Y_{n}\times_{X}X^{\prime}

of
f_{n-1}^{\prime}
is an isomorphism. In particular, the pullback

Y_{n-1}\times_{X}X^{\prime}\rightarrow Y_{n}\times_{X}X^{\prime}

of the composition
Y_{n-1}\rightarrow X
is a strict Nisnevich cover.

Replacing (
4.4.1
) by

Y_{0}^{\prime}\times_{X}X^{\prime}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow Y_{n-1}\times_{X}X^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime},

we can apply the above process since
X^{\prime}\in lSm/k
.
In this way, we obtain a log modification
X^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow X
such that the pullback

Y_{0}\times_{X}X^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime\prime}

of the composition
Y_{0}\rightarrow X
is a strict Nisnevich cover.
∎

Recall from Definition
A.11.12
that
X_{div}
denotes the category of log modifications over
X\in lSm/k
,
and
X_{div}^{Sm}
is the full subcategory of
X_{div}
consisting of log modifications
Y\rightarrow X
such that
Y\in SmlSm/k
.

Lemma 4.4.2
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a strict Nisnevich (resp. strict étale, resp. Kummer étale) sheaf on
SmlSm/k
.
There is an isomorphism

\begin{split}a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq&\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\mathcal{F}(Y)\\
\text{ (resp.\ }a_{d\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\mathcal{F}(Y),&\text{ resp.\ }a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\mathcal{F}(Y)\text{)}.\end{split}

(4.4.2)

Démonstration.

We will only consider the strict Nisnevich case since the proofs are similar.
Let
X_{dNis}
denote the small dividing Nisnevich site of
X
.
Recall from
[
2
, Remarque II.3.3]
that there is a functor
L_{dNis}
such that
a_{dNis}^{*}=L_{dNis}\circ L_{dNis}
given by

L_{dNis}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{X^{\prime}\in X_{dNis}}\operatorname{Ker}(\mathcal{F}(X^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(+,-)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{F}(X^{\prime}\times_{X}X^{\prime})).

The notation is shorthand for the difference between the two morphisms induced by the projections
X^{\prime}\times_{X}X^{\prime}\longrightarrow X^{\prime}
.

By Proposition
4.4.1
, for any dividing Nisnevich covering
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
, there is a refinement

Y^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\rightarrow}}Y^{\prime\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\rightarrow}}X

such that
g
is a strict Nisnevich cover and
h
is a log modification.
Further, we may assume that
Y^{\prime\prime}\in SmlSm/k
by applying Proposition
A.10.2
.
Since

Y^{\prime}\times_{X}Y^{\prime}\simeq Y^{\prime}\times_{Y^{\prime\prime}}Y^{\prime\prime}\times_{X}Y^{\prime\prime}\times_{Y^{\prime\prime}}Y^{\prime}\simeq Y^{\prime}\times_{Y^{\prime\prime}}Y^{\prime},

we have

\operatorname{Ker}(\mathcal{F}(Y^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(+,-)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{F}(Y^{\prime}\times_{X}Y^{\prime}))\simeq\operatorname{Ker}(\mathcal{F}(Y^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(+,-)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{F}(Y^{\prime}\times_{Y^{\prime\prime}}Y^{\prime}))\simeq\mathcal{F}(Y^{\prime\prime})

because
\mathcal{F}
is assumed to be a strict Nisnevich sheaf.
This gives the identification

L_{dNis}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\mathcal{F}(Y).

Let us show that
L_{dNis}\mathcal{F}
is a strict Nisnevich sheaf.
For every strict Nisnevich cover
f\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
the naturally induced functor
f^{*}\colon X_{div}^{Sm}\rightarrow X_{div}^{\prime Sm}

is cofinal owing to Propositions
A.11.15
and
A.14.14
.
Thus to show that
L_{dNis}\mathcal{F}
is a strict Nisnevich sheaf, it suffices to show there is an exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\mathcal{F}(Y)\rightarrow\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\mathcal{F}(Y\times_{X}X^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\mathcal{F}(Y\times_{X}X^{\prime}\times_{X}X^{\prime}).

Owing to Proposition
A.11.16

X_{div}^{Sm}
is a filtered category.
Since filtered colimits preserve exact sequences, it suffices to show there is an exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(Y)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(Y\times_{X}X^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(Y\times_{X}X^{\prime}\times_{X}X^{\prime})

for all
Y\in X_{div}
.
This follows from the assumption that
\mathcal{F}
is a strict Nisnevich sheaf.

Applying the above to
L_{dNis}\mathcal{F}
we deduce the isomorphisms

a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Z\in Y_{div}^{Sm}}\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Z\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\mathcal{F}(X).

∎

Lemma 4.4.3
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a strict Nisnevich (resp. strict étale, resp. Kummer étale) sheaf on
lSm/k
.
There is an isomorphim

\begin{split}a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq&\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}}\mathcal{F}(Y)\\
\text{ (resp.\ }a_{d\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}}\mathcal{F}(Y),&\text{ resp.\ }a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}}\mathcal{F}(Y)\text{)}.\end{split}

(4.4.3)

Démonstration.

The proof is parallel to that of Lemma
4.4.2
if we use Proposition
A.11.14
instead of Proposition
A.11.16
.
∎

For the definition of a log modification along a smooth center, we refer to Definition
A.14.10
.

Definition 4.4.4
.

Let
X_{divsc}^{Sm}
be the full subcategory of
X_{div}^{Sm}
given by maps
Y\rightarrow X
that are isomorphic to a composition of log modifications along a smooth center.

Corollary 4.4.5
.

If
X\in lSm/k
, then the category
X_{divsc}^{Sm}
is cofinal in
X_{div}^{Sm}
.

Démonstration.

Immediate from Theorem
A.14.12
.
∎

Lemma 4.4.6
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a strict Nisnevich (resp. strict étale, resp. Kummer étale) sheaf on
SmlSm/k
.
There is an isomorphism

\begin{split}a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq&\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{divsc}^{Sm}}\mathcal{F}(Y)\\
\text{ (resp.\ }a_{d\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{divsc}^{Sm}}\mathcal{F}(Y),&\text{ resp.\ }a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{divsc}^{Sm}}\mathcal{F}(Y)\text{)}.\end{split}

Démonstration.

Immediate from Lemma
4.4.2
and Corollary
4.4.5
.
∎

4.5.
Examples of topologies compatible with log transfers

This section shows that the strict Nisnevich, strict étale, Kummer étale, dividing Nisnevich, dividing étale, and Kummer étale topologies are compatible with log transfers.
We refer to §
3.1
for the definitions of these topologies.
For the strict Nisnevich topology, we use arguments similar to
[
64
, Lemma 6.2, Proposition 6.12]
originating in
[
89
]
.

Proposition 4.5.1
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme log smooth over
k
. Then
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)
is a strict étale sheaf.

Démonstration.

For fs log schemes
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
log smooth over
k
we have

\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y_{1}\amalg Y_{2})=\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y_{1})\oplus\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y_{2}).

Thus it suffices to check that the sequence of
\Lambda
-modules

0\rightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y)\rightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(U)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(+,-)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(U\times_{Y}U)

(4.5.1)

is exact for every strict étale cover
p\colon U\rightarrow Y
.

There is an induced commutative diagram of
\Lambda
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X-\partial X)(Y-\partial Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X-\partial X)(U-\partial U).}

By
[
64
, Lemma 6.2]
,
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X-\partial X)
is an étale sheaf.
Thus the lower horizontal morphism is injective.
By Lemma
2.3.2
, the vertical morphisms are injective.
Thus the upper horizontal morphism is also injective.

It remains to show that the sequence (
4.5.1
) is exact at
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(U)
.
To that end, we consider the cartesian square of fs log schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U-\partial U)\times(X-\partial X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y-\partial Y)\times(X-\partial X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times X.}

Suppose
W\in\operatorname{lCor}(U,X)
is a finite log correspondence with trivial image in
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(U\times_{Y}U)
,
and form the finite correspondence

W-\partial W\in\operatorname{Cor}(U-\partial U,X-\partial X).

Since
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X-\partial X)
is an étale sheaf, there exists a finite correspondence

V^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Cor}(Y-\partial Y,X-\partial X)

mapping to
W-\partial W
.
We let
\underline{V}
be the closure of
V^{\prime}
in
\underline{Y}\times\underline{X}
.

Consider the induced morphism
u\colon\underline{W}\rightarrow\underline{U}\times_{\underline{Y}}\underline{V}
of closed subschemes of
\underline{U}\times\underline{X}
.
By construction its pullback to
(U-\partial U)\times(X-\partial X)
is the isomorphism

W-\partial W\rightarrow(U-\partial U)\times_{(Y-\partial Y)}(V-\partial V)

Since
\underline{p}\colon\underline{U}\rightarrow\underline{Y}
is strict étale and
V-\partial V
is dense in
\underline{V}
,
it follows that

(U-\partial U)\times_{(Y-\partial Y)}(V-\partial V)

is dense in
\underline{U}\times_{\underline{Y}}\underline{V}
.
Moreover,

W-\partial W
is dense in
\underline{W}
because
u
is a closed immersion.
We note that
\underline{V}
is finite over
\underline{Y}
by
[
30
, IV.2.7.1(xv)]
since
\underline{U}\times_{\underline{Y}}\underline{V}
is finite over
\underline{U}

and
\underline{p}\colon\underline{U}\rightarrow\underline{Y}
is an étale cover.
There is a naturally induced morphism
v\colon\underline{W}\rightarrow\underline{V}
.
Since
u
is an isomorphism,
v
is a pullback of
p
, and hence
v
is étale.

Let
V^{N}
be the fs log scheme whose underlying scheme is the normalization of
\underline{V}
and whose log structure is induced by
Y
.
By
[
30
, 11.3.13(ii)]
the underlying fiber product
\underline{V^{N}}\times_{\underline{V}}\underline{W}
is normal since
v
is étale.
Thus we have the cartesian square of schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{W^{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{V^{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{V}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}

The log transfer structure gives rise to a morphism
r\colon W^{N}\rightarrow X
of fs log schemes over
k
.
By assumption,
we can identify the two composite morphisms in the diagram

W^{N}\times_{U}(U\times_{Y}U)\rightrightarrows W^{N}\overset{r}{\rightarrow}X.

Since
p
is strict étale there is a morphism
V^{N}\rightarrow X
of fs log schemes over
k
such that the composition
W^{N}\rightarrow V^{N}\rightarrow X
is equal to
r
,
see
[
76
, Corollary III.1.4.5]
.
The pair
(\underline{V},V^{N}\rightarrow X)
gives a finite log correspondence
V
from
X
to
Y
since
\underline{V}
is finite over
\underline{Y}
,
and the pullback of
V
to
U
is
W
.
∎

Proposition 4.5.2
.

Let
t
be one of the following topologies : strict Nisnevich, strict étale, and Kummer étale topologies.
For every
t
-cover
f\colon U\rightarrow X
of fs log schemes in
lSm/k
,
the Čech complex

\cdots\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(U\times_{X}U)\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(U)\rightarrow a_{t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\rightarrow 0

(4.5.2)

is exact as a complex of
t
-sheaves.

Démonstration.

Arguing as in the proof of
[
64
, Proposition 6.12]
we check there is an exact sequence of
\Lambda
-modules

\ \cdots\rightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(U\times_{X}U)(T)\rightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(U)(T)\rightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(T)\rightarrow 0,

(4.5.3)

where
\underline{T}
is a hensel local scheme (resp.
\underline{T}
is strictly local, resp.
T
is log strictly local) if
t=sNis
(resp.
t=s\acute{e}t
, resp.
t=k\acute{e}t
).
Here
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}({X})(T)
denotes the colimit
\varinjlim\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(T_{i})
when
T
is a limit of fs log schemes
T_{i}
log smooth over
k
.

Let
Z
be a saturated log scheme over
T
such that
\underline{Z}
quasi-finite over
\underline{T}
.
We denote by
L(Z/T)
the free abelian group generated by the pairs

(\underline{W},W^{N}\rightarrow Z),

where
\underline{W}
is an irreducible component of
\underline{Z}
that is finite and surjective over a component of
T
,

W^{N}
is the saturated log scheme whose underlying scheme is the normalization of
\underline{W}
and whose log structure is induced by
T
,
and
W^{N}\rightarrow Z
is a morphism of saturated log schemes.
We note that
L(Z/T)
is covariantly functorial on
Z
.
Now (
4.5.3
) is the colimit of the complexes of the form

\cdots\rightarrow L((Z_{U})_{Z}^{2}/T)\rightarrow L(Z_{U}/T)\rightarrow L(Z/T)\rightarrow 0,

where

Z_{U}:=Z\times_{X}U,\;(Z_{U})_{Z}^{n}:=\underbrace{Z_{U}\times_{Z}\cdots\times_{Z}Z_{U}}_{n\text{ times}}.

The colimit runs over strictly closed subschemes
Z
of
T\times X
such that
Z
is strict, finite, and surjective over
T
.
Hence it suffices to check that the latter sequence is exact.

Note that
Z
is henselian (resp. strictly henselian, resp. log strictly henselian) being finite over the henselian (resp. strictly henselian, resp. log strictly henselian) saturated log scheme
T
, see
A.12.4
for the log strictly henselian case.
Thus the strict Nisnevich (resp. strict étale, resp. Kummer étale) cover
Z_{U}\rightarrow Z
splits, see Lemma
A.12.6
for the Kummer étale case.
Letting
s\colon Z\rightarrow Z_{U}
be a splitting,
the maps

L(s\times_{Z}{\rm id})\colon L((Z_{U})_{Z}^{n})\rightarrow L((Z_{U})_{Z}^{n+1})

furnish contracting homotopies, and the desired exactness follows.
∎

Remark 4.5.3
.

Combining Propositions
4.5.1
and
4.5.2
we can remove
a_{t}^{*}
in the equation (
4.5.2
) if
t=sNis
or
t=s\acute{e}t
.

Proposition 4.5.4
.

The strict Nisnevich topology, the strict étale topology, and the Kummer étale topology on
lSm/k
are compatible with log transfers.

Démonstration.

Follows from Propositions
4.2.13
and
4.5.2
.
∎

Lemma 4.5.5
.

Let
f\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
be a log modification in
lSm/k
.
For every
Y\in lSm/k
and finite log correspondence
V\in\operatorname{lCor}(Y,X)
, there exists a dividing Zariski cover
g\colon Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
and a finite log correspondence
W\in\operatorname{lCor}(Y^{\prime},X^{\prime})
fitting in a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}

in
lCor/k
.

Démonstration.

The question is Zariski local on
Y
, so we may assume that
Y
has an fs chart
P
.
Let
p\colon V^{N}\times_{X}X^{\prime}\rightarrow V^{N}
be the projection, and let
q\colon V^{N}\rightarrow Y
be the structure morphism.
By Lemma
A.11.5
, there is a subdivision of fans
M\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
such that the projection
V^{N}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{M}\rightarrow V^{N}
admits a factorization

V^{N}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{M}\rightarrow V^{N}\times_{X}X^{\prime}\rightarrow V^{N}.

Setting
Y^{\prime}:=Y\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{M}
and
V^{\prime}:=V\circ u
where
u\colon Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
denotes the projection,
the structure morphism
v\colon V^{\prime N}\rightarrow X
factors through
V^{N}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}\simeq V^{N}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{M}
, so that
v
factors through
V^{N}\times_{X}X^{\prime}
.
It follows that
v
factors through
X^{\prime}
.

Replacing
Y
by
Y^{\prime}
and
V
by
V^{\prime}
, we may assume that the structure morphism
V^{N}\rightarrow X
factors through
X^{\prime}
.
In this case, there is a commutative diagram of fs log schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}

Since
X^{\prime}
is proper over
X
, the morphism
w
is proper.
Let
\underline{W}
be the image of

\underline{V^{N}}\rightarrow\underline{Y}\times\underline{X^{\prime}},

which we consider as a closed subscheme of
\underline{Y}\times\underline{X^{\prime}}
with the reduced scheme structure.
Then
\underline{V^{N}}
is the normalization of
\underline{W}
since
\underline{V^{N}}
is the normalization of the image of

\underline{V^{N}}\rightarrow\underline{Y}\times\underline{X}.

Thus
w
gives a correspondence
W
from
Y
to
X^{\prime}
with image
V
in
\operatorname{lCor}(Y^{\prime},X)
.
∎

Lemma 4.5.6
.

For every log modification
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
in
lSm/k
the induced morphism

a_{dNis}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)\rightarrow a_{dNis}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)

of dividing Nisnevich sheaves is an isomorphism.

Démonstration.

Let
T
be an fs log scheme log smooth over
k
.
In the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{lCor}(T,Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{lCor}(T,X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Cor}(T-\partial T,Y-\partial Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Cor}(T-\partial T,X-\partial X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

the vertical morphisms are injections by Lemma
2.3.2
,
and the lower horizontal morphism is an isomorphism since
Y-\partial Y\simeq X-\partial X
.
Thus the upper horizontal morphism is an injection.
Hence
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)\rightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)
is a monomorphism,
and likewise for

a_{dNis}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)\rightarrow a_{dNis}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)

since
a_{dNis}^{*}
and
\gamma^{*}
are exact, see (
4.2.7
) and Proposition
4.2.10
.
To show that it is an epimorphism,
let
V\in\operatorname{lCor}(T,X)
be a finite log correspondence.
Due to Lemma
4.5.5
there exists a dividing Nisnevich cover
g\colon T^{\prime}\rightarrow T
and a finite log correspondence
W\in\operatorname{lCor}(T^{\prime},Y)
such that
f\circ W=V\circ g
.
This finishes the proof.
∎

Theorem 4.5.7
.

The dividing Nisnevich topology, the dividing étale topology, and the log étale topology on
lSm/k
are compatible with log transfers.

Démonstration.

We write the proof in the case of the log étale topology.
Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
be a log étale cover.
We need to show that the Čech complex

\cdots\rightarrow a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y\times_{X}Y)\rightarrow a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)\rightarrow a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\rightarrow 0

is exact, see Proposition
4.2.13
.
There exists a log modification
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
such that the pullback
f^{\prime}\colon Y\times_{X}X^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
of
f
is a Kummer étale cover owing to Proposition
4.4.1
.
Set
Y^{\prime}:=Y\times_{X}X^{\prime}
.
Then using Lemma
4.5.6
, it suffices to show that the Čech complex

\cdots\rightarrow a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y^{\prime}\times_{X^{\prime}}Y^{\prime})\rightarrow a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y^{\prime})\rightarrow a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\gamma^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X^{\prime})\rightarrow 0

is exact.
Since the sheafification functor
a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}
is exact, we are done by Proposition
4.5.2
.
∎

4.6.
Dividing log correspondences

For
X,Y\in lSm/k
, we have used
\operatorname{lCor}(X,Y)
to define dividing Nisnevich sheaves with log transfers.
Since
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)
is not a dividing Nisnevich sheaf one cannot in general identify
\operatorname{lCor}(X,Y)
with

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y),a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)).

However,
it turns out that algebraic cycles describe
a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)(X)
.
To that end, we introduce the notion of dividing log correspondences.

Definition 4.6.1
.

For
X,Y\in lSm/k
, a
dividing elementary log correspondence

Z
from
X
to
Y
is an integral closed subscheme
\underline{Z}
of
\underline{X}\times\underline{Y}

that is finite and surjective over a component of
\underline{X}
together with a log modification
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
and a morphism

u^{\prime}\colon Z^{\prime N}\rightarrow X^{\prime}\times Y

satisfying the following properties :

(i)

The image of the composition
\underline{Z^{\prime N}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\underline{u^{\prime}}}}{{\rightarrow}}\underline{X^{\prime}}\times\underline{Y}\rightarrow\underline{X}\times\underline{Y}
is
\underline{Z}
.

(ii)

\underline{Z^{\prime N}}
is the normalization of
\underline{Z}\times_{\underline{X}}\underline{X^{\prime}}
.

(iii)

The composition
Z^{\prime N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u^{\prime}}}{{\rightarrow}}X^{\prime}\times Y\rightarrow X^{\prime}
is strict.

A
dividing log correspondence
from
X
to
Y
is a formal sum
\sum n_{i}Z_{i}
of dividing elementary log correspondences
Z_{i}
from
X
to
Y
.
Let
\operatorname{lCor}_{k}^{div}(X,Y)
denote the free abelian group of dividing log correspondences from
X
to
Y
.
When no confusion seems likely to arise, we shall omit the subscript
k
and write
\operatorname{lCor}^{div}(X,Y)
.

Remark 4.6.2
.

For
X,Y\in lSm/k
, a finite log correspondence
Z\in\operatorname{lCor}(X,Y)
is determined by
Z-\partial Z\in\operatorname{lCor}(X-\partial X,Y-\partial Y)
owing to Lemma
2.3.2
.
Thus
Z
is determined by its underlying scheme
\underline{Z}
.
Hence we arrive at the following equivalent definition :
An elementary log correspondence
Z
from
X
to
Y
is an integral closed subscheme
\underline{Z}
of
\underline{X}\times\underline{Y}
that is finite and surjective over a component of

\underline{X}
together with a morphism

u\colon Z^{N}\rightarrow X\times Y

satisfying the following properties :

(i)

The image of
\underline{u}\colon\underline{Z^{N}}\rightarrow\underline{X}\times\underline{Y}
is
\underline{Z}
.

(ii)

\underline{Z^{N}}
is the normalization of
\underline{Z}
.

(iii)

The composition
Z^{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u}}{{\rightarrow}}X\times Y\rightarrow X
is strict.

Given the latter description, we can interpret any dividing log correspondence as a finite log correspondence after replacing
X
with a log modification of
X
.

Proposition 4.6.3
.

For every
X,Y\in lSm/k
there are isomorphisms

\operatorname{lCor}^{div}(X,Y)\otimes\Lambda\simeq a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)(X)\simeq a_{d\acute{e}t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)(X).

Démonstration.

With the description of elementary log correspondences in Remark
4.6.2
, this follows from Lemma
4.4.3
.
∎

Construction 4.6.4
.

For
X,Y,Z\in lSm/k
, let us construct a composition for dividing log correspondences

\circ\colon\operatorname{lCor}^{div}(X,Y)\times\operatorname{lCor}^{div}(Y,Z)\rightarrow\operatorname{lCor}^{div}(X,Z).

(4.6.1)

Owing to Proposition
4.6.3
and the Yoneda lemma we have

\operatorname{lCor}^{div}(X,Y)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{dNis}^{*}\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),a_{dNis}^{*}\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)).

(4.6.2)

We obtain (
4.6.1
) because we have compositions in
\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\mathbb{Z})
.
Moreover, the composition is associative, and for
g\in\operatorname{lCor}(X,Y)
we have

{\rm id}\circ f=f=f\circ{\rm id}.

Thus we can form a category
lCor^{div}/k
with the same objects as
lSm/k
and with morphisms given by
\operatorname{lCor}^{div}(X,Y)
.

Definition 4.6.5
.

A
presheaf of
\Lambda
-modules with dividing log transfers
is an additive presheaf of
\Lambda
-modules on the category
lCor^{div}/k
.
We let
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{dltr}}(k,\Lambda)
denote the category of presheaves of
\Lambda
-modules with dividing log transfers.

A
dividing Nisnevich sheaf of
\Lambda
-modules with dividing log transfers
is a presheaf of
\Lambda
-modules with dividing log transfers such that the restriction to
lSm/k
is a dividing Nisnevich sheaf.

We let
\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\mathrm{dltr}}(k,\Lambda)
(resp.
\mathbf{Shv}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm dltr}(k,\Lambda)
) denote the category of dividing Nisnevich (resp. dividing étale) sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules with dividing log transfers.

Proposition 4.6.6
.

There are equivalences of categories

\displaystyle\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\mathrm{dltr}}(k,\Lambda)\simeq\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda),

\displaystyle\mathbf{Shv}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm dltr}(k,\Lambda)\simeq\mathbf{Shv}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda),

\displaystyle\mathbf{Shv}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm dltr}(k,\Lambda)\simeq\mathbf{Shv}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda).

Démonstration.

We will only consider the dividing Nisnevich case since the proofs are similar.
It suffices to show that any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda)
admits a unique dividing log transfer structure.
For any
X,Y\in lSm/k
,
owing to (
4.6.2
),
we have that

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y))\simeq\ lCor^{div}(X,Y).

Thus for any
V\in\operatorname{lCor}^{div}(X,Y)
the Yoneda lemma furnishes a canonical transfer map

\begin{split}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),\mathcal{F})\\
\rightarrow&\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda)}(a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y),\mathcal{F})\simeq\mathcal{F}(Y).\end{split}

Thus there exists a dividing log transfer structure on
\mathcal{F}
.

For uniqueness,
suppose that
\mathcal{G}_{0}
and
\mathcal{G}_{1}
are two sheaves with dividing log transfers whose restrictions to
\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda)
are isomorphic to
\mathcal{F}
.
Since
\mathcal{G}_{0}
and
\mathcal{G}_{1}
agree on objects,
it suffices to show that
\mathcal{G}_{0}(V)=\mathcal{G}_{1}(V)
for every
V\in\operatorname{lCor}^{div}(X,Y)
.
Owing to Proposition
4.6.3
,
there exists a dividing Nisnevich cover
p\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
such that
V^{\prime}:=V\circ u\in\operatorname{lCor}^{div}(X^{\prime},Y)
is an element in
\operatorname{lCor}(X^{\prime},Y)
.
By the assumption that
\mathcal{F}
is the restriction of
\mathcal{G}_{0}
and
\mathcal{G}_{1}
, we have an equality of homomorphisms

\mathcal{G}_{0}(V^{\prime})=\mathcal{G}_{1}(V^{\prime})\colon\mathcal{F}(Y)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(X^{\prime}).

Since
\mathcal{F}
is a dividing Nisnevich sheaf and
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
is a dividing Nisnevich cover,
\mathcal{F}(p)\colon\mathcal{F}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(X^{\prime})
is injective.
Thus we can conclude
\mathcal{G}_{0}(V^{\prime})=\mathcal{G}_{1}(V^{\prime})
.
∎

Definition 4.6.7
.

Let
lCor_{SmlSm}/k
(resp.
lCor_{SmlSm}^{div}/k
) denote the full subcategory of
lCor/k
(resp.
lCor^{div}/k
) consisting of all objects in
SmlSm/k
.

Lemma 4.6.8
.

Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
be a log modification in
lSm/k
.
Then
f
is an isomorphism in
lCor^{div}/k
.

Démonstration.

Consider the composition

\Gamma_{f}^{t}\colon Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Gamma_{f}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Y\times X\rightarrow X\times Y,

where
\Gamma_{f}
is the graph morphism, and the second morphism switches the factors.
The induced pullback

Y\times_{X}Y\rightarrow(Y\times_{X}X)\times Y,

is isomorphic to the diagonal morphism
Y\rightarrow Y\times Y
.
Thus
\Gamma_{f}^{t}
is a dividing log correspondence from
X
to
Y
,
and it follows that
f
is invertible in
lCor^{div}/k
.
∎

Remark 4.6.9
.

Lemma
4.6.8
furnishes a class of non-finite morphisms in
lSm/k
that become invertible in
lCor^{div}/k
,
but not in
lCor/k
.
Following the approach to motives with modulus in
[
49
]
,
this suggests that we should invert every proper birational morphism
f:Y\rightarrow X
for which the naturally induced morphism

Y-\partial Y\rightarrow X-\partial X

is an isomorphism.
However,
our strategy is different from
[
49
]
.
Assuming resolution of singularities,
in Theorem
7.6.7
we show that the naturally induced morphism of log motives

M(f):M(Y)\rightarrow M(X)

is an isomorphism.

Lemma 4.6.10
.

There is an equivalence of categories

lCor_{SmlSm}^{div}/k\simeq lCor^{div}/k.

Démonstration.

Since
lCor_{SmlSm}^{div}/k
is a full subcategory of
lCor^{div}/k
, it remains to show that any object
X
of
lCor^{div}/k
is isomorphic to an object of
lCor_{SmlSm}^{div}/k
.
Owing to Proposition
A.10.2
, there is a log modification
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
such that
Y\in SmlSm/k
.
Then we are done since
f
is an isomorphism in
lCor^{div}/k
by Lemma
4.6.8
.
∎

4.7.
Sheaves with log transfers on
SmlSm/k

There are many interesting examples of Nisnevich sheaves with transfers,
e.g.,
\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
\Omega^{i}
.
If
\mathcal{F}
is a Nisnevich sheaf with transfers on
Sm/k
,
one may ask whether it extends to a dividing Nisnevich sheaf with log transfers on
lSm/k
,
such that
\mathcal{F}(X)
makes sense for every
X\in lSm/k
.
In practice, one can divide this problem into two steps.
The first step is to define
\mathcal{F}(X)
for every
X\in SmlSm/k
.
And the second step is to define
\mathcal{F}(X)
for a general
X\in lSm/k
.
In this subsection, we explain this second step.

Definition 4.7.1
.

We set

\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda):=\mathbf{Psh}(lCor_{SmlSm}/k),

\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{dltr}}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda):=\mathbf{Psh}(lCor_{SmlSm}^{div}/k).

For
t=dNis
,
d\acute{e}t
, and
l\acute{e}t
, we let
\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\rm ltr}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)
(resp.
\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\rm dltr}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)
) denote the full subcategory of
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)

(resp.
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{dltr}}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)
) consisting of
t
-sheaves on
SmlSm/k
.

Lemma 4.7.2
.

For
t=dNis
,
d\acute{e}t
, and
l\acute{e}t
, there is an equivalence of categories

\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)\simeq\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\rm log}(k,\Lambda).

Démonstration.

We will only consider the dividing Nisnevich topology since the proofs are similar.
For any
X\in lSm/k
, there exists a log modification
Y\rightarrow X
such that
Y\in SmlSm/k
owing to Proposition
A.10.2
.
We conclude using the implication (i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) in
[
2
, Théorème III.4.1]
.
∎

Let
\iota
denote the inclusion functor
lCor_{SmlSm}/k\rightarrow lCor/k
.
For
t=dNis
,
d\acute{e}t
, and
l\acute{e}t
, we have functors

\displaystyle\iota^{*}\colon\mathbf{Psh}^{\rm dltr}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Psh}^{\rm dltr}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)

(4.7.1)

\displaystyle\iota^{*}\colon\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\rm ltr}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)

(4.7.2)

mapping
\mathcal{F}
to
\mathcal{F}\circ\iota
.
As in (
4.1.1
) there are faithful functors

\gamma\colon lSm/k\rightarrow lCor^{div}/k,\;\gamma\colon SmlSm/k\rightarrow lCor_{SmlSm}^{div}/k.

These induce functors

\gamma^{*}\colon\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{dltr}}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda),\;\gamma^{*}\colon\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{dltr}}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Psh}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)

mapping
\mathcal{F}
to
\mathcal{F}\circ\gamma
.

Lemma 4.7.3
.

For
t=dNis
,
d\acute{e}t
, and
l\acute{e}t
, there is an equivalence of categories

\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\rm dltr}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)\simeq\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\rm dltr}(k,\Lambda).

Démonstration.

We will only consider the dividing Nisnevich topology since the proofs are similar.
There is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{dltr}}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{dltr}}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}

The restriction functor
\iota^{*}
is an equivalence by Lemma
4.6.10
.
Hence it suffices to show that for every
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{dltr}}(k,\Lambda)
,
\gamma^{*}(\mathcal{F})
is a dividing Nisnevich sheaf if and only if its image in
\mathbf{Psh}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)
is a dividing Nisnevich sheaf.
This follows from Lemma
4.7.2
.
∎

Proposition 4.7.4
.

For
t=dNis
,
d\acute{e}t
, and
l\acute{e}t
, there is an equivalence of categories

\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\rm dltr}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)\simeq\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\rm ltr}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda).

Démonstration.

Parallel to the proof of Proposition
4.6.6
.
∎

Proposition 4.7.5
.

For
t=dNis
,
d\acute{e}t
, and
l\acute{e}t
, the functor in (
4.7.2
) is an equivalence.

Démonstration.

Follows from Propositions
4.6.6
,
4.7.4
, and Lemma
4.7.3
.
∎

Thus the functor in (
4.7.2
) admits a left adjoint

\iota_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\rm ltr}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda).

Lemma 4.7.6
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be an object of
\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\rm ltr}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)
, where
t
is one of
dNis
,
d\acute{e}t
, and
l\acute{e}t
.
For every
X\in lSm/k
we have that

\iota_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\rightarrow X}\mathcal{F}(Y),

where the colimit runs over log modifications
Y\rightarrow X
such that
Y\in SmlSm/k
.

Démonstration.

We only discuss the dividing Nisnvich topology since the proofs are parallel.
Since
\iota^{*}
is an equivalence by Proposition
4.7.5
, we have
\iota^{*}\iota_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}\simeq\mathcal{F}
, so that

\iota_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}(Y)\simeq\mathcal{F}(Y)

for every
Y\in SmlSm/k
.
Recall that
X_{div}^{Sm}
is the full subcategory of
X_{div}
consisting of
Y\rightarrow X
such that
Y\in SmlSm/k
.
According to Proposition
A.11.15
,
X_{div}^{Sm}
is cofinal in
X_{div}
,
and hence

\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}}\iota_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}(Y)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\iota_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}(Y)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\mathcal{F}(Y).

(4.7.3)

Since
\iota_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}(X)
is a dividing Nisnevich sheaf,
we deduce

\iota_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}}\iota_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}(Y)

(4.7.4)

Combining (
4.7.3
) and (
4.7.4
) gives the assertion.
∎

5.
Construction of triangulated categories of logarithmic motives

This section is dedicated to constructing and studying some basic properties of our categories of log motivic sheaves.
First, we explain how the dividing Nisnevich cohomology groups are related to the strict Nisnevich cohomology groups.
Next, we construct the triangulated categories
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
and
{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
of effective log motives,
with and without transfers,
in the Nisnevich and étale topologies on log schemes over
k
.
There are two equivalent ways of constructing
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
,
see Section
5.4
.
One way is to use sheaves on
lSm/k
, and the other way is via sheaves on
SmlSm/k
.
The advantage of having the latter category at our disposal is that it allows us to construct motivic sheaves that are, a priori,
only defined and functorial on log schemes for which the underlying scheme is smooth over
k
and boundary a normal crossing divisor.
In Section
9
we use this trick to prove the representability of Hodge cohomology and cyclic homology.

5.1.
Dividing Nisnevich cohomology groups

A fundamental invariant of the dividing Nisnevich topology is the corresponding sheaf cohomology theory.
In what follows, we shall express the dividing Nisnevich cohomology groups in terms of strict Nisnevich cohomology groups by promoting Subsection
4.4
to cohomology.
We also discuss the étale version of this result.
As an application, we discuss finite cohomological dimension property for our various topologies.

Lemma 5.1.1
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a strict Nisnevich (resp. strict étale, resp. Kummer étale) sheaf on
lSm/k
.
If
\mathcal{F}
is flabby, then
a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F}
(resp.
a_{d\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F}
, resp.
a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F}
) is flabby.

Démonstration.

We focus on the strict Nisnevich case since the proofs are similar.
Owing to the implication
(b)\Rightarrow(a)
in
[
66
, Proposition III.2.12]
it suffices to show that for every dividing Nisnevich cover
p:U\rightarrow X
in
lSm/k
and integer
i>0
,
the corresponding Čech cohomology group vanishes

H^{i}(a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{U}))=0.

Here
\mathcal{U}
is the Čech nerve associated with
U\rightarrow X
.

According to Proposition
4.4.1
, there exists a log modification
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
such that the projection
U^{\prime}:=U\times_{X}X^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
is a strict Nisnevich cover.
Since
a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F}
is a complex of dividing Nisnevich sheaves, there is an isomorphism

a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{U})\simeq a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{U}\times_{X}X^{\prime}).

Hence we can replace
U\rightarrow X
with
U^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
, so we reduce to the case when
p
is a strict Nisnevich cover.

By Lemma
4.4.3
the complex
a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{U})
is isomorphic to

\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y_{0}\in X_{div}}\mathcal{F}(Y_{0})\rightarrow\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y_{1}\in U_{div}}\mathcal{F}(Y_{1})\rightarrow\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y_{2}\in(U\times_{X}U)_{div}}\mathcal{F}(Y_{2})\rightarrow\cdots.

Applying Corollary
A.14.15
, we see that the latter complex is isomorphic to

\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}}\mathcal{F}(Y)\rightarrow\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}}\mathcal{F}(Y\times_{X}U)\rightarrow\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}}\mathcal{F}(Y\times_{X}U\times_{X}U)\rightarrow\cdots.

This is a filtered colimit according to Proposition
A.11.14
.
Since every filtered colimit in the category of
\Lambda
-modules is exact, it remains to check that
H^{i}
of the complex

\mathcal{F}(Y)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(Y\times_{X}U)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(Y\times_{X}U\times_{X}U)\rightarrow\cdots

is trivial for every
Y\in X_{div}
and
i>0
.
To conclude we use the implication
(a)\Rightarrow(b)
in
[
66
, Proposition III.2.12]
and the assumption that
\mathcal{F}
is flabby.
∎

Theorem 5.1.2
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a bounded below complex of strict Nisnevich sheaves on
lSm/k
.
Then for every
X\in lSm/k
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{dNis}^{i}(X,a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}}\mathbf{H}_{sNis}^{i}(Y,\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\text{(resp.\ }\mathbf{H}_{d\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,a_{d\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}}\mathbf{H}_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(Y,\mathcal{F}),

\displaystyle\text{resp.\ }\mathbf{H}_{l\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}}\mathbf{H}_{k\acute{e}t}^{i}(Y,\mathcal{F})\text{)}.

Démonstration.

We only consider the strict Nisnevich case since the proofs are similar.
We can replace
\mathcal{F}
by its injective resolution because the sheafification functor
a_{dNis}^{*}
is exact.
In particular,
\mathcal{F}^{i}
is flabby for every integer
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Owing to Lemma
5.1.1
we see that
a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F}^{i}
is flabby.
It follows that

\mathbf{H}_{dNis}^{i}(X,a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F})\simeq H^{i}(a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F}(X)).

Due to Lemma
4.4.3
there is an isomorphism

a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}}\mathcal{F}(Y).

Since this colimit is filtered by Proposition
A.11.14
and every filtered colimit in the category of
\Lambda
-modules is exact,
we deduce that

\mathbf{H}_{dNis}^{i}(X,a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F})\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}}H^{i}(\mathcal{F}(Y)).

To conclude the proof, we observe that

\mathbf{H}_{sNis}^{i}(Y,\mathcal{F})\simeq H^{i}(\mathcal{F}(Y)).

∎

Remark 5.1.3
.

Suppose
\mathcal{F}
is a torsion sheaf on the small log étale site
X_{l\acute{e}t}
.
Then for every fs log scheme
X
with an fs chart,
the isomorphism

H_{l\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})\simeq H_{k\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})

is proven in
[
70
, Proposition 5.4(2)]
.

Corollary 5.1.4
.

The category
lSm/k
has finite
sNis
and
dNis
-cohomological dimension for
\Lambda
-linear coefficients.

Démonstration.

For every
X\in lSm/k
and
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{Shv}_{sNis}^{\rm log}(k,\Lambda)
,
if
i>\dim X
, then due to
[
64
, Example 12.2]
we have the vanishing

H_{sNis}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})=H_{Nis}^{i}(\underline{X},\mathcal{F})=0.

If
Y
is a log modification over
X
, then
\dim Y=\dim X
by Lemma
3.3.24
.
Thus we have the vanishing

H_{sNis}^{i}(Y,\mathcal{F})=0.

Theorem
5.1.2
finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.1.5
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme.
Suppose
\mathcal{F}
is a sheaf of
\mathbb{Q}
-modules on the small strict étale site
X_{s\acute{e}t}
.
Then, for every integer
i\geq 0
, there is an isomorphism of cohomology groups

H_{sNis}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})\simeq H_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F}).

Démonstration.

Equivalently, we need to show there is an isomorphism

H_{Nis}^{i}(\underline{X},\mathcal{F})\simeq H_{\acute{e}t}^{i}(\underline{X},\mathcal{F}).

This is the content of
[
64
, Proposition 14.23]
.
∎

Lemma 5.1.6
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme.
Suppose
\mathcal{F}
is a sheaf of
\mathbb{Q}
-modules on the small Kummer étale site
X_{k\acute{e}t}
.
Then, for every integer
i\geq 0
, there is an isomorphism of cohomology groups

H_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})\simeq H_{k\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F}).

Démonstration.

Let
\varepsilon_{*}\colon\mathbf{Shv}(X_{k\acute{e}t})\rightarrow\mathbf{Shv}(X_{s\acute{e}t})
be the functor sending
\mathcal{F}
to
\mathcal{F}|_{X_{s\acute{e}t}}
.
We need to show that
R^{i}\varepsilon_{*}\mathcal{F}=0
for every integer
i>0
.
Since
R^{i}\varepsilon_{*}\mathcal{F}
is the strict étale sheaf associated with the presheaf

(Y\in X_{s\acute{e}t})\mapsto H_{k\acute{e}t}^{i}(Y,\mathcal{F}),

it suffices to show the vanishing

H_{k\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})=0

for every
i>0
whenever
\underline{X}
is strictly local.
According to
[
69
, Proposition 4.1]
,
see also
[
70
, Proposition 1.2]
,
the group
H_{k\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})
is a torsion abelian group by
[
95
, Corollary 6.11.4]
.
Since
\mathcal{F}
is a sheaf of
\mathbb{Q}
-modules, this implies the desired vanishing.
∎

Corollary 5.1.7
.

Let
t
be one of the following topologies on
lSm/k
:

s\acute{e}t,\;d\acute{e}t,\;k\acute{e}t,\text{ and }l\acute{e}t.

If
k
has finite
\acute{e}t
-cohomological dimension for
\Lambda
-linear coefficients, then
lSm/k
has finite
t
-cohomological dimension for
\Lambda
-linear coeffieicnts.

Démonstration.

For any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\rm log}(k,\Lambda)
, the kernel and cokernel of the natural morphism

\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathbb{Q}

are torsion sheaves.
Hence we only need to deal with the two separate cases :
\Lambda
is a torsion ring, and
\mathbb{Q}\subset\Lambda
.

If
\Lambda
is a torsion ring, then
lSm/k
has finite
s\acute{e}t
and
k\acute{e}t
-cohomological dimension for
\Lambda
-linear coefficients due to
[
2
, Corollaries X.4.2, X.5.3]
and
[
70
, Theorem 7.2]
.
To show that
lSm/k
has finite
d\acute{e}t
and
l\acute{e}t
-cohomological dimension for
\Lambda
-linear coefficients, apply Lemma
3.3.24
and Theorem
5.1.2
.

If
\mathbb{Q}\subset\Lambda
, then Lemmas
5.1.5
and
5.1.6
show that
lSm/k
has finite
s\acute{e}t
and
k\acute{e}t
-cohomological dimension for
\Lambda
-linear coefficients since
lSm/k
has finite
sNis
-cohomological dimension due to Corollary
5.1.4
.
Then apply Lemma
3.3.24
and Theorem
5.1.2
again to finish the proof.
∎

Theorem 5.1.8
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a bounded below complex of strict Nisnevich (resp. strict étale, resp. Kummer étale) sheaves on
SmlSm/k
.
Then for every
X\in SmlSm/k
and integer
i\in\mathbb{Z}
there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{dNis}^{i}(X,a_{dNis}^{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\mathbf{H}_{sNis}^{i}(Y,\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\text{(resp.\ }\mathbf{H}_{d\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,a_{d\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\mathbf{H}_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(Y,\mathcal{F}),

\displaystyle\text{resp.\ }\mathbf{H}_{l\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\mathbf{H}_{k\acute{e}t}^{i}(Y,\mathcal{F})\text{)}.

The same holds if we replace
X_{div}^{Sm}
by
X_{divsc}^{Sm}
.

Démonstration.

The proof is parallel to that of Theorem
5.1.2
if we use Lemma
4.4.2
and Proposition
A.11.16
as replacements for Lemma
4.4.3
and Proposition
A.11.14
.
To show the claim for
X_{divsc}^{Sm}
use Corollary
4.4.5
and Lemma
4.4.6
instead.
∎

5.2.
Effective log motives

Voevodsky’s category of effective motives
{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
(resp. effective motives without transfers
{\bf DA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
) is the homotopy category with respect to the
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local descent model structure on

\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{Nis}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda))
(resp.
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{Nis}(k,\Lambda))
).
The motive
M(X)
of
X\in Sm/k
is defined as the object
\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)
(resp.
\Lambda(X)
) in
{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
(resp.
{\bf DA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
).
The category
{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
was initially constructed for bounded above complexes, see e.g.,
[
64
, Lecture 14]
.
We refer to
[
21
, Definition 11.1.1]
for a generalization to unbounded complexes.

By abuse of notation, we let
\overline{\Box}
be shorthand for the class of projections
X\times\overline{\Box}\rightarrow X
,
where
X\in lSm/k
.
We also consider
\overline{\Box}
