as the corresponding classes of morphisms of representable objects in
\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda)
or
\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda)
.

Definition 5.2.1
.

The derived category of effective log motives

{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)

is the homotopy category of
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda))
with respect to the
\overline{\Box}
-local descent model structure.
The motive of
X\in lSm/k
is the object

M(X):=a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\in{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).

Similarly,
by abuse of notation,
we write
{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
for the homotopy category of
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))
with respect to the
\overline{\Box}
-local descent model structure and
M(X)
for
a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda(X)
.

Recall that we set
\overline{\Box}:=(\mathbb{P}^{1},\infty)
.
By Proposition
B.1.8
,
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
and
{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
are symmetric monoidal triangulated categories such that for
X,Y\in lSm/k
, we have

M(X)\otimes M(Y)=M(X\times Y).

For any dividing Nisnevich cover
Y\rightarrow X
in
lSm/k
, we note that

Y-\partial Y\rightarrow X-\partial X

is a Nisnevich cover in
Sm/k
.
Moreover,
we have that

\omega(\overline{\Box})=\mathbb{A}^{1}.

Thus by (
4.3.3
) and (
4.3.6
), there are naturally induced adjunctions

L\gamma_{\sharp}:{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda):R\gamma^{*},

and

\omega_{\sharp}:{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda):R\omega^{*}.

(5.2.1)

Similarly, we have the naturally induced adjunctions

L\gamma_{\sharp}:{\bf DA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda):R\gamma^{*},

and

\omega_{\sharp}:{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows{\bf DA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda):R\omega^{*}.

Further, there is a diagram of adjunct functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf DA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\gamma_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\gamma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\gamma_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\gamma^{*}}

where
L\gamma_{\sharp}
commutes with
\omega_{\sharp}
.

Definition 5.2.2
.

A complex of dividing Nisnevich sheaves
\mathcal{F}
is
strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant
if for every fs log scheme
X
log smooth over
k
and integer
i\in\mathbb{Z}
,
the projection
X\times\overline{\Box}\rightarrow X
induces an isomorphism on sheaf cohomology groups

\mathbf{H}_{dNis}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\mathbf{H}_{dNis}^{i}(X\times\overline{\Box},\mathcal{F}).

By definition of
\overline{\Box}
-local model structure, a complex of dividing Nisnevich sheaves
\mathcal{F}
is fibrant for the
\overline{\Box}
-local descent model structure if and only if
\mathcal{F}
is fibrant for the local model structure and
\mathcal{F}
is strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant.

Proposition 5.2.3
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant complex of dividing Nisnevich sheaves with log transfers.
Then,
for every fs log scheme
X
log smooth over
k
and an integer

i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\mathcal{F}[i])\simeq\mathbf{H}_{dNis}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F}).

Similar statement holds for strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant complex of dividing Nisnevich sheaves
\mathcal{F}
and
{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.

Démonstration.

We focus on
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
since the proofs are similar.
We may assume that
\mathcal{F}
is a fibrant object in
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda))
for the descent model structure given in §
4.3
.
By Proposition
B.1.8
(1),
\mathcal{F}
is a fibrant object in
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda))
with respect to the
\overline{\Box}
-local descent model structure.
Thus we have

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\mathcal{F}[i])\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda))}(a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X),\mathcal{F}[i]).

Proposition
4.3.2
finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.2.4
.

Let
\{\mathcal{F}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a set of strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant complexes of dividing Nisnevich sheaves.
Then
\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{F}_{i}
is strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant too.
The same holds for strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant complexes of dividing étale or log étale sheaves if
k
has finite étale cohomological dimension for
\Lambda
-linear coefficients.

Démonstration.

We first treat the dividing Nisnevich case.
Due to Corollary
5.1.4
and Proposition
3.2.7
for every
X\in lSm/k
we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{dNis}^{i}(X,\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{F})\simeq\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathbf{H}_{dNis}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{dNis}^{i}(X\times\overline{\Box},\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{F})\simeq\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathbf{H}_{dNis}^{i}(X\times\overline{\Box},\mathcal{F}).

Combining these isomorphisms finishes the proof.
For the dividing étale and log étale cases, apply Corollary
5.1.7
instead.
∎

Proposition 5.2.5
.

The effective log motives
M(X)[n]
indexed by
X\in lSm/k
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
form a set of compact generators for the categories
{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
and
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.

Démonstration.

We focus on
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
since the proofs are similar.
Let
\{\mathcal{F}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a set of strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant dividing Nisnevich complexes of sheaves with log transfers.
Then
\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{F}_{i}
is strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant too by Lemma
5.2.4
.
Owing to Proposition
5.2.3
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{F}_{i}[n])\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}(a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda(X),\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{F}_{i}[n]),

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i\in I}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\mathcal{F}_{i}[n])\simeq\bigoplus_{i\in I}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}(a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda(X),\mathcal{F}_{i}[n]).

Proposition
3.2.7
and Corollary
5.1.4
finish the proof.
∎

Remark 5.2.6
.

Similarly, the objects
M(X)[n]
, where
X\in Sm/k
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, are compact in
{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
(resp.
{\bf DA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
).
The same objects generate
{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
(resp.
{\bf DA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
) by the above or
[
21
, Theorem 11.1.13]
(resp.
[
21
, Proposition 5.2.38]
).

Definition 5.2.7
.

If
f:Y\rightarrow X
is a morphism in
lSm/k
,
let

M(Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\rightarrow}}X)

be the cone in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
associated with the complex

a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)\rightarrow a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)

in
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda))
.
We use the same notation for the object in
{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
associated with the complex
a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda(Y)\rightarrow a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda(X)
in
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))
.

For a simplicial object
\mathscr{X}
in
lSm/k
, let
M(\mathscr{X})
denote the object in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
associated with
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathscr{X})
in
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda))

We use the same notation for the object in
{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
associated with
\Lambda(\mathscr{X})
in
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))
.

Definition 5.2.8
.

We denote by

{\bf logDM}_{\rm prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)

the smallest triangulated subcategory of
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
that is closed under small sums and shifts,
and contains
M(X)
,
where
X\in lSm/k
and its underlying scheme
\underline{X}
is proper over
\operatorname{Spec}(k)
.

Remark 5.2.9
.

We are interested in
{\bf logDM}_{\rm prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
because for a proper log smooth
X
,
the log motive
M(X)
is practically an object in
{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
Indeed, if
k
admits resolution of singularities,
Theorem
8.2.17
shows there is an equivalence of triangulated categories

{\bf logDM}_{\rm prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\simeq{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).

Moreover,
we note that the corresponding statement is false in the étale topology ; see Remark
5.3.6
.

Proposition 5.2.10
.

(1)

For every strict Nisnevich distinguished square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}

in
lSm/k
, there is a naturally induced isomorphism

M(Y^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime})\rightarrow M(Y\rightarrow X)

in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
The same holds in
{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.

(2)

For every log modification
f:Y\rightarrow X
, there is a naturally induced isomorphism

M(Y)\rightarrow M(X)

in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
The same holds true in
{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.

Démonstration.

Let us treat first the case of
{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
To show (1), it suffices to show the homomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y\rightarrow X),\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}),\mathcal{F})

is an isomorphism for any strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant complex of dividing Nisnevich sheaf
\mathcal{F}
.
For this it suffices to show there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}(a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda(Y\rightarrow X),\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{t}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda))}(a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda(Y^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}),\mathcal{F}).

(5.2.2)

This follows from the exactness of (
4.3.10
).
Part (2) follows similarly, using a dividing distinguished square instead of a strict Nisnevich distinguished square.

To show (1) in the case of
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
, it suffices to show the homomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y\rightarrow X),\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}),\mathcal{F})

is an isomorphism for any strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant complex of dividing Nisnevich sheaf
\mathcal{F}
with log transfers.
This follows from Proposition
5.2.3
since (
5.2.2
) is an isomorphism.
We omit the proof of (2) because it is similar to (1).
∎

Example 5.2.11
.

The commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{P}^{1},0)\simeq\overline{\Box}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}}

(5.2.3)

is a strict Nisnevich distinguished square, so that the induced morphism

M(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1})\rightarrow M(\overline{\Box}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1})\simeq\Lambda(1)[2]

is an isomorphism in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
This is analogous to the isomorphism

M(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\rightarrow M(\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbb{A}^{1})\simeq\Lambda(1)[2]

in
{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
and
{\bf DA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
Indeed, applying
\omega
to the square (
5.2.3
) we retain the Nisnevich distinguished square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}-\{0\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}}

for the standard covering of the projective line.
However, in the log situation,
we note that
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}})
is not a subsheaf of
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})
.

Definition 5.2.12
.

Recall that
div
is the class of morphisms in
lSm/k
consisting of log modifications
Y\rightarrow X
in
lSm/k
.
We denote by
(\overline{\Box},div)
the class of morphisms in
lSm/k
consisting of all the projections
X\times\overline{\Box}\rightarrow X
, where
X\in lSm/k
, and the log modifications
Y\rightarrow X
in
lSm/k
.

Example 5.2.13
.

A useful property of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant presheaf
\mathcal{F}
with transfers is that for any Zariski cover
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
of an open subscheme
U
of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
,
there is a split exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(U_{1})\oplus\mathcal{F}(U_{2})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(U_{1}\cap U_{2})\rightarrow 0,

(5.2.4)

see
[
64
, Lemma 22.4]
.
This is no longer true for
\overline{\Box}
-invariant presheaves with log transfers.
In what follows, we check that
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})
provides a counterexample.

For
X\in lSm/k
, we have that

\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})(X)=\operatorname{Cor}(\underline{X},\mathbb{A}^{1}).

Let
V
be an elementary correspondence from
\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
to
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
For any point
x
of
\underline{X}
, consider the restriction
V_{x}
of
V
to
\{x\}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Since
V_{x}
is finite over
\{x\}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
V_{x}
is proper over
k
.
Thus the image of
V^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{A}^{1}
is again proper, so the image should be a union of points.
Let
y
be a rational point of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
let
V^{\prime}
(resp.
V_{x}^{\prime}
) be the restriction of
V
to
\underline{X}\times\{y\}
(resp.
\{x\}\times\{y\}
) and let

p:\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow\underline{X}
and
p_{x}:\{x\}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow\{x\}
be the projections.
From the above we have
p_{x}^{*}V_{x}^{\prime}=V_{x}
and hence
p^{*}V^{\prime}=V
.
Thus the morphism

p^{*}:\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})(X)\rightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})(X\times\mathbb{P}^{1})

is surjective.
Since
p
has a section, it follows that
p^{*}
is injective.
So far we have observed that
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})
is
\overline{\Box}
-invariant.

Next, we turn to the split exactness of (
5.2.4
).
Set
U:=\mathbb{A}^{1}=\operatorname{Spec}(k[x])
,
U_{1}:=\mathbb{A}^{1}-\{0\}
, and
U_{2}:=\mathbb{A}^{1}-\{1\}
.
Then there is an element

[1/x+1/(x-1)]\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})(U_{1}\cap U_{2}),

where for
f:U_{1}\cap U_{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
, we let
[f]
denotes the associated correspondence.
On the other hand,
the closure of
[1/x+1/(x-1)]
in
U_{j}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
is not surjective over
U_{j}
, i.e., it does not define an elementary log correspondence in
\operatorname{lCor}(U_{j},\mathbb{A}^{1})
for
j=1,2
.
It follows that the homomorphism

\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(U_{1})\oplus\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(U_{2})\rightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(U_{1}\cap U_{2})

is not even surjective.

Remark 5.2.14
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be an
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant Nisnevich sheaf
\mathcal{F}
with transfers.
The spilt exactness of (
5.2.4
) is used to prove that also the presheaf
H_{Nis}^{n}(-,\mathcal{F})
is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant,
see e.g.,
[
64
, Theorem 24.1]
.
Due to Example
5.2.13
,
it is unclear whether for any
\overline{\Box}
-invariant strict Nisnevich sheaf
\mathcal{F}
with transfers, the presheaf
H_{sNis}^{n}(-,\mathcal{F})
is
\overline{\Box}
-invariant.

5.3.
Effective étale log motives

In this subsection we define the étale versions of
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
and
{\bf logDA}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.

Definition 5.3.1
.

The derived category of effective dividing étale log motives

{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)

is the homotopy category of

\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda))

with respect to the
\overline{\Box}
-local descent model structure.
The motive of
X\in lSm/k
is the object

M(X):=a_{d\acute{e}t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\in{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).

Similarly,
we write
{\bf logDA}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
for the homotopy category of
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm log}(k,\Lambda))
with respect to the
\overline{\Box}
-local descent model structure and
M(X)
for
a_{d\acute{e}t}^{*}\Lambda(X)
.

The derived category of effective log étale log motives

{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)

is the homotopy category of
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda))
with respect to the
\overline{\Box}
-local descent model structure.
The effective log étale motive of
X\in lSm/k
is the object

M(X):=a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\in{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).

Similarly,
we write
{\bf logDA}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
for the homotopy category of
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm log}(k,\Lambda))
with respect to the
\overline{\Box}
-local descent model structure and
M(X)
for
a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\Lambda(X)
.

By Proposition
B.1.8
,
{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
,
{\bf logDA}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
,
{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
, and
{\bf logDA}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
are symmetric monoidal triangulated categories such that for all
X,Y\in lSm/k
,

M(X)\otimes M(Y)=M(X\times Y).

For the descent model structures,
there are Quillen adjunctions

a_{d\acute{e}t}^{*}:\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda)):a_{d\acute{e}t*}

and

a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}:\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda)):a_{l\acute{e}t*}.

Owing to
[
44
, Theorem 3.3.20(1)]
, this is again a Quillen adjunction for the
\overline{\Box}
-local model structures.
By passing to the homotopy categories, we obtain the adjunctions

La_{d\acute{e}t}^{*}:{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda):Ra_{d\acute{e}t*}

and

La_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}:{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda):Ra_{l\acute{e}t*}.

Definition 5.3.2
.

Let
Y\rightarrow X
be a morphism in
lSm/k
, and let
\mathscr{X}
be a simplicial object of
lSm/k
.
As in Definition
5.2.7
, we associate objects

M(Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\rightarrow}}X)\text{ and }M(\mathscr{X})

in
{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
,
{\bf logDA}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
,
{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
, and
{\bf logDA}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.

Definition 5.3.3
.

A complex of dividing étale sheaves
\mathcal{F}
is
strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant
if for every fs log scheme
X
log smooth over
k
and integer
i\in\mathbb{Z}
,
the projection
X\times\overline{\Box}\rightarrow X
induces an isomorphism on sheaf cohomology groups

\mathbf{H}_{d\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\mathbf{H}_{d\acute{e}t}^{i}(X\times\overline{\Box},\mathcal{F}).

Similarly, a complex of log étale sheaves
\mathcal{F}
is
strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant
if for every fs log scheme
X
log smooth over
k
and integer
i\in\mathbb{Z}
,
the projection
X\times\overline{\Box}\rightarrow X
induces an isomorphism on sheaf cohomology groups

\mathbf{H}_{l\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\mathbf{H}_{l\acute{e}t}^{i}(X\times\overline{\Box},\mathcal{F}).

Proposition 5.3.4
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant complex of dividing étale (resp. log étale) sheaves with log transfers.
Then,
for every fs log scheme
X
log smooth over
k
and an integer

i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\mathcal{F})\simeq\mathbf{H}_{d\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})

\text{(resp.\ }\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\mathcal{F})\simeq\mathbf{H}_{l\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})\text{)}.

Démonstration.

The proof is parallel to that of Proposition
5.2.3
.
∎

Definition 5.3.5
.

We denote by

{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t,prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\;\text{(resp.\ }{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t,prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\text{)}

the smallest triangulated subcategory of
{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
(resp.
{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
) that is closed under small sums and shifts,
and contains
M(X)
, where
X\in lSm/k
and its underlying scheme is proper over
\operatorname{Spec}(k)
.

Remark 5.3.6
.

Suppose
k
has positive characteristic
p>0
.
In contrast to Theorem
8.2.17
, we do
not
have an equivalence of triangulated categories between

{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t,prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\mathbb{Z})\text{ and }{\bf DM}_{\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\mathbb{Z}).

To wit, note that
\mathbb{Z}/p\simeq 0
in
{\bf DM}_{\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\mathbb{Z})
by
[
64
, Example 9.16]
.
Notice that since
\mathbb{Z}\simeq M(k)\in{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t,prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\mathbb{Z})
, we have
\mathbb{Z}/p\in{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t,prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\mathbb{Z})
.
In Example
9.7.3
we will see that
\mathbb{Z}/p
is nontrivial in
{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t,prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\mathbb{Z})
.

Proposition 5.3.7
.

Suppose that
k
has finite
\acute{e}t
-cohomological dimension for
\Lambda
-linear coefficients.
Then the effective log motives
M(X)[n]
indexed by
X\in lSm/k
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
form a set of compact generators for the categories

{\bf logDA}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda),\;{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda),\;{\bf logDA}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda),\text{ and }{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).

Démonstration.

Argue as in Proposition
5.2.5
by appealing to Corollary
5.1.7
.
∎

5.4.
Construction of motives using
SmlSm/k

We may use the results of Section
4.7
to construct categories of effective log motives for objects in
SmlSm/k
.

Definition 5.4.1
.

Let

{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)

be the homotopy category of

\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda))

with respect to the
\overline{\Box}
-local descent model structure.

Similarly, we let

{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)

be the homotopy category of

\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm ltr}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda))

with respect to the
\overline{\Box}
-local descent model structure.

Proposition 5.4.2
.

There are naturally induced equivalences of triangulated categories

\displaystyle{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)

\displaystyle\simeq{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda),

\displaystyle{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)

\displaystyle\simeq{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda),

\displaystyle{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)

\displaystyle\simeq{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).

Démonstration.

We will only consider the dividing Nisnevich topology since the proofs are similar.
Owing to Proposition
4.7.5
there is an equivalence of categories

\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)\simeq\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda).

Let us consider the descent model structures on the categories

\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda))

and

\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda)).

Since the descent model structures are invariant under equivalences of categories,
there is a Quillen equivalence for the descent model structures

\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda)).

Owing to
[
44
, Theorem 3.3.20(1)]
this becomes a Quillen equivalence if we impose the
\overline{\Box}
-local descent model structure.
∎

Construction 5.4.3
.

Suppose
\mathcal{F}
is a complex of dividing Nisnevich sheaves with log transfers on
SmlSm/k
.
Recall that
X_{div}^{Sm}
denotes the category of log modifications
Y\rightarrow X
,
where
Y\in SmlSm/k
.
Owing to Lemma
4.7.6
, we can naturally extend
\mathcal{F}
to a complex
i_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}
on
lSm/k
via the formula

\iota_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}(X):=\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in X_{div}^{Sm}}\mathcal{F}(Y).

By Lemma
4.7.2
there is an equivalence of categories

\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}(SmlSm/k,\Lambda)\simeq\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\mathrm{log}}(k,\Lambda).

This implies that for every
X\in SmlSm/k
and integer
i\in\mathbb{Z}
we have an isomorphism of cohomology groups

H_{dNis}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})\simeq H_{dNis}^{i}(X,\iota_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}).

(5.4.1)

If
\mathcal{F}
is strict
\overline{\Box}
-invariant,
i.e.,
for every
X\in SmlSm/k

H_{dNis}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})\simeq H_{dNis}^{i}(X\times\overline{\Box},\mathcal{F}),

(
5.4.1
) shows
\iota_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}
is strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant too.
Owing to Proposition
5.2.3
, for every
X\in SmlSm/k
and integer
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we obtain an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\iota_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}[i])\simeq H_{dNis}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F}).

(5.4.2)

If
\mathcal{F}
is a complex of dividing étale (resp. log étale) sheaves with log transfers on
SmlSm/k
, then we can argue similarly to conclude the isomorphisms

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\iota_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}[i])\simeq H_{d\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})

(5.4.3)

and

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\iota_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}[i])\simeq H_{l\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F}).

(5.4.4)

6.
Calculus of fractions and homotopy theory of presheaves

Throughout this section,
T
is a small category with finite limits.
Let
\operatorname{pt}
denote the final object in
T
.
Suppose
\mathcal{A}
is a set of morphisms in
T
that admits a calculus of right fractions.
We let
I
be an object of
T
admitting morphisms

i_{0},i_{1}:{\rm pt}\rightarrow I,\;\;p:I\rightarrow{\rm pt}

in
T
and a morphism

\mu:I\times I\rightarrow I

in
T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]
satisfying

\mu(i_{0}\times{\rm id})=\mu({\rm id}\times i_{0})=i_{0}p,

\mu(i_{1}\times{\rm id})=\mu({\rm id}\times i_{1})={\rm id}.

Moreover,
we assume

i_{0}\amalg i_{1}:{\rm pt}\amalg{\rm pt}\rightarrow I

is a monomorphism.
Note that
I
is an interval of
T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]
in the sense of
[
68
, §2.3]
.

Recall that
\mathcal{A}
admits a
calculus of right fractions
if the following conditions holds
[
38
, dual of I.2.2]
.

(i)

The set
\mathcal{A}
contains the isomorphisms and is closed under composition.

(ii)

(Right Ore condition) For any diagram
Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\rightarrow}}X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\leftarrow}}X^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{C}
with
f\in\mathcal{A}
, there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

in
\mathcal{C}
such that
f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
.

(iii)

(Right cancellability condition) For any diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

in
\mathcal{C}
with
u\in\mathcal{A}
and
u\circ f=u\circ g
, there is a morphism
v:W\rightarrow Z
in
\mathcal{A}
such that
f\circ v=g\circ v
.

In
[
68
, §2.3]
, Morel-Voevodsky constructs the singular functor
{\rm Sing}
for an interval
I
of
T
.
Since our assumption is that
I
is an interval of
T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]
,
instead of
T
,
we cannot apply the same arguments verbatim.
For example,
in the proof of
[
68
, Corollary 3.5, p. 89]
it is essential that
\mu\in T
when dealing with the multiplication map
\mu:I\times I\rightarrow I
.
In this section,
we construct a singular functor
{\rm Sing}
for
\mu
in
T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]
.
We argue that there is another way of constructing
{\rm Sing}
in
[
6
]
without assuming the existence of
\mu
.
The style of this construction is different from that in
[
68
, §2.3]
.
See
[
9
, Section 4.5]
for a way of constructing the Sing functor obtained by
forming a weak interval object
[
9
, Definition 2.19]
in the category of simplicial presheaves on
T
.

6.1.
Properties of localization functors

Definition 6.1.1
.

Let
s\mathbf{Psh}(T)
denote the category of simplicial presheaves on
T
.
A morphism
f:F\rightarrow G
of simplicial presheaves is called

(i)

a
pointwise weak equivalence
if
F(X)\rightarrow G(X)
is a weak equivalence of simplicial sets for any object
X
of
T
.

(ii)

an
injective cofibration
if
F(X)\rightarrow G(X)
is a cofibration of simplicial sets for any object
X
of
T
.

(iii)

an
injective fibration
if it has the right lifting property with respect to injective cofibrations that are pointwise weak equivalences.

(iv)

a
projective fibration
if
F(X)\rightarrow G(X)
is a fibration of simplicial sets for any object
X
of
T
.

(v)

a
projective cofibration
if it has the left lifting property with respect to projective fibrations that are pointwise weak equivalences.

Recall that the model structure on
s\mathbf{Psh}(T)
with the pointwise weak equivalences, injective cofibrations, and injective fibrations is called the
injective model structure
(see
[
48
, Theorem 5.8]
).
The model structure on
s\mathbf{Psh}(T)
with the pointwise weak equivalences, projective cofibrations, and projective fibrations is called the
projective model structure
(see
[
48
, §5.5]
).

Definition 6.1.2
.

Associated to the localization functor

v:T\rightarrow T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]

we let

v^{*}:s\mathbf{Psh}(T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}])\rightarrow s\mathbf{Psh}(T)

denote the functor given by

v^{*}F(X):=F(X).

Here
F
is a simplicial presheaf on
T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]
and
X
is an object of
T
.

By
[
2
, Proposition I.5.1]
, there exist adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbf{Psh}(T)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbf{Psh}(T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{*}}

where
v_{\sharp}
is left adjoint to
v^{*}
, and
v^{*}
is left adjoint to
v_{*}
.

Definition 6.1.3
.

For any object
X
of
T
, we denote by
\mathcal{A}\downarrow X
the category where an object is a morphism
Y\rightarrow X
in
\mathcal{A}
, and a morphism is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

in
T
such that
g\in\mathcal{A}
.

Since
\mathcal{A}
admits a calculus of right fractions,
by the dual of
[
38
, I.2.3]
,
we have the formula

\operatorname{Hom}_{T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]}(X,S)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in\mathcal{A}\downarrow X}\operatorname{Hom}_{T}(Y,S).

(6.1.1)

Proposition 6.1.4
.

The functor
v_{\sharp}
is given by

v_{\sharp}F(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in\mathcal{A}\downarrow X}F(Y).

(6.1.2)

Démonstration.

By
[
2
, I.5.1.1]
, we reduce to the case when
F=Z\times\Delta[n]
, where
Z\in T
.
In this case (
6.1.2
) follows from (
6.1.1
).
∎

Proposition 6.1.5
.

The adjunction

v_{\sharp}:s\mathbf{Psh}(T)\rightleftarrows s\mathbf{Psh}(T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]):v^{*}

is a Quillen pair with respect to the projective model structures.

Démonstration.

The functor
v:T\rightarrow T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]
commutes with finite limits by using the dual of
[
38
, Proposition I.3.1]
.
Thus
v
is a morphism of sites for the trivial topologies.
We are done by
[
32
, Corollary 8.3]
.
∎

Proposition 6.1.6
.

The functor

v^{*}:s\mathbf{Psh}(T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}])\rightarrow s\mathbf{Psh}(T)

preserves pointwise weak equivalences and injective cofibrations. In particular, the adjunction

v^{*}:s\mathbf{Psh}(T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}])\rightleftarrows s\mathbf{Psh}(T):v_{*}

is a Quillen pair with respect to the injective model structures.

Démonstration.

Pointwise weak equivalences and injective cofibrations are defined objectwise, so the claim follows from the fact that
v:T\rightarrow T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]
is essentially surjective (
[
38
, 1.1]
).
∎

Definition 6.1.7
.

For simplicial presheaves
F
and
G
on
T
, let
{\mathbf{S}}(F,G)
denote the simplicial enrichment (or internal hom), which is given by the simplicial set

{\mathbf{S}}(F,G)_{n}:=\operatorname{Hom}_{s\mathbf{Psh}(T)}(F\times\Delta[n],G).

Definition 6.1.8
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a set of morphisms in
T
.

(1)

A simplicial presheaf
F
on
T
is
\mathcal{A}
-invariant
if the morphism

F(X\times Z)\rightarrow F(Y\times Z)

is a pointwise weak equivalence for any morphism
Y\rightarrow X
in
\mathcal{A}
and any
Z\in T
.

(2)

A morphism
f:F\rightarrow G
of simplicial presheaves on
T
is a
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalence
if for any
\mathcal{A}
-invariant simplicial presheaf
H
on
T
,

f
induces a weak equivalence of simplicial sets

{\mathbf{S}}(G,H)\rightarrow{\mathbf{S}}(F,H).

(3)

A morphism
f:F\rightarrow G
of simplicial presheaves on
T
is an
injective
\mathcal{A}
-fibration
if it has the right lifting property with respect to injective cofibrations that are
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalences.

Recall that the left Bousfield localization of the injective model structure on
s\mathbf{Psh}(T)
by
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalences is called the
injective
\mathcal{A}
-local model structure
,
which exists by
[
68
, Theorem 2.5, p. 71]
.
Note that the classes of
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalences, injective cofibrations, and injective
\mathcal{A}
-fibrations form the injective
\mathcal{A}
-local model structure.

Proposition 6.1.9
.

The functor
v_{\sharp}:s\mathbf{Psh}(T)\rightarrow s\mathbf{Psh}(T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}])
maps
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalences to pointwise weak equivalences.

Démonstration.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be the class of morphisms
f:F\rightarrow G
of simplicial presheaves on
T
such that
v_{\sharp}f
is a pointwise weak equivalence.
By
[
3
, Proposition 4.2.74]
, it suffices to check the following conditions.

(i)

\mathcal{C}
contains pointwise weak equivalences and morphisms of the form

u\times{\rm id}:Y\times F\rightarrow X\times F,

where
F
is a simplicial presheaf on
T
, and
u:Y\rightarrow X
is a morphism in
\mathcal{A}
.

(ii)

\mathcal{C}
has the 2-out-of-3 property.

(iii)

The class of projective cofibrations in
\mathcal{C}
is stable by pushouts and transfinite compositions.

By
[
38
, Proposition I.3.1]
the functor
v
commutes with finite limits.
Therefore
v_{\sharp}(u\times{\rm id})
is an isomorphism since
v_{\sharp}u
is an isomorphism.
If
f:F\rightarrow G
is a pointwise weak equivalence, then
F(X)\rightarrow G(X)
is a weak equivalence for any object
X
of
T
.
According to
[
31
, Proposition 7.3]
, filtered colimits of pointwise weak equivalences are pointwise weak equivalences.
Since
\mathcal{A}
admits a calculus of right fractions, we see that
\mathcal{A}\downarrow X
is cofiltered.
From the formula (
6.1.2
) we deduce that
v_{\sharp}F(X)\rightarrow v_{\sharp}G(X)
is a pointwise weak equivalence.
This verifies condtion (i).

The conditions (ii) and (iii) follow from Proposition
6.1.5
and the fact that the class of pointwise weak equivalences and class of projective cofibrations satisfy (ii) and (iii).
∎

Proposition 6.1.10
.

Let
F
be a simplicial presheaf on
T
.
If
F
is
\mathcal{A}
-invariant, then the unit map

F\rightarrow v^{*}v_{\sharp}F

is a pointwise weak equivalence.

Démonstration.

By Proposition
6.1.4
, we need to show that the induced morphism

F(X)\rightarrow\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in\mathcal{A}\downarrow X}F(Y)

(6.1.3)

is a pointwise weak equivalence for any
X\in T
.
Each
F(X)\rightarrow F(Y)
with
Y\in\mathcal{A}\downarrow X
is a pointwise weak equivalence since
F
is
\mathcal{A}
-invariant,
and the category
\mathcal{A}\downarrow X
is cofiltered since
\mathcal{A}
admits a calculus of right fractions.
Thus (
6.1.3
) is a pointwise weak equivalence since any filtered colimit of pointwise weak equivalences is again a pointwise weak equivalence by
[
31
, Proposition 7.3]
.
∎

Proposition 6.1.11
.

The unit of the adjunction
{\rm id}\rightarrow v^{*}v_{\sharp}
is an
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalence.

Démonstration.

Let
F
be a simplicial presheaf on
T
and choose an injective
\mathcal{A}
-fibrant resolution
f:F\rightarrow G
.
Note that
\mathcal{G}
is
\mathcal{A}
-invariant.
Then
v^{*}v_{\sharp}f
is a pointwise weak equivalence according to Propositions
6.1.6
and
6.1.9
.
Hence to show that
F\rightarrow v^{*}v_{\sharp}F
is an
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalence, it suffices to show that
G\rightarrow v^{*}v_{\sharp}G
is an
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalence.
For the latter, see Proposition
6.1.10
.
∎

Definition 6.1.12
.

Let
(I,\mathcal{A})
denote the set of morphisms
\{i_{0}:\operatorname{pt}\rightarrow I\}\cup\mathcal{A}
in
T
.
From Definition
6.1.8
(2), we have the notions of
I
-weak equivalences and
(I,\mathcal{A})
-weak equivalences.

Proposition 6.1.13
.

The functor

v^{*}:s\mathbf{Psh}(T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}])\rightarrow s\mathbf{Psh}(T)

maps
I
-weak equivalences to
(I,\mathcal{A})
-weak equivalences.

Démonstration.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be the class of morphisms
f:F\rightarrow G
of simplicial presheaves on
T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]
such that
v^{*}f
is an
(I,\mathcal{A})
-weak equivalence.
By
[
3
, Proposition 4.2.74]
, it suffices to check the following conditions.

(i)

\mathcal{C}
contains pointwise weak equivalences and morphisms of the form

i_{0}\times{\rm id}:{\rm pt}\times F\rightarrow I\times F,

where
F
is a simplicial presheaf on
T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]
.

(ii)

\mathcal{C}
has the 2-out-of-3 property.

(iii)

The class of morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
that are injective cofibrations is stable by pushouts and transfinite compositions.

Since
v^{*}
is a right adjoint of
v_{\sharp}
, it follows that
v^{*}
commutes with limits.
Thus to show
v^{*}(i_{0}\times{\rm id})
is an
(I,\mathcal{A})
-weak equivalence, it suffices to show that
v^{*}i_{0}
is an
(I,\mathcal{A})
-weak equivalence.
Note that
v^{*}i_{0}\simeq v^{*}v_{\sharp}i_{0}
.
Consider the induced commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm pt}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{*}v_{\sharp}{\rm pt}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{*}v_{\sharp}I}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{*}v_{\sharp}i_{0}}

of simplicial presheaves on
T
.
The upper horizontal morphism is an
I
-weak equivalence, and the vertical morphisms are
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalences by Proposition
6.1.11
.
Hence
v^{*}v_{\sharp}i_{0}
is an
(I,\mathcal{A})
-weak equivalence.
By Proposition
6.1.6
,
\mathcal{C}
contains the pointwise weak equivalences.
This verifies condition (i).

The conditions (ii) and (iii) follow from Proposition
6.1.6
since the
(I,\mathcal{A})
-weak equivalences and the injective cofibrations satisfy (ii) and (iii).
∎

6.2.
Construction of the singular functor
{\rm Sing}

Since
I
is an interval in
T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]
, we are entitled to the cosimplicial presheaf

\Delta_{I}^{\bullet}\in T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]

defined as follows, see
[
68
, p. 88]
.
On objects we set
\Delta_{I}^{n}:=I^{n}
.
Let
p:I^{n}\rightarrow{\rm pt}
denote the canonical morphism to the final object, and let
pr_{k}:I^{k}\rightarrow I
denote the
k
-th projection.
For any morphism
f:\{0,\ldots,n\}\rightarrow\{0,\ldots,m\}
in
\Delta
, let

\varphi(f):\{1,\ldots,m\}\rightarrow\{0,\ldots,n+1\}

denote the function given by

\varphi(f)(i):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\min\{l\in\{0,\ldots,n\}:f(l)\geq i\}&\text{if this set is nonempty}\\
n+1&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

The morphism
\Delta_{I}(f):I^{n}\rightarrow I^{m}
is given by

pr_{k}\circ\Delta_{I}(f):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}pr_{\varphi(f)(k)}&\text{if }\varphi(f)(k)\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\\
i_{0}\circ p&\text{if }\varphi(f)(k)=n+1\\
i_{1}\circ p&\text{if }\varphi(f)(k)=0.\end{array}\right.

We also have the simplicial presheaf

{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}^{I}(F)\in T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]

defined in
[
68
, p. 88]
.
Recall that

{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}^{I}(F)
is the diagonal simplicial presheaf of the bisimplicial presheaf

(\Delta_{I}^{\bullet},F_{\bullet}):(m,n)\mapsto\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{Psh}(T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}])}(\Delta_{I}^{m},F_{n}).

Definition 6.2.1
.

For any simplicial presheaf
F
on
T
, we set

{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}^{(I,\mathcal{A})}(F):=v^{*}{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}^{I}(v_{\sharp}F).

For simplicity we shall often omit the superscript
(I,\mathcal{A})
in
{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}^{(I,\mathcal{A})}(F)
.

Definition 6.2.2
.

Let
f,g:F\rightarrow G
be two morphisms of simplicial presheaves on
T
.
An
elementary
(I,\mathcal{A})
-homotopy
is a morphism

H:v_{\sharp}F\times I\rightarrow v_{\sharp}G

of simplicial presheaves on
T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]
such that

H\circ i_{0}=v_{\sharp}f\text{ and }H\circ i_{1}=v_{\sharp}g.

Two morphisms of simplicial presheaves on
T
are
(I,\mathcal{A})
-homotopic
if they can be connected by a finite number of
(I,\mathcal{A})
-homotopies.

A morphism
f:F\rightarrow G
of simplicial presheaves on
T
is a
strict
(I,\mathcal{A})
-homotopy equivalence
if there is a morphism
g:G\rightarrow F

of simplicial presheaves on
T
such that
f\circ g
and
g\circ f
are
(I,\mathcal{A})
-homotopic to the identity morphism.

In the following results, we argue following
[
68
, Proposition 3.4 - Corollary 3.8, pp. 89-90]
and
[
9
, Proposition 4.23 - Proposition 4.26]
.

Proposition 6.2.3
.

The canonical morphism

i_{0}:{\rm pt}\rightarrow I

is a strict
(I,\mathcal{A})
-homotopy equivalence.

Démonstration.

The diagram of simplicial presheaves on
\mathcal{T}[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I\times I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}\times{\rm id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}\times{\rm id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

displays an elementary
(I,\mathcal{A})
-homotopy from
{\rm id}=\mu\circ(i_{0}\times{\rm id})
to
i_{0}p=\mu\circ(i_{1}\times{\rm id})
.
Since
pi_{0}
is the identity morphism on
\operatorname{pt}
, we are done.
∎

Proposition 6.2.4
.

Suppose
f,g:F\rightarrow G
are
(I,\mathcal{A})
-homotopic morphisms of simplicial presheaves on
T
.
Then the induced morphisms

{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}(f),{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}(g):{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}(F)\rightarrow{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}(G)

are simplicially homotopic.

Démonstration.

We may assume there is an elementary
(I,\mathcal{A})
-homotopy
H:v_{\sharp}F\times I\rightarrow v_{\sharp}G
from
f
to
g
.
Then
H
is an
I
-homotopy from
v_{\sharp}f
to
v_{\sharp}g
, so
{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}(v_{\sharp}f)
and
{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}(v_{\sharp}g)
are simplicially homotopic by
[
68
, Proposition 3.4 in p. 89]
.
It remains to show that
v^{*}
preserves simplicial homotopies.

Since
v^{*}
is a right adjoint of
v_{\sharp}
, it follows that
v^{*}
commutes with limits.
Thus

v^{*}(F\times\Delta[1])\simeq v^{*}F\times\Delta[1]

for any simplicial presheaf
F
on
T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]
,
so
v^{*}
preserves simplicial homotopies.
∎

Corollary 6.2.5
.

For any simplicial presheaf
F
on
T
, the morphism

{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}(X)\rightarrow{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}(X\times I)

induced by
i_{0}:{\rm pt}\rightarrow I
is a pointwise weak equivalence.

Démonstration.

Follows from Propositions
6.2.3
and
6.2.4
.
∎

Proposition 6.2.6
.

For any simplicial presheaf
F
on
T
, the canonical morphism

F\rightarrow{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}(F)

is an
(I,\mathcal{A})
-weak equivalence.

Démonstration.

We have the factorization

F\rightarrow v^{*}v_{\sharp}F\rightarrow v^{*}{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}^{I}(v_{\sharp}F).

The first morphism is an
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalence due to Proposition
6.1.11
.
Thus,
by Proposition
6.1.13
,
it suffices to show the canonical morphism

v_{\sharp}F\rightarrow{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}^{I}(v_{\sharp}F)

is an
I
-weak equivalence.
This follows from
[
68
, Corollary 3.8, p. 89]
.
∎

Recall that
\Lambda
is a commutative unital ring.

Definition 6.2.7
.

Let
s\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda)
denote the category of simplicial sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules on
T
,
and let
\mathbf{C}^{\leq 0}(\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda))
denote the category of complexes
\mathcal{F}
of sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules on
T
such that
H^{i}(\mathcal{F})=0
for any
i>0
.

By the Dold-Kan correspondence
[
95
, Theorem 8.4.1]
, there are equivalences of categories

N:s\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows\mathbf{C}^{\leq 0}(\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda)):\Gamma

and

N:s\mathbf{Psh}(T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}],\Lambda)\rightleftarrows\mathbf{C}^{\leq 0}(\mathbf{Psh}(T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}],\Lambda)):\Gamma

Definition 6.2.8
.

An object
F
of
\mathbf{C}^{\leq 0}(\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda))
is
\mathcal{A}
-invariant
if
\Gamma(F)
is
\mathcal{A}
-invariant.
A morphism
f:F\rightarrow G
in
\mathbf{C}^{\leq 0}(\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda))
(resp.
\mathbf{C}^{\leq 0}(\mathbf{Psh}(T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}],\Lambda))
) is a
(I,\mathcal{A})
-weak equivalence (resp.
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalence)
if
\Gamma(f):\Gamma(F)\rightarrow\Gamma(G)
is a
(I,\mathcal{A})
-weak equivalence (resp.
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalence).

Let
\mathbf{C}^{-}(\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda))
denote the category of bounded above complexes of sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules.
Then an object
F\in\mathbf{C}^{-}(\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda))
is called
\mathcal{A}
-
invariant
if there exists an integer
n\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
F[n]\in\mathbf{C}^{\leq 0}(\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda))
is
\mathcal{A}
-
invariant
.

The
(I,\mathcal{A})
-weak equivalences in
\mathbf{C}^{-}(\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda))
and the
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalences in
\mathbf{C}^{-}(\mathbf{Psh}(T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}],\Lambda))
are defined similarly.

Suppose
G
is a presheaf of
\Lambda
-modules on
T
and consider the simplicial presheaf
\Lambda
-modules
\operatorname{Sing}_{\bullet}^{(I,\mathcal{A})}(G)
on
T
.
Taking the alternating sum of the face maps yields the
Suslin complex
of presheaves

C_{*}^{(I,\mathcal{A})}G.

We let
C_{*}G
be shorthand for
C_{*}^{(I,\mathcal{A})}G
when no confusion seems likely to arise, and define

C_{*}^{DK}(G):=N({\rm Sing}_{\bullet}^{(I,\mathcal{A})}(G)).

Here
C_{*}^{DK}(G)
is canonically a normalized subcomplex of
C_{*}(G)
.
The morphism

C_{*}^{DK}(G)\rightarrow C_{*}(G)

is a quasi-isomorphism, but not an isomorphism in general.

If
F
is a bounded above complex of presheaves on
T
,
we can consider
C_{*}F
and
C_{*}^{DK}F
as double complexes and form their total complexes
{\rm Tot}(C_{*}F)
and
{\rm Tot}(C_{*}^{DK}F)
.

Suppose
H\in s\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda)
.
The Eilenberg-Zilber theorem says that there is a quasi-isomorphism

N({\rm Sing}_{\bullet}^{(I,\mathcal{A})}(H))\simeq{\rm Tot}(C_{*}^{DK}(N(H))).

(6.2.1)

As in Definition
6.1.2
there exist adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}^{\leq 0}(\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}^{\leq 0}(\mathbf{Psh}(T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}],\Lambda)),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{*}}

where
v_{\sharp}
is left adjoint to
v^{*}
, and
v^{*}
is left adjoint to
v_{*}
.
For
F\in\mathbf{C}^{\leq 0}(\mathbf{Psh}(T[\mathcal{A}^{-1}],\Lambda))
and
X\in T
, we have

\Gamma(v^{*}F)(X)\simeq\Gamma(v^{*}F(X))=\Gamma(F(v(X))=\Gamma(F)(v(X))\simeq v^{*}(\Gamma(F))(X).

Thus
\Gamma
commutes with
v^{*}
.
Similarly, we can show that
N
commutes with
v^{*}
.
By adjunction we see that both
\Gamma
and
N
commute with
v_{\sharp}
.

Proposition 6.2.9
.

Let
F
be a bounded above complex of presheaves of
\Lambda
-modules on
T
.
Then the following properties hold.

(1)

If
F
is
\mathcal{A}
-invariant, then the unit of the adjunction

F\rightarrow v^{*}v_{\sharp}F

is a quasi-isomorphism.

(2)

The unit of the adjunction

F\rightarrow v^{*}v_{\sharp}F

is an
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalence.

(3)

The morphism

{\rm Tot}(C_{*}F)\rightarrow{\rm Tot}(C_{*}(F\times I))

induced by
i_{0}:{\rm pt}\rightarrow I
is a quasi-isomorphism.

(4)

The canonical morphism

F\rightarrow{\rm Tot}(C_{*}F)

is an
(I,\mathcal{A})
-weak equivalence.

Démonstration.

We observe that all the results in this section hold verbatim for simplicial presheaves of
\Lambda
-modules.
It suffices to prove these assertions for the shifted complex
F[n]
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Thus we may assume
F\in\mathbf{C}^{\leq 0}(\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda))
.
As noted above both
\Gamma
and
N
commute with both
v_{\sharp}
and
v^{*}
.
Moreover,
quasi-isomorphisms (resp.
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalences, resp.
(I,\mathcal{A})
-weak equivalences) in
\mathbf{C}^{\leq 0}(\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda))
corresponds to pointwise weak equivalences
(resp.
\mathcal{A}
-weak equivalences, resp.
(I,\mathcal{A})
-weak equivalences) in
s\mathbf{Psh}(T,\Lambda)
under
\Gamma
and
N
.

Suppose
F
is
\mathcal{A}
-invariant.
Then
\Gamma(F)
is
\mathcal{A}
-invariant,
and the
\Lambda
-module version of Proposition
6.1.10
shows the morphism

\Gamma(F)\rightarrow v^{*}v_{\sharp}\Gamma(F)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Gamma(v^{*}v_{\sharp}F)

is a pointwise weak equivalence.
Thus
F\rightarrow v^{*}v_{\sharp}F
is a quasi-isomorphism, which shows (1).
A similar argument shows (2).
Due to (
6.2.1
) we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\rm Tot}(C_{*}F)\simeq{\rm Tot}(C_{*}^{DK}F)\simeq N({\rm Sing}_{\bullet}^{(I,\mathcal{A})}(\Gamma(F))),

\displaystyle{\rm Tot}(C_{*}(F\times I))\simeq{\rm Tot}(C_{*}^{DK}(F\times I))\simeq N({\rm Sing}_{\bullet}^{(I,\mathcal{A})}(\Gamma(F\times I))).

Owing to the
\Lambda
-module version of Corollary
6.2.5
the composite morphism

{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}^{(I,\mathcal{A})}(\Gamma(F))\rightarrow{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}^{(I,\mathcal{A})}(\Gamma(F)\times I)\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\rm Sing}_{\bullet}^{(I,\mathcal{A})}(\Gamma(F\times I))

is an isomorphism.
Combining the above, we deduce (3).
A similar argument shows (4).
∎

7.
Properties of logarithmic motives

In this section, we formulate and prove the fundamental properties of our categories of logarithmic motives.
Most of these properties hold for categories associated with a base scheme
S
,
not necessarily a field,
subject to a list of axioms listed below.
This setup affords the case of
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
and its variants, see Proposition
7.0.2
.

It can be quite interesting to observe that certain implications hold among the axioms.
For example,
(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})
-invariance can be deduced by assuming Nisnevich descent (Nis-desc),

\overline{\Box}
-invariance, and dividing-descent, see Proposition
7.3.1
.
On the other hand,
we can include
(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})
-invariance as part of the axioms.
In that case dividing-descent can be deduced from (Nis-desc) and
(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})
-invariance for every
n\geq 1
.
We refer to Section
7.7
for further details.

After proving some general properties about
\mathbb{P}^{n}
-bundles and
\overline{\Box}^{n}
-bundles,
we begin by showing an invariance property for certain admissible blow-ups.
This is used for showing that log motives satisfy the
(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})
-invariance property.
We proceed by defining Thom motives,
a.k.a. motivic Thom spaces,
prove a log version of homotopy purity,
and construct Gysin maps for closed embeddings.

Assuming that
k
admits resolution of singularities allow us to prove a more potent invariance property for proper birational morphisms,
see Theorem
7.6.7
.
Finally we discuss strictly
(\mathbb{P}^{\bullet},\mathbb{P}^{\bullet-1})
-invariant and strictly dividing invariant complexes of sheaves on
SmlSm/k
.
This provides a convenient tool for constructing objects of
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
without explicitly verifying the dividing-invariance property on the level of sheaf cohomology.

Throughout this section, we fix a base scheme
S
and let
\mathscr{S}/S
denote either
lSm/S
or
SmlSm/S
.
Moreover, we fix a symmetric monoidal triangulated category
\mathcal{T}
,
and we fix a monoidal functor

M\colon\mathbf{Square}_{\mathscr{S}/S}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}

satisfying (Sq) and (MSq).
For the category
\mathbf{Square}_{\mathscr{S}/S}
,
n
-squares, and the conditions (Sq) and (MSq), we refer to Appendix
C
.
We choose to use
n
-squares because this is a convenient way to construct certain cones canonically within triangulated categories without referring to model categories or
\infty
-categories.
For example,
2
-squares are used in
[
29
]
to develop the theory of Gysin triangles in
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-motivic theory.
In most of our argument we only need
n
-squares for
n\leq 2
, but we need
3
-squares in Subsection
7.4
.

We shall often assume some of the following properties.

(
Zar
-sep)

Zariski separation property.
If
\{X_{1},\ldots,X_{r}\}
is a finite Zariski cover of
X
and
I=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{s}\}\subset\{1,\ldots,r\}
, we set

X_{I}:=X_{i_{1}}\times_{S}\cdots\times_{S}X_{i_{s}}.

Then for any commutative square of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X,}

(7.0.1)

the morphism

M(Y^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime})\rightarrow M(Y\rightarrow X)

is an isomorphism if for every nonempty
I\subset\{1,\ldots,r\}
there is an isomorphism

M(Y_{I}^{\prime}\rightarrow X_{I}^{\prime})\rightarrow M(Y_{I}\rightarrow X_{I})

where

Y_{I}:=Y\times_{X}X_{I},X_{I}^{\prime}:=X^{\prime}\times_{X}X_{I},\text{ and }Y_{I}^{\prime}:=Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y_{I}.

(
\overline{\Box}
-inv)

\overline{\Box}
-invariance property.
For any
X\in\mathscr{S}/S
the projection
X\times\overline{\Box}\rightarrow X
induces an isomorphism

M(X\times\overline{\Box})\xrightarrow{\simeq}M(X).

(
sNis
-des)

sNis
-descent property.

Every strict Nisnevich distinguished square of the form (
7.0.1
) induces an isomorphism

M(Y^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\simeq}M(Y\rightarrow X).

(
div
-des)

div
-descent property.

Every log modification
f:Y\rightarrow X
in
\mathscr{S}/S
induces an isomorphism

M(Y)\xrightarrow{\simeq}M(X).

(
(\mathbb{P}^{\bullet},\mathbb{P}^{\bullet-1})
-inv)

(\mathbb{P}^{\bullet},\mathbb{P}^{\bullet-1})
-invariance property.

For any
X\in\mathscr{S}/S
and positive integer
n>0
the projection
X\times(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})\rightarrow X
induces an isomorphism

M(X\times(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1}))\xrightarrow{\simeq}M(X).

Here we consider
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
as the hyperplane of
\mathbb{P}^{n}
defined by the equation
x_{n}=0
with respect to the coordinates
[x_{0}:\cdots:x_{n}]
.

(adm-blow)

Admissible blow-up property.

For every proper birational morphism
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
in
\mathscr{S}/S
such that the naturally induced morphism
Y-\partial Y\rightarrow X-\partial X
is an isomorphism, there is a naturally induced isomorphism

M(Y)\xrightarrow{\simeq}M(X).

The following figure illustrates how these properties are related.
Here, ROS stands for resolution of singularities.

(
Zar
-sep)

(
\overline{\Box}
-inv)

(
sNis
-des)

(adm-blow)

\mathscr{S}=lSm/k
(
