\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant.
∎

8.2.
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local objects in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)

We denote by

v\colon lCor/k\rightarrow lCor/k[(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}l/k)^{-1}]

the evident localization functor, where
\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}l/k
is the class of admissible blow-ups in Definition
7.6.1
.
As observed in Definition
C.2.1
there exist adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}^{\leq 0}(\mathbf{Psh}(lCor/k,\Lambda))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}^{\leq 0}(\mathbf{Psh}(lCor/k[(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}l/k)^{-1}],\Lambda)).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{*}}

Here
v_{\sharp}
is left adjoint to
v^{*}
and
v^{*}
is left adjoint to
v_{*}
.

Proposition 8.2.1
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Let
X
be a smooth and proper scheme over
k
, and let
Y
be an fs log scheme log smooth over
k
.
Then there is a naturally induced isomorphism

v^{*}v_{\sharp}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y)\simeq\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y-\partial Y).

Démonstration.

Let
Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
be an admissible blow-up.
The induced morphism

Y^{\prime}-\partial Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y-\partial Y

of schemes over
k
is an isomorphism.
Thus we can form the composite homomorphism of
\Lambda
-modules

\varphi_{Y^{\prime}}\colon\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y^{\prime})\rightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y^{\prime}-\partial Y^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y-\partial Y).

From the formula (
6.1.2
) we have the isomorphism

v^{*}v_{\sharp}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}l/k)\downarrow Y}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y^{\prime}).

Collecting the
\varphi_{Y^{\prime}}
’s, we get the induced homomorphism of
\Lambda
-modules

\varphi\colon v^{*}v_{\sharp}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y)\rightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)(Y-\partial Y).

Let
V\in\operatorname{lCor}(Y^{\prime},X)
be a finite log correspondence.
If
\varphi_{Y^{\prime}}(V)=0
, then
V=0
since
V
is the closure of
\varphi_{Y^{\prime}}(V)
in
Y^{\prime}\times X
.
Thus
\varphi
is injective.
It remains to show that
\varphi
is surjective.

Let
W\in\operatorname{Cor}(Y-\partial Y,X)
be an elementary correspondence.
By Raynaud-Gruson
[
79
, Théorème 5.7.9]
,
there is an algebraic space
\underline{Y^{\prime}}
and a blow-up
\underline{f}\colon\underline{Y^{\prime}}\rightarrow\underline{Y}
such that
\underline{f}
is an isomorphism on
Y-\partial Y
and the closure
\underline{W^{\prime}}
of
\underline{W}
in
\underline{Y^{\prime}}\times\underline{X}
is flat over
\underline{Y^{\prime}}
.
The algebraic space
\underline{Y^{\prime}}
is a scheme because it is a blow-up of a scheme.
Further,

\underline{W^{\prime}}
is finite over
\underline{Y^{\prime}}
since
\underline{Y^{\prime}}\times\underline{X}
is proper over
\underline{Y^{\prime}}
.

By resolution of singularities,
there is a blow-up
g\colon\underline{Y^{\prime\prime}}\rightarrow\underline{Y^{\prime}}
such that
\underline{Y^{\prime\prime}}
is smooth over
k
and the complement of
(\underline{f}\circ\underline{g})^{-1}(Y-\partial Y)
in
\underline{Y^{\prime\prime}}
consists of strict normal crossing divisors
Z_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,Z_{r}^{\prime}
.
Setting
Y^{\prime\prime}:=(\underline{Y^{\prime\prime}},Z_{1}^{\prime}+\cdots+Z_{r}^{\prime})
the induced morphism
Y^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow Y
of fs log schemes log smooth over
k
is an admissible blow-up.
Since the closure
\underline{W^{\prime\prime}}
of
\underline{W}
in
\underline{Y^{\prime\prime}}\times\underline{X}
is a closed subscheme of
\underline{W^{\prime}}\times_{\underline{Y^{\prime}}}\underline{Y^{\prime\prime}}
,
\underline{W^{\prime\prime}}
is finite over
\underline{Y^{\prime\prime}}
.
It follows that
W
can be extended to a finite correspondence from
\underline{Y^{\prime\prime}}
to
X
.
Since
X
has the trivial log structure,
such a finite correspondence gives a finite log correspondence from
Y^{\prime\prime}
to
X
by Example
2.1.3
(2).
Thus
\varphi
is surjective.
∎

Proposition 8.2.2
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
For every smooth and proper scheme
X
over
k
, there is an isomorphism

a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\simeq\omega^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)

in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
Likewise, there are isomorphisms

a_{d\acute{e}t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\simeq\omega^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)\text{ and }a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\simeq\omega^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)

in
{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
and
{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
,
respectively.

Démonstration.

We only treat the case of dividing Nisnevich coverings since the proofs are similar.
Owing to Lemma
4.4.3
we have an isomorphism

a_{dNis}^{*}v^{*}v_{\sharp}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\simeq v^{*}v_{\sharp}a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X).

Moreover, Proposition
8.2.1
implies

a_{dNis}^{*}v^{*}v_{\sharp}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\simeq a_{dNis}^{*}\omega^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X),

and from Remark
7.6.8
we deduce

v^{*}v_{\sharp}a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)\simeq a_{dNis}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X).

To conclude, note that
\omega^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)
is a dividing Nisnevich sheaf, see Lemma
8.1.11
.
∎

For a bounded above complex
\mathcal{F}
of presheaves with log transfers, recall the Suslin double complex
C_{*}\mathcal{F}
from Definition
C.2.1
.
If
\mathcal{F}
is a presheaf with log transfers, then for
U\in lSm/k
, we have

C_{*}\mathcal{F}(U):=(\cdots\rightarrow\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}l/k)\downarrow(U\times\Delta_{\overline{\Box}}^{1})}\mathcal{F}(Y)\rightarrow\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}l/k)\downarrow U}\mathcal{F}(Y)).

For a bounded above complex
\mathcal{F}
of presheaves with log transfers, we also have the Suslin double complex
C_{*}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathcal{F}
.
If
\mathcal{F}
is a presheaf with transfers, then for
U\in Sm/k
,

C_{*}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathcal{F}(U):=(\cdots\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(U\times\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{1})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(U)).

Proposition 8.2.3
.

Suppose
\mathcal{F}
is a bounded above complex of presheaves with log transfers on
Sm/k
.
Then there is a quasi-isomorphism of total complexes

{\rm Tot}(C_{*}\omega^{*}\mathcal{F})\simeq{\rm Tot}(\omega^{*}C_{*}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathcal{F}).

Démonstration.

Using the technique in
[
64
, Lemma 9.12]
, we reduce to the case when
\mathcal{F}
is a presheaf with log transfers.
For every
X\in lSm/k
, admissible blow-up
Y\rightarrow\Delta_{\overline{\Box}}^{n}\times X
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
we have

\omega^{*}\mathcal{F}(Y)=\mathcal{F}(Y-\partial Y)\simeq\mathcal{F}(\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{n}\times(X-\partial X)).

Thus for the internal hom object

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{n},\mathcal{F})

mapping
U\in Sm/k
to
\mathcal{F}(U\times\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{n})
, we have that

\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}l/k)\downarrow(\Delta_{\overline{\Box}}^{n}\times X)}\omega^{*}\mathcal{F}(Y)=\mathcal{F}(\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{n}\times(X-\partial X))\simeq(\omega^{*}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{n},\mathcal{F}))(X).

Since

C_{*}\omega^{*}\mathcal{F}(U):=(\cdots\rightarrow\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}l/k)\downarrow(U\times\Delta_{\overline{\Box}}^{1})}\omega^{*}\mathcal{F}(Y)\rightarrow\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}l/k)\downarrow U}\omega^{*}\mathcal{F}(Y)),

and

\omega^{*}C_{*}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathcal{F}(U):=(\cdots\rightarrow\omega^{*}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{1},\mathcal{F})(X)\rightarrow\omega^{*}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{0},\mathcal{F})(X)),

we deduce the desired isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 8.2.4
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Let
X
be a scheme smooth over
k
.
Then there are isomorphisms

\omega^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)\simeq C_{*}\omega^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)\simeq\omega^{*}C_{*}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)

in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
,
{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
, and
{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.

Démonstration.

For the first isomorphism, apply Proposition
6.2.9
(4) and Remark
7.6.8
.
For the second isomorphism, apply Proposition
8.2.3
.
∎

Proposition 8.2.5
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Let
X
be a scheme smooth over
k
, and let
Y
be an fs log schemes log smooth over
k
.
Then for every integer
i\in\mathbb{Z}
there is a naturally induced isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y),\omega^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)[i])\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y-\partial Y),M(X)[i]).

Démonstration.

Due to Proposition
8.2.4
, we have an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y),\omega^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)[i])\\
\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y),\omega^{*}C_{*}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)[i]).

Since the Suslin complex
C_{*}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)
is strictly
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant for the Nisnevich topology by
[
64
, Corollary 14.9]
,
we deduce
\omega^{*}C_{*}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)
is strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant for the dividing Nisnevich topology by Proposition
8.1.12
.
Thus owing to Proposition
5.2.3
there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y),\omega^{*}C_{*}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)[i])\simeq\mathbf{H}_{dNis}^{i}(Y,\omega^{*}C_{*}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)).

From Lemma
8.1.10
we obtain the isomorphism

\mathbf{H}_{dNis}^{i}(Y,\omega^{*}C_{*}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X))\simeq\mathbf{H}_{Nis}^{i}(Y-\partial Y,C_{*}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X)).

We also have an isomorphism due to
[
64
, Proposition 14.16]

\mathbf{H}_{Nis}^{i}(Y-\partial Y,C_{*}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(X))\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y-\partial Y),M(X)[i]).

To conclude, we combine the above isomorphisms.
∎

Proposition 8.2.6
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Let
X
be a smooth and proper scheme over
k
, and let
Y
be an fs log scheme log smooth over
k
.
Then for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there is a natural isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],M(X))\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y-\partial Y)[i],M(X)).

Démonstration.

Immediate from Propositions
8.2.2
and
8.2.5
.
∎

Remark 8.2.7
.

We do not expect that Proposition
8.2.6
holds for non-proper schemes.

Recall from (
) the adjunction

\omega_{\sharp}:{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda):R\omega^{*}.

Proposition 8.2.8
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Let
X
be a proper and smooth scheme over
k
.
Then the unit of the adjunction
{\rm id}\rightarrow R\omega^{*}\omega_{\sharp}
induces an isomorphism

M(X)\rightarrow R\omega^{*}\omega_{\sharp}M(X)

in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.

Démonstration.

Appealing to the generators
M(Y)[i]
of
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
,
where
Y\in lSm/k
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
,
it suffices to show there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],M(X))\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],R\omega^{*}M(X)).

This follows from Proposition
8.2.6
and the isomorphisms

\begin{split}&\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],R\omega^{*}M(X))\\
\simeq&\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(\omega_{\sharp}M(Y)[i],M(X))\\
\simeq&\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y-\partial Y)[i],M(X)).\end{split}

∎

Proposition 8.2.9
.

Let
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r}
be divisors forming a strict normal crossing divisor on a smooth scheme
X
over
k
.
Suppose the smooth subscheme
Z
of
X
has strict normal crossing and is not contained in
Z_{1}\cup\cdots\cup Z_{r}
.
Let
E
be the exceptional divisor of the blow-up
B_{Z}X
.
Then for

Y:=(X,Z_{1}+\cdots+Z_{r})

there is a commutative diagram in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M((B_{Z}Y,E)\rightarrow Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}M((Y-\partial Y\up{Z}\rightarrow Y-\partial Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces MTh(N_{Z}Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}(MTh(N_{Z-\partial Y\cap Z}(Y-\partial Y))).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

The vertical morphisms are the Gysin isomorphisms, and the horizontal morphisms are induced by the unit of the adjunction
{\rm id}\rightarrow R\omega^{*}\omega_{\sharp}
.

Démonstration.

With the same notations in the proof of Theorem
7.5.4
,
we set

\displaystyle U:=(B_{Z}Y,E),\;V:=Y,\;U^{\prime}:=(B_{Z\times\overline{\Box}}(D_{Z}Y),E^{D}),\;V^{\prime}:=B_{Z\times\overline{\Box}}(D_{Z}Y),

\displaystyle U^{\prime\prime}:=(B_{Z}(N_{Z}Y),E^{N}),\;V^{\prime\prime}:=N_{Z}Y.

The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(U\rightarrow V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}M((U-\partial U)\rightarrow(V-\partial V))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(U^{\prime}\rightarrow V^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}M((U-\partial U)\rightarrow(V^{\prime}-\partial V^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(U^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow V^{\prime\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}M((U^{\prime\prime}-\partial U^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow(V^{\prime\prime}-\partial V^{\prime\prime}))}

commutes because the horizontal morphisms are obtained from an adjunction.
Our assertion follows from this.
∎

Proposition 8.2.10
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme in
SmlSm/k
with a rank
n
vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
.
View
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})
as the closed subscheme of
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O})
at infinity.
Then
\mathcal{F}:=\mathcal{E}-\partial\mathcal{E}
is a vector bundle over
X-\partial X
,
and there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces MTh(\mathcal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}MTh(\mathcal{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}\oplus\mathcal{O})).}

(8.2.1)

Here the vertical morphisms are obtained from
Proposition
7.4.5
and
[
68
, Proposition 2.17, p. 112]
,
and the horizontal morphisms are induced by the unit of the adjunction
{\rm id}\rightarrow R\omega^{*}\omega_{\sharp}
.

Démonstration.

We form the fs log schemes
Y
,
Y^{\prime}
and the divisors
E
,
E^{\prime}
as in the proof of Proposition
7.4.5
,
and set

V:=(Y,E)-\partial(Y,E),\;V^{\prime}:=(Y^{\prime},E^{\prime})-\partial(Y^{\prime},E^{\prime}).

Then there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M((Y,E)\rightarrow\mathcal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M((Y^{\prime},E^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}M(V\rightarrow\mathcal{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}M(V^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}\oplus\mathcal{O}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}\oplus\mathcal{O})).}

In the proof of Proposition
7.4.5
and
[
68
, Proposition 2.17, p. 112]
it is shown that the horizontal morphisms in the right-hand side square are isomorphisms.
After inverting these, we obtain the required commutative diagram.
∎

Theorem 8.2.11
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Let
X
be an fs log scheme log smooth over
k
. If the underlying scheme
\underline{X}
is proper over
k
,
then the unit of the adjunction
\operatorname{id}\rightarrow R\omega^{*}\omega_{\sharp}
induces an isomorphism

M(X)\rightarrow R\omega^{*}\omega_{\sharp}M(X).

Therefore, for every
Y\in lSm/k
and an integer
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there is a natural isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],M(X))\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y-\partial Y)[i],M(X-\partial X)).

Démonstration.

The question is dividing local on
X
, so we may assume that
X\in SmlSm/k
by Proposition
A.10.2
.
Then
\partial X
is a strict normal crossing divisior formed by smooth divisors
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r}
on
X
,
and we can form

\displaystyle X_{1}:=(\underline{X},Z_{2}+\cdots+Z_{r}),\;U:=X-\partial X,\;U_{1}:=X_{1}-\partial X_{1},

\displaystyle V:=Y-\partial Y,\;W_{1}:=Z_{1}-\partial X\cap Z_{1}.

The proof proceeds by induction on
r
.
Proposition
8.2.8
implies the claim for
r=0
.
Assuming
r>0
, by Proposition
8.2.10
there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces MTh(N_{Z_{1}}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}MTh(N_{W_{1}}U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathbb{P}(N_{Z_{1}}X)\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(N_{Z_{1}}X\oplus\mathcal{O}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}M(\mathbb{P}(N_{W_{1}}U)\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(N_{W_{1}}U\oplus\mathcal{O})).}

The vertical morphisms are isomorphisms by Proposition
7.4.5
and
[
68
, Proposition 2.17, p. 112]
.
By induction, there are isomorphisms

M(\mathbb{P}(N_{Z_{1}}X))\xrightarrow{\simeq}R\omega^{*}M(\mathbb{P}(N_{W_{1}}U))

and

M(\mathbb{P}(N_{Z_{1}}X\oplus\mathcal{O}))\xrightarrow{\simeq}R\omega^{*}M(\mathbb{P}(N_{W_{1}}U\oplus\mathcal{O})).

It follows that the lower horizontal morphism is an isomorphism, and hence the upper horizontal morphism is an isomorphism.

Due to Proposition
8.2.9
there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(X\rightarrow X_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}M(U\rightarrow U_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces MTh(N_{Z_{1}}X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}MTh(N_{W_{1}}U).}

The above shows the lower horizontal morphism is an isomorphism.
The vertical morphisms are isomorphisms by Theorem
7.5.4
and
[
64
, Theorem 15.15]
,
respectively.
This allows us to conclude for the upper horizontal morphism.

By induction
M(X_{1})\rightarrow R\omega^{*}M(U_{1})
is an isomorphism.
The five lemma implies
M(X)\rightarrow R\omega^{*}M(U_{1})
is an isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 8.2.12
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Then there is an adjunction

R\omega^{*}:{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda):R\omega_{*}

Démonstration.

By
[
21
, Proposition 1.3.19]
we need to show that
{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
admits a set of objects
\mathcal{G}
with the following properties.

(1)

For each
K\in\mathcal{G}
,
K
(resp.
R\omega^{*}K
) is compact in
{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
(resp.
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
).

(2)

{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
(resp.
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
) is generated by
\mathcal{G}
(resp.
R\omega^{*}\mathcal{G}
).

(3)

{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
is compactly generated.

By Remark
5.2.6
(resp. Proposition
5.2.5
),
{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
(resp.
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
) is generated by compact objects
M(X)[i]
for
X\in Sm/k
(resp.
X\in lSm/k
).
Hence it remains to check that
R\omega^{*}M(X)
is a compact object in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.

By resolution of singularities,
there is a projective fs log scheme
\overline{X}
in
SmlSm/k
with
X\simeq\overline{X}-\partial\overline{X}
.
Theorem
8.2.11
shows there is an isomorphism

M(\overline{X})\xrightarrow{\simeq}R\omega^{*}M(\overline{X}-\partial\overline{X})\simeq R\omega^{*}M(X).

Thus
R\omega^{*}M(X)
is compact in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
since the motive
M(\overline{X})
is compact in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
∎

To summarize the above, we note there are adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\omega_{*}}

Theorem 8.2.13
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Then the functor

R\omega^{*}\colon{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)

is fully faithful.

Démonstration.

We need to show that the unit of the adjunction
{\rm id}\rightarrow R\omega_{*}R\omega^{*}
is an isomorphism.
By invoking the generators
M(X)[i]
of
{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
,
where
X\in Sm/k
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
,
we only need to show there is an isomorphism

M(X)\rightarrow R\omega_{*}R\omega^{*}M(X).

Again by invoking the generators, we reduce to showing there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],M(X))\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],R\omega_{*}R\omega^{*}M(X))

for any
Y\in Sm/k
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

By resolution of singularities, there are proper fs log schemes
\overline{X}
and
\overline{Y}
in
SmlSm/k
such that
X\simeq\overline{X}-\partial\overline{X}
and
Y\simeq\overline{Y}-\partial\overline{Y}
.
Owing to Theorem
8.2.11
we have

R\omega^{*}M(X)\simeq M(\overline{X}),\;R\omega^{*}M(Y)\simeq M(\overline{Y}).

Applying Theorem
8.2.11
we deduce the isomorphisms

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],M(X))\simeq

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(\overline{Y})[i],M(\overline{X}))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(R\omega^{*}M(Y)[i],R\omega^{*}M(X)).

By adjunction, this concludes the proof.
∎

Definition 8.2.14
.

An object
\mathcal{F}
of
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
is called
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local
if for any
X\in lSm/k
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
the projection
X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\rightarrow X
induces an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X)[i],\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X\times\mathbb{A}^{1})[i],\mathcal{F}).

We note that if a complex
\mathcal{F}
of dividing Nisnevich sheaves with log transfers is both strictly
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant and strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant,
then the associated object in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local.

Proposition 8.2.15
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Let
\mathcal{F}
be an
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local object of
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
Then, for every
Y\in lSm/k
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there is a natural isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],\mathcal{F})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y-\partial Y)[i],\mathcal{F}).

Démonstration.

We may assume
\mathcal{F}
is a strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant complex of dividing Nisnevich sheaves with log transfers by taking a fibrant replacement.
Then,
by Proposition
5.2.3
,
there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],\mathcal{F})\simeq\mathbf{H}_{dNis}^{i}(Y,\mathcal{F}).

If
Y
has the trivial log structure, then the dividing Nisnevich topology on
Y
agrees with the Nisnevich topology on
Y
, and we obtain

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],\mathcal{F})\simeq\mathbf{H}_{Nis}^{i}(Y,\mathcal{F}).

(8.2.2)

Thus the restriction
\mathcal{G}
of
\mathcal{F}
on
Sm/k
is a strictly
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant complex of Nisnevich sheaves with transfers.

The claim is dividing Nisnevich local on
Y
, so we may assume
\partial Y
is a strict normal crossing divisor formed by smooth divisors
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r}
on
Y
.
We set

Y_{1}:=(\underline{Y},Z_{2}+\cdots+Z_{r}),

W=(Z_{1},Z_{1}\cap Z_{2}+\cdots+Z_{1}\cap Z_{r}),

and proceed by induction on
r
. The claim is evident for
r=0
.

In the following, we assume
r\geq 1
and set

Y^{\prime}:=(D_{Z_{1}}Y_{1},Z_{1}\times\overline{\Box}),\;Y_{1}^{\prime}:=D_{Z_{1}}Y,\;Y^{\prime\prime}:=(N_{Z_{1}}Y_{1},Z_{1}),\;Y_{1}^{\prime\prime}:=N_{Z_{1}}Y_{1}.

There is a naturally induced commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y\rightarrow Y_{1})[i],\mathcal{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y-\partial Y\rightarrow Y_{1}-\partial Y_{1})[i],\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y_{1}^{\prime})[i],\mathcal{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y^{\prime}-\partial Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y_{1}^{\prime}-\partial Y_{1}^{\prime})[i],\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow Y_{1}^{\prime\prime})[i],\mathcal{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y-\partial Y\rightarrow Y_{1}^{\prime\prime}-\partial Y_{1}^{\prime\prime})[i],\mathcal{G}).}

The left vertical morphisms are isomorphisms by Theorem
7.5.4
, and the right vertical morphisms are isomorphisms by (
8.2.2
) and the proof of
[
89
, Proposition 3.5.4]
.
The lower horizontal morphism is an isomorphism by induction, so the upper horizontal morphism is also an isomorphism.
By induction, there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y_{1})[i],\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y_{1}-\partial Y_{1})[i],\mathcal{G})

This finishes the proof on account of the five lemma.
∎

Theorem 8.2.16
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Then the essential image of the functor

R\omega^{*}\colon{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)

is the full subcategory of
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
consisting of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local objects.

Démonstration.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be an object of
{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
Then, for every
Y\in lSm/k
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
,
the isomorphisms

\begin{split}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],R\omega^{*}\mathcal{G})&\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(\omega_{\sharp}M(Y)[i],\mathcal{G})\\
&\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y-\partial Y)[i],\mathcal{G})\\
&\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M((Y-\partial Y)\times\mathbb{A}^{1})[i],\mathcal{G})\\
&\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(\omega_{\sharp}M(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1})[i],\mathcal{G})\\
&\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1})[i],R\omega^{*}\mathcal{G})\end{split}

show that
R\omega^{*}\mathcal{G}
is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local.

Now let
\mathcal{F}
be an
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local object of
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
To show that
\mathcal{F}
is in the essential image of
R\omega^{*}
, it suffices to show that the unit of the adjunction
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow R\omega^{*}\omega_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}
is an isomorphism.
We show this by checking that, for any
Y\in lSm/k
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there is a naturally induced isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(\omega_{\sharp}M(Y)[i],\omega_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}).

The open immersion
Y-\partial Y\rightarrow Y
induces a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],\mathcal{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(\omega_{\sharp}M(Y)[i],\omega_{\sharp}\mathcal{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y-\partial Y)[i],\mathcal{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(\omega_{\sharp}M(Y-\partial Y)[i],\omega_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}).}

The left vertical homomorphism is an isomorphism by Proposition
8.2.15
, and the right vertical homomorphism is an isomorphism since

\omega_{\sharp}M(Y)\simeq M(Y-\partial Y)\simeq\omega_{\sharp}M(Y-\partial Y).

Hence to show the upper horizontal homomorphism is an isomorphism, it suffices to show the lower horizontal homomorphism is an isomorphism,
i.e., we are reduced to the case when
Y\in Sm/k
.

We may view
\mathcal{F}
as a strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant object of
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda))
.
Recall from (
) the adjunction

L\lambda_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{Nis}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda))\colon\lambda^{*}\simeq R\lambda^{*}\simeq\omega_{\sharp}.

Thus, for every
Y\in Sm/k
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there are isomorphisms

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{Nis}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda))}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(Y)[i],\omega_{\sharp}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{Nis}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda))}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(Y)[i],\lambda^{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda))}(L\lambda_{\sharp}\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(Y)[i],\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}^{\mathrm{ltr}}_{dNis}(k,\Lambda))}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(Y)[i],\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],\mathcal{F})

Together with the assumption that
\mathcal{F}
is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local, we deduce
\omega_{\sharp}F
is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local,
and

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}_{Nis}^{\mathrm{tr}}(k,\Lambda))}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}(Y)[i],\omega_{\sharp}\mathcal{F})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],\omega_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}).

This shows the isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],\mathcal{F})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],\omega_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}),

To conclude the proof, we note that

\omega_{\sharp}M(Y)\simeq M(Y).

∎

Theorem 8.2.17
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Then there is an equivalence of triangulated categories

{\bf logDM}_{\rm prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\simeq{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).

Démonstration.

Owing to Theorem
8.2.16
it suffices to identify
{\bf logDM}_{\rm prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
with the essential image of the functor

R\omega^{*}\colon{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).

The essential image of
R\omega^{*}
is the smallest triangulated subcategory of
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
that is closed under small sums and contains
R\omega^{*}M(X)
for every
X\in Sm/k
.
On the other hand,
{\bf logDM}_{\rm prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
is the smallest triangulated subcategory of
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
that is closed under small sums and contains the motive
M(Y)
for every
Y\in lSm/k
,
where
Y
is proper over
k
.

Owing to Theorem
8.2.11
, there is an isomorphism

M(Y)\simeq R\omega^{*}M(Y-\partial Y).

Thus
{\bf logDM}_{\rm prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\subset\operatorname{Im}R\omega^{*}
.
By resolution of singularities, there exists an object
X^{\prime}\in SmlSm/k
such that
X^{\prime}-\partial X^{\prime}\simeq X
.
Moreover, by Theorem
8.2.11
, there is an isomorphism

M(X^{\prime})\simeq R\omega^{*}M(X).

Thus we have the inclusion

\operatorname{Im}R\omega^{*}\subset{\bf logDM}_{\rm prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).

∎

Example 8.2.18
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Then the functor

R\omega^{*}\colon{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)

is
not
essentially surjective.
Indeed,
in Theorem
9.7.1
we show that for every
X\in SmlSm/k
and
i,j\geq 0
there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\Omega^{j}_{-/k}[i])\simeq H^{i}_{Zar}(\underline{X},\Omega^{j}_{X/k}).

However, the groups
H^{i}_{Zar}(\underline{X},\Omega^{j}_{X/k})
and
H^{i}_{Zar}(\underline{X}\times\mathbb{A}^{1},\Omega^{j}_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/k})
are non-isomorphic,
so that Theorem
8.2.16
implies
\Omega^{j}_{X/k}
is not in the essential image of
R\omega^{*}
.

8.3.
Projective bundle formula

In this subsection, we formulate and prove the projective bundle formula under the assumption of resolution of singularities.
We begin by discussing orientations on
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.

Definition 8.3.1
.

We say that
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
admits an
orientation
if for any
X\in lSm/k
, there is a morphism

c_{1}\colon{\rm Pic}(\underline{X})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\Lambda(1)[2])

of sets,
functorial in
X
,
such that the class of the canonical line bundle in
{\rm Pic}(\mathbb{P}^{1})
corresponds to the canonical morphism
M(\mathbb{P}^{1})\rightarrow\Lambda(1)[2]
.

Example 8.3.2
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Recall that
\Lambda(1)[2]=M(\operatorname{pt}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1})
.
Since
\operatorname{pt}
and
\mathbb{P}^{1}
are smooth and proper, by Proposition
8.2.6
, we have

{\rm Pic}(X-\partial X)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\Lambda(1)[2]).

Thus we are entitled to the composition

{\rm Pic}(\underline{X})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u^{*}}}{{\rightarrow}}{\rm Pic}(X-\partial X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\Lambda(1)[2]),

where
u\colon X-\partial X\rightarrow\underline{X}
is the induced open immersion.
Hence
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
admits an orientation.

Construction 8.3.3
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme log smooth over
k
, and let
\mathcal{P}
be a
\mathbb{P}^{n}
-bundle over
X
,
i.e., Zariski locally on
X
, the morphism
\mathcal{P}\rightarrow X
is isomorphic to the projection
\mathbb{P}^{n}\times X\rightarrow X
.
If
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
admits an orientation, then the canonical line bundle over
\mathcal{P}
induces a morphism

\tau_{1}\colon M(\mathcal{P})\rightarrow\Lambda(1)[2]

in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
More generally,
for
1\leq i\leq n
,
there is a morphism

\tau_{i}\colon M(\mathcal{P})\rightarrow\Lambda(i)[2i]

(8.3.1)

given by the composition

M(\mathcal{P})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{\longrightarrow}}M(\mathcal{P})^{\otimes i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau_{1}^{\otimes i}}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\Lambda(1)[2])^{\otimes i}\simeq\Lambda(i)[2i].

We also have a morphism

\sigma_{i}\colon M(\mathcal{P})\rightarrow M(X)(i)[2i]

given by the composition

M(\mathcal{P})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{\longrightarrow}}M(\mathcal{P})\otimes M(\mathcal{P})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{id}\times\tau_{i}}}{{\longrightarrow}}M(\mathcal{P})\otimes\Lambda(i)[2i]\longrightarrow M(X)\otimes\Lambda(i)[2i]\simeq M(X)(i)[2i].

This defines an induced morphism

\sigma\colon M(\mathcal{P})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}M(X)(i)[2i].

Proposition 8.3.4
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
For every
X\in lSm/k
and integer
n\geq 0
, the morphism

\sigma\colon M(\mathbb{P}_{X}^{n})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}M(X)(i)[2i]

is an isomorphism.

Démonstration.

Using the monoidal structure, we have that

M(\mathcal{P})\simeq M(X)\otimes M(\mathbb{P}^{n})\in{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).

Hence we may assume that
X=\operatorname{Spec}(k)
,
i.e.,
for our purposes, it suffices to show there is an isomorphism

M(\mathbb{P}^{n})\simeq\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}\Lambda(i)[2i].

It suffices to show that for
Y\in lSm/k
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],M(\mathbb{P}^{n}))\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}\Lambda(i)[2i]).

By Proposition
8.2.6
,
there is a commutative diagram with vertical isomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],M(\mathbb{P}^{n}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)[i],\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}\Lambda(i)[2i])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y-\partial Y)[i],M(\mathbb{P}^{n}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y-\partial Y)[i],\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}\Lambda(i)[2i]).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Hence it suffices to show there is an isomorphism

M(\mathbb{P}^{n})\simeq\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}\Lambda(i)[2i]

in
{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
, which is noted in
[
64
, Corollary 15.5, Exercise 15.11]
.
∎

Theorem 8.3.5
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a vector bundle of rank
n+1
over an fs log scheme
X
log smooth over
k
.
Then the naturally induced morphism

\sigma\colon M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}))\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}M(X)(i)[2i]

is an isomorphism in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.

Démonstration.

We argue as in
[
29
, Theorem 3.2]
.
For
\mathcal{P}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})
and an open immersion
U\rightarrow X
of fs log schemes over
k
, consider the induced commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{U}\times\mathcal{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{P}}

of fs log schemes over
k
,
where
\mathcal{P}_{U}:=\mathcal{P}\times_{X}U
and the horizontal morphisms are the graph morphisms.
There are induced morphisms

M(\mathcal{P}_{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{P})\rightarrow M(\mathcal{P}_{U}\times\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{P})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}M(\mathcal{P}_{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{P})\otimes M(\mathcal{P})\rightarrow M(U\rightarrow X)\otimes M(\mathcal{P}).

Using the morphism
M(\mathcal{P})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}\Lambda(i)[2i]
from Construction
8.3.3
,
we obtain

M(\mathcal{P}_{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{P})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}M(U\rightarrow X)(i)[2i].

We use induction on the finite number
m
of Zariski open subsets appearing in a trivialization of
\mathcal{E}
.
The case
m=1
is already done in Proposition
8.3.4
.
Hence suppose
m>1
.
Then there exists a Zariski cover
\{V_{1},\ldots,V_{m}\}
such that
\mathcal{E}
is trivial on
V_{i}
for every
i
.
Set

U_{1}:=V_{1},\;U_{2}:=V_{2}\cup\cdots\cup V_{m},\;\text{and }U_{12}:=U_{1}\cap U_{2}.

There is a naturally induced commutative diagram in
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathcal{P}_{U_{12}}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{U_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}M(U_{12}\rightarrow U_{2})(i)[2i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathcal{P}_{U_{1}}\rightarrow\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}M(U_{1}\rightarrow X)(i)[2i].}

The vertical morphisms are isomorphisms by Mayer-Vietoris since
\{U_{1},U_{2}\}
is a Zariski cover of
X
.
By induction the claim holds for
U_{1}
,
U_{2}
, and
U_{12}
.
This implies the upper horizontal morphism is an isomorphism so that the lower horizontal arrow is also an isomorphism.

As in
[
29
, Lemma 3.1]
, there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathcal{P}_{U_{1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathcal{P}_{U_{1}}\rightarrow\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathcal{P}_{U_{1}})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i}M(U_{1})(i)[2i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i}M(X)(i)[2i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i}M(U_{1}\rightarrow X)(i)[2i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i}M(U_{1})(i)[2i+1]}

The first and third vertical morphisms are isomorphisms so that the second vertical morphism is also an isomorphism.
∎

Remark 8.3.6
.

In the proof of
[
64
, Theorem 15.12]
an extra argument is needed to justify the induction argument,
see the proofs of
[
29
, Lemma 3.1, Theorem 3.2]
.

Theorem 8.3.7
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a rank
n
vector bundle over
X\in SmlSm/k
.
Then there is a canonical isomorphism

MTh(\mathcal{E})\simeq M(X)(n)[2n].

Démonstration.

Recall that we have the morphisms

\tau_{i}\colon M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}))\rightarrow\Lambda(i)[2i]\text{ and }\tau_{i}\colon M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}))\rightarrow\Lambda(i)[2i]

The pullback of the canonical line bundle over
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O})
by the closed immersion
u\colon\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O})
is isomorphic to the canonical line bundle over
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})
.
This means that there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda(1)[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda(1)[2].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rm id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{1}}

Thus for every integer
i\geq 0
, there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}))^{\otimes i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda(1)[2]^{\otimes i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}))^{\otimes i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda(1)[2]^{\otimes i}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{1}^{\otimes i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rm id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{1}^{\otimes i}}

Applying the above for
1\leq i\leq n-1
, we obtain the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n-1}M(X)(i)[2i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}M(X)(i)[2i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n-1}M(X)(i)[2i],}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta:=\sigma\circ M(u)\circ\sigma^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rm id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

where
p
is the canonical projection.
It follows that there is a canonical commutative diagram with horizontal split distinguished triangles and vertical isomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n-1}M(X)(i)[2i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}M(X)(i)[2i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(X)(n)[2n]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n-1}M(X)(i)[2i+1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces MTh(\mathcal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}))[1].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

The canonical composite morphism

\alpha\colon M(X)(n)[2n]\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}M(X)(i)[2i]\xrightarrow{\simeq}M(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}))\rightarrow MTh(\mathcal{E})

makes the above diagram into a morphism of distinguished triangles, where the first morphism is the canonical embedding.
By the five lemma,
\alpha
is an isomorphism.
∎

Corollary 8.3.8
.

Assume that
k
admits resolution of singularities.
For
X\in SmlSm
and
Z
a codimension
n
smooth subscheme of
\underline{X}
such that
Z
has strict normal crossing
with
\partial X
and is not contained in
\partial X
,
there is a canonical isomorphism

M((B_{Z}X,E)\rightarrow X)\rightarrow M(Z)(n)[2n].

Here
E
is the exceptional divisor of the blow-up
B_{Z}X
.

Démonstration.

Immediate from Theorems
7.5.4
and
8.3.7
.
∎

8.4.
Motives with rational coefficients

If
\Lambda
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra, then according to
[
64
, Theorem 14.30]
, there is an equivalence of Voevodsky’s triangulated categories of Nisnevich motives and étale motives

{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\simeq{\bf DM}_{\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).

In what follows, we argue as in
[
64
, Lecture 14]
and prove an analog in the log setting :
If
\mathbb{Q}\subset\Lambda
, then there is an equivalence of triangulated categories

{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\simeq{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).

Lemma 8.4.1
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a strict Nisnevich sheaf of
\mathbb{Q}
-modules with log transfers.
Then
\mathcal{F}
is a strict étale sheaf.

Démonstration.

Owing to Proposition
4.5.4
the strict étale topology is compatible with log transfers.
Thus
a_{s\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F}
is a strict étale sheaf of
\mathbb{Q}
-modules with log transfers.
We need to show there is a naturally induced isomorphism

p\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow a_{s\acute{e}t*}a_{s\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F}.

To that end, it suffices to show that

a_{s\acute{e}t}^{*}(\operatorname{Ker}p)=a_{s\acute{e}t}^{*}(\operatorname{Coker}p)=0.

Hence we are reduced to consider the case when
a_{s\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F}=0
.

We may assume that
\mathcal{F}
is nontrivial.
Then there exists a henselization
T
of an fs log scheme in
lSm/k
such that
\mathcal{F}(T)\neq 0
, i.e.,
\underline{T}
is a henselization of
\underline{Z}
for some
Z\in lSm/k
and
T=\underline{T}\times_{\underline{Z}}Z
.
Choose a nonzero element
c\in\mathcal{F}(T)
.
Since
a_{s\acute{e}t}^{*}\mathcal{F}=0
,
there exists a strict étale cover
f\colon X\rightarrow T
such that
f^{*}(c)\in\mathcal{F}(X)
is zero.
Owing to the implication
(a)\Rightarrow(c^{\prime\prime})
in the proof of
[
30
, Théorème IV.18.5.11]
there exists a factorization
g\colon Y\rightarrow T
of
f
,
where
g
is a finite étale morphism.
It follows that
g^{*}(c)=0\in\mathcal{F}(Y)
.

The transpose of the graph of
g\colon Y\rightarrow T
gives an elementary log correspondence
h\in\operatorname{lCor}(T,Y)
.
If
d
is the degree of
g
,
then the composition
g\circ h\in\operatorname{lCor}(T,T)
is equal to
d
times the identity correspondence.
Thus the composition

\mathcal{F}(T)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g^{*}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{F}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h^{*}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{F}(T)

is an isomorphism since
\mathcal{F}
is a sheaf of
\mathbb{Q}
-modules.
This implies
c=0
since
g^{*}(c)=0
, which is a contradiction.
∎

Remark 8.4.2
.

The proof of Lemma
8.4.1
does not work for the Kummer étale topology.
Indeed,
in this case,

Y
is not necessarily strict over
X
,
and hence the transpose of the graph of
g
is not an elementary log correspondence from
S
to
Y
.

Lemma 8.4.3
.

Suppose
\mathcal{F}
be a dividing Nisnevich sheaf of
\mathbb{Q}
-modules with log transfers.
Then
\mathcal{F}
is a dividing étale sheaf.
Thus if
\mathbb{Q}\subset\Lambda
, there is an equivalence of categories

\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda)\simeq\mathbf{Shv}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda).

Démonstration.

Owing to Lemma
8.4.1

\mathcal{F}
is a strict étale sheaf.
For any log modification
Y\rightarrow X
in
lSm/k
there is an isomorphism
\mathcal{F}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(Y)
since
\mathcal{F}
is a dividing sheaf.
To conclude, we apply Lemma
4.4.3
.
∎

Proposition 8.4.4
.

If
\mathbb{Q}\subset\Lambda
, there is an equivalence of triangulated categories

{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\simeq{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).

Démonstration.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be the set of objects of the form
\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(X)
for
X\in lSm/k
.
With respect to this set, we are entitled to the corresponding descent model structures and the equivalent categories

\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda))\text{ and }\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda)),

and hence a Quillen equivalence

\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda)).

Due to
[
44
, Theorem 3.3.20(1)]
the above is also a Quillen equivalence for the
\overline{\Box}
-local descent model structures,
and we are done by passing to the homotopy categories.
∎

8.5.
Locally constant log étale sheaves

If
\Lambda
is a torsion ring coprime to the exponential characteristic of
k
, then
[
64
, Theorem 9.35]
shows there is an equivalence of triangulated categories

\mathbf{D}^{-}(k_{\acute{e}t},\Lambda)\rightarrow{\bf DM}_{\acute{e}t}^{{\rm eff},-}(k,\Lambda).

The proof relies on Suslin’s rigidity theorem
[
64
, Theorem 7.20]
.
We refer to
[
20
]
for a way to remove boundedness.
In this subsection, we discuss the log étale version of the above equivalence.

Let
\eta\colon k_{\acute{e}t}\rightarrow lSm/k
denote the inclusion functor.
Then
\eta
is a continuous and cocontinuous functor with respect to the étale topology on
k_{\acute{e}t}
and the log étale topology on
lSm/k
.
Since
\eta
preserves finite limits,
\eta
is a morphism of sites due to
[
2
, IV.4.9.2]
.
As a consequence there exist adjunct functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Shv}(k_{\acute{e}t},\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Shv}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm log}(k,\Lambda),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{*}}

(8.5.1)

and
\eta_{\sharp}
is exact.

Definition 8.5.1
.

A log étale sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
lSm/k
is
locally constant
if the counit of the adjunction
\eta_{\sharp}\eta^{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
in (
) is an isomorphism.

Proposition 8.5.2
.

The functor
\eta_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{Shv}(k_{\acute{e}t},\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Shv}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm log}(k,\Lambda)
is fully faithful.

Démonstration.

The functor
\eta\colon k_{\acute{e}t}\rightarrow lSm/k
is fully faithful, so for every
X\in k_{\acute{e}t}
the unit

\Lambda(X)\rightarrow\eta^{*}\eta_{\sharp}\Lambda(X)

is an isomorphism.
Since
\eta_{\sharp}
and
\eta^{*}
commute with colimits, for every
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{Shv}(k_{\acute{e}t},\Lambda)
we have

\eta^{*}\eta_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}\simeq\eta^{*}\eta_{\sharp}\mathop{\rm colim}_{X\rightarrow\mathcal{F}}\Lambda(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{X\rightarrow\mathcal{F}}\eta^{*}\eta_{\sharp}\Lambda(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{X\rightarrow\mathcal{F}}\Lambda(X)\simeq\mathcal{F}.

It follows that
\eta_{\sharp}
is fully faithful.
∎

Proposition 8.5.3
.

Suppose that there is a cartesian square of locally noetherian fs log schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

with the following properties.

(i)

X
and
X^{\prime}
have trivial log structure, and
X^{\prime}\simeq\mathop{\rm lim}X_{i}
for some projective system of étale schemes of finite type over
X
such that every transition morphism
X_{j}\rightarrow X_{i}
is affine.

(ii)

f
is a morphism of finite type.

Then for every
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{D}(X_{l\acute{e}t},\Lambda)
there is a base change isomorphism

g^{*}Rf_{*}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\simeq}Rf_{*}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}\mathcal{F}.

Démonstration.

As in the proof of
[
20
, Theorem 1.1.14]
, this can be reduced to showing that for every log étale sheaf
\mathcal{F}
of
\Lambda
-modules on
X_{l\acute{e}t}
there is a base change isomorphism

g^{*}f_{*}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\simeq}f_{*}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}\mathcal{F}.

(8.5.2)

We can also replace
X^{\prime}
by
X_{i}
in the projective system and
Y^{\prime}
by
Y\times_{X}X_{i}
and then apply the limit.
Hence we reduce to the case when
g
is étale.
In this case, (
8.5.2
) can be proved directly.
∎

Proposition 8.5.4
.

Suppose
\Lambda
is a torsion ring coprime to the exponential characteristic of
k
.
Let
X
be an fs log scheme in
lSm/k
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
.
For every complex
\mathcal{F}
of locally constant sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules on
lSm/k
, the projection morphism
p\colon X\times\overline{\Box}\rightarrow X
induces an isomorphism of log étale cohomology groups

p^{*}\colon\mathbf{H}_{l\acute{e}t}^{q}(X,\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathbf{H}_{l\acute{e}t}^{q}(X\times\overline{\Box},\mathcal{F}).

Démonstration.

By applying Proposition
8.5.3
to the case when
g
is
\operatorname{Spec}(k^{s})\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(k)
, we reduce to the case when
k
is separably closed.
Then
X
and
X\times\overline{\Box}
have finite cohomological dimension by virtue of
[
70
, Theorem 7.2(2)]
.
Apply
[
95
, 5.7.9]
to obtain two strongly convergent hypercohomology spectral sequences

E_{pq}^{2}=H^{p}(X,a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}H^{q}(\mathcal{F}))\Rightarrow\mathbf{H}_{l\acute{e}t}^{p+q}(X,\mathcal{F}).

and

E_{pq}^{\prime 2}=H^{p}(X\times\overline{\Box},a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}H^{q}(\mathcal{F}))\Rightarrow\mathbf{H}_{l\acute{e}t}^{p+q}(X\times\overline{\Box},\mathcal{F}).

Since there is a naturally induced morphism of spectral sequences
E_{pq}\rightarrow E_{pq}^{\prime}
, we reduce to case when
\mathcal{F}
is a locally constant sheaf of
\Lambda
-modules on
X_{l\acute{e}t}
.

Since
k
has become separably closed,
\mathcal{F}
is a constant torsion sheaf.
We can further reduce to the case when
\mathcal{F}=\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
for some integer
n>0
invertible on
k
.
Thanks to
[
70
, Theorem 5.17]
there are isomorphisms

H_{k\acute{e}t}^{q}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{\simeq}H_{l\acute{e}t}^{q}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}),\;H_{k\acute{e}t}^{q}(X\times\overline{\Box},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{\simeq}H_{l\acute{e}t}^{q}(X\times\overline{\Box},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}).

For this reason, it suffices to show that there is an isomorphism of Kummer étale cohomology groups

p^{*}\colon H_{k\acute{e}t}^{q}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{\simeq}H_{k\acute{e}t}^{q}(X\times\overline{\Box},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}).

Owing to
[
47
, Corollary 7.5]
there are isomorphisms

\displaystyle H_{k\acute{e}t}^{q}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\simeq}H_{\acute{e}t}^{q}(X-\partial X,\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}),

\displaystyle H_{k\acute{e}t}^{q}(X\times\overline{\Box},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\simeq}H_{\acute{e}t}^{q}((X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{A}^{1},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}).

By
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-homotopy invariance of étale cohomology
[
2
, Corollaire XV.2.2]
there is an isomorphism

H_{\acute{e}t}^{q}(X-\partial X,\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{\simeq}H_{\acute{e}t}^{q}((X-\partial X)\times\mathbb{A}^{1},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}).

Combining these isomorphisms concludes the proof.
∎

Example 8.5.5
.

For every integer
n>1
coprime to the exponential characteristic of
k
,
the sheaf of
n
th roots of unity
\mu_{n}
is
not
strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant in the strict étale topology.
Indeed, for every integer
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

H_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(\overline{\Box},\mu_{n})\simeq H_{\acute{e}t}^{i}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mu_{n}).

In general, the latter group is non-isomorphic to
H_{\acute{e}t}^{i}(\operatorname{Spec}(k),\mu_{n})
.
In particular,
if
k
contains all
n
th roots of unity,
then
\mathbb{Z}/n
is
not
strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant in the strict étale topology.

Proposition 8.5.6
.

With
\Lambda
as above, every locally constant log étale sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
lSm/k
admits a unique log transfer structure.

Démonstration.

Owing to
[
64
, Lemma 6.11]

\mathcal{F}
has a unique transfer structure on
Sm/k
.
For every
X\in lSm/k
, apply Proposition
8.5.4
to have an isomorphism

\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\mathcal{F}(X-\partial X).

Thus for every finite log correspondence
V\in\operatorname{lCor}(X,Y)
,
where
Y\in lSm/k
,
the transfer map

(V-\partial V)^{*}\colon\mathcal{F}(X-\partial X)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(Y-\partial Y)

extends uniquely to a transfer map

V^{*}\colon\mathcal{F}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(Y).

∎

With
\Lambda
as above, as a consequence we have an adjunction

\eta_{\sharp}:\mathbf{Shv}(k_{\acute{e}t},\Lambda)\rightleftarrows\mathbf{Shv}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda):\eta^{*},

(8.5.3)

and
\eta_{\sharp}
is exact and fully faithful.
From (
) we deduce the canonical Quillen adjunction

\eta_{\sharp}:\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}(k_{\acute{e}t},\Lambda))\rightleftarrows\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda)):\eta^{*}

with respect to the descent model structure on the left side and the
\overline{\Box}
-local descent model structure on the right side.
On the corresponding homotopy categories, we obtain the canonical adjunction :

L\eta_{\sharp}:\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}(k_{\acute{e}t},\Lambda))\rightleftarrows{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda):R\eta^{*}

Since
\eta_{\sharp}
is exact,
L\eta_{\sharp}\simeq\eta_{\sharp}
.

Proposition 8.5.7
.

With
\Lambda
as above, the functor

\eta_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}(k_{\acute{e}t},\Lambda))\rightarrow{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)

is fully faithful.

Démonstration.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be an object of
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}(k_{\acute{e}t},\Lambda))
.
By Proposition
8.5.4
,
\eta_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}
is strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant,
and hence there is an isomorphism

R\eta^{*}\eta_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}\simeq\eta^{*}\eta_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}.

To conclude, we observe that the functor

\eta_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}(k_{l\acute{e}t},\Lambda))\rightarrow\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{Shv}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm ltr}(k,\Lambda))

is fully faithful since the functor
\eta_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{Shv}(k_{l\acute{e}t},\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{Shv}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm log}(k,\Lambda)
is fully faithful.
∎

Next, we state a rigidity conjecture which is a log analog of
[
64
, Theorem 9.35]
.

Conjecture 8.5.8
.

With
\Lambda
as above, the functor

\eta_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{D}(\mathbf{Shv}(k_{l\acute{e}t},\Lambda))\rightarrow{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)

is an equivalence.

Related to Conjecture
8.5.8
we have the following log analog of Suslin’s rigidity theorem
[
64
, Theorem 7.20]
.

Conjecture 8.5.9
.

Every strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant log étale sheaf
\mathcal{F}
equipped with log transfers and torsion coprime to the exponential characteristic of
k
is locally constant.

Remark 8.5.10
.

It is unclear whether one can expect that every
\overline{\Box}
-invariant log étale sheaf with log transfers is locally constant.
The sheaf
a_{l\acute{e}t}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{ltr}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})
is a potential counterexample, see Example
5.2.13
.

8.6.
Comparison with Voevodsky’s étale motives

In this section we state our expectations regarding an étale version of Theorem
8.2.17
.
Since Lemma
8.1.1
fails in the étale topology, the proof of Theorem
8.2.17
cannot be directly adopted to étale log motives.

Conjecture 8.6.1
.

There is an equivalence of triangulated categories

{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\simeq{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda).

Remark 8.6.2
.

Theorem
9.7.1
on Hodge sheaves provides some first evidence for Conjecture
8.6.1
in the form of the isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\Omega_{/k}^{j}[i])\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\Omega_{/k}^{j}[i]).

On the other hand,
since
\mathbb{Z}/n
is not strictly
\overline{\Box}
-invariant for the strict étale topology,
it is unclear whether the cohomology groups

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\mathbb{Z}/n[i])\text{ and }\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X),\mathbb{Z}/n[i])

are isomorphic, see Example
8.5.5
.

Conjecture 8.6.3
.

Suppose that
\Lambda=\mathbb{Z}/n
, where
n
is invertible in
k
.
Then there are equivalences of triangulated categories

{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\leftarrow{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t,prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow{\bf DM}_{\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda),

and

{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\leftarrow{\bf logDM}_{l\acute{e}t,prop}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)\rightarrow{\bf DM}_{\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda),

Remark 8.6.4
.

The Hodge sheaves
\Omega_{/k}^{j}\in{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
by Theorem
9.7.1
.
We expect the same holds for the de Rham-Witt sheaves
W_{m}\Omega_{/k}^{j}
.
Moreover,
one may guess that all the objects of
{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
can be built from
W_{m}\Omega_{/k}^{j}
and objects of
{\bf DM}_{\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
Due to the vanishing

W_{m}\Omega_{X/k}^{j}(X)\otimes\mathbb{Z}[1/p]=0,

where
p
denotes the characteristic of the base field
k
,
the de Rham-Witt sheaves may become trivial in
{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
under our assumption.
This indicates that
{\bf logDM}_{d\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
and
{\bf DM}_{\acute{e}t}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
are comprised of the same objects.

9.
Hodge sheaves

The main goal of this section is to show that Hodge cohomology for (log) schemes defined over a perfect field
k
is representable in the category of effective log motives
{\bf logDM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
This demonstrates in clear terms a fundamental difference between our category and Voevodsky’s category of motives
{\bf DM}^{\rm eff}(k,\Lambda)
.
Hodge cohomology is not representable in the latter category.

In the main step towards representability of Hodge cohomology,
see Theorem
9.6.4
,
we show that the presheaf
\Omega^{j}
on
lSm/k
given by the assignment

X\mapsto\Omega^{j}_{X/k}(X)

extends to a presheaf with log transfers on
lSm/k
in the sense of Section
2
.
Analogous to an argument in
[
14
, §6.2]
,
showing
(\mathbb{P}^{\bullet},\mathbb{P}^{\bullet-1})
-invariance is then fairly straightforward by using a suitable interpretation of the projective bundle formula.

The action of finite correspondences on Hodge sheaves (without log structure) is well-known and follows, for example, from the computations in
[
61
]
(at least in characteristic zero).
A more conceptual approach,
based on coherent duality and cohomology with support,
was developed by Chatzistamatiou and Rülling in
[
17
]
and
[
18
]
.
We will adapt the same script to the logarithmic setting.

9.1.
Logarithmic derivations and differentials

For every fs log scheme,
X
over
k
and
i\geq 0
the sheaf of logarithmic differentials on
X
is defined by

\Omega^{i}_{X/k}:=\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}_{X}&i=0\\
\wedge^{i}\Omega^{1}_{X/k}&i>0.\end{cases}

We shall often omit the subscript
/k
and write
\Omega_{X}^{i}
when no confusion seems likely to arise.
Recall that
\Omega^{1}_{X}
is the sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules generated by universal log derivations.
We refer to
[
76
, Chapter IV]
and Section
A.7.1
for details.

If
X\in SmlSm/k
, and
(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n})
are local coordinates,
we recall that
\Omega^{1}_{X}
is the free
\mathcal{O}_{\underline{X}}
-module generated by the symbols
dt_{1},\ldots dt_{n}
.
For the open immersion

j\colon\underline{X}-\partial X\to\underline{X},

the sheaf

\Omega^{1}_{X}=\Omega^{1}_{\underline{X}}(\log\partial X)

of differentials with log poles along
\partial X
is the subsheaf of
j_{*}j^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\underline{X}}
generated by

d\log t_{1},\ldots,d\log t_{r},dt_{r+1},\ldots dt_{n}.

Here, the ideal sheaf of
\partial X
in
\underline{X}
is locally defined by
I=(t_{1}\cdots t_{r})
.

A
quasi-coherent
sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on an fs log scheme
X
is a quasi-coherent sheaf on
\underline{X}
.
We similarly define
coherent
sheaves and
locally free
coherent sheaves on
X
.
For example,
\Omega_{X}^{i}
is a coherent sheaf for every fs log scheme
X
of finite type over
k
.
Indeed, if
i=1
this is
[
76
, Lemma IV. 1.2.16]
and
\Omega_{X}^{i}
is the
i
th exterior power of
\Omega_{X}^{1}
for
i>1
.
Moreover, if
X\in SmlSm/k
, then
\Omega_{X}^{i}
is locally free.

By generalizing
[
66
, Proposition III 3.7, Remark III 3.8]
to the Nisnevich case we obtain isomorphisms

H_{Zar}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})\simeq H_{sNis}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})\simeq H_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})

(9.1.1)

for every quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
X
and integer
i\geq 0
.

Lemma 9.1.1
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme.
Then every quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
X
is a log étale sheaf.

Démonstration.

The question is Zariski local on
X
.
Hence we may assume that there exists a presentation of the form

\bigoplus_{j\in J}\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\rightarrow 0.

Since the sheafification functor is exact, we only need to show that
\mathcal{O}_{X}
is a log étale sheaf.
This follows from
[
57
, §3.3]
.
∎

9.2.
(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})
-invariance of logarithmic differentials

For every
n\geq 1
,
let
(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})
be the log smooth log scheme
\mathbb{P}^{n}=\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}
with compactifying log structure induced by the open immersion

j\colon\mathbb{A}^{n}=\mathbb{P}^{n}-H\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{n},

where
H\simeq\mathbb{P}^{n-1}\subset\mathbb{P}^{n}
is a
k
-rational hyperplane.
If
X
is an fs log scheme in
lSm/k
,
we form the product
X\times(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})
and the projection

\pi\colon X\times(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})\to X.

Proposition 9.2.1
.

Suppose
X\in SmlSm/k
.
For every
i\geq 1
the naturally induced map

\pi^{*}\colon\Omega^{i}_{X}\to{R}\pi_{*}\Omega^{i}_{X\times(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})}

is an isomorphism in the bounded derived category of coherent sheaves
{\bf D}^{b}(\underline{X}_{\rm Zar})
.

Démonstration.

(Cf.
[
14
, §6.2]
). We begin by setting our notation.
Let
X=(\underline{X},\partial X)
,
where
\partial X
is a strict normal crossing divisor in
\underline{X}
.
Let
\iota_{H}\colon H\to\mathbb{P}^{n}
be the closed embedding of the rational hyperplane
H\simeq\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
in
\mathbb{P}^{n}
.
We need to prove there is an isomorphism

\pi^{*}\colon\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}}(\log\partial X)\to R\pi_{*}\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}(\log H+\partial X)

(9.2.1)

in
D^{b}(\underline{X}_{\rm Zar})
.
On the right hand side of (
),
we write
H+\partial X
for the strict normal crossing divisor on
\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}
given by
\underline{X}\times H+\partial X\times\mathbb{P}^{n}
.
We shall prove the claim by induction on the number of components of
\partial X
.

Let us write

\partial X=\partial X_{1}+\ldots+\partial X_{m},

where the
\partial X_{i}
’s are the irreducible components of
\partial X
.
When
\partial X=\emptyset
,
the statement follows from the projective bundle formula for the sheaves of logarithmic differential forms.
This should be well known, but for the lack of a reference, we give the argument.

Write
Res_{H}
for the residue map along
\underline{X}\times H\subset\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}
that fits into a short exact sequence (see e.g.,
[
34
, §2.3]
)

0\to\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}\to\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}(\log H)\xrightarrow{Res_{H}}\iota_{H,*}\Omega^{i-1}_{\underline{X}\times H}\to 0

(9.2.2)

of
\mathcal{O}_{\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}
-modules.
Pushing forward to
\underline{X}
we get a distinguished triangle

R\pi_{H,*}\Omega^{i-1}_{\underline{X}\times H}[-1]\xrightarrow{f}R\pi_{*}\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}\to R\pi_{*}\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}(\log H)\rightarrow R\pi_{H,*}\Omega^{i-1}_{\underline{X}\times H}

(9.2.3)

in
{\bf D}^{b}(\underline{X}_{\rm Zar})
,
where
\pi_{H}\colon\underline{X}\times H\to\underline{X}
is the projection.
Taking cup products with the powers of the first Chern class

\xi=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}(H))\in H_{Zar}^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{n},\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}})

of
H
determines an isomorphism in the derived category of bounded complexes of
\mathcal{O}_{\underline{X}}
-modules

\bigoplus_{0\leq j\leq n}\Omega^{i-j}_{\underline{X}}[-j]\xrightarrow{\simeq}R\pi_{*}\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}},\quad(a_{0},\ldots,a_{n})\mapsto\sum_{0\leq j\leq n}\pi^{*}(a_{j})\cup\xi^{j}.

(9.2.4)

This implies the projective bundle formula in Hodge cohomology for the trivial bundle.
The existence of this map is clear once one interprets
\xi
as a morphism
\xi\colon\mathbb{Z}[-1]\to\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{1}
in the derived category of coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-modules
{\bf D}^{b}(\underline{X}_{\rm Zar})
. The quasi-isomorphism is well-known to follow from a direct local computation.
See for example
[
1
, Lemma 3.2]
but the result is much older (see
[
39
]
).

Similarly, there is an isomorphism

\bigoplus_{0\leq j\leq n-1}\Omega^{i-1-j}_{\underline{X}}[-j]\xrightarrow{\simeq}R(\pi_{H})_{*}\Omega^{i-1}_{\underline{X}\times H},\quad(a_{0},\ldots,a_{n-1})\mapsto\sum_{0\leq j\leq n-1}\pi_{H}^{*}(a_{j})\cup\xi^{j}_{H},

(9.2.5)

where

\xi_{H}\in H^{1}(H,\Omega^{1}_{H/k})

is the restriction
\iota_{H}^{*}(\xi)
of
\xi
to
H
.
For every local section
a\in\Omega^{i-1-j}_{\underline{X}}
a direct local computation and the definition of the residue map imply

f(\pi_{H}^{*}(a)\cup\xi_{H}^{j})=\pi^{*}(a)\cup\xi^{j+1}.

(9.2.6)

Combining (
), (
), (
), and (
) we obtain the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{0\leq j\leq n-1}\Omega^{i-1-j}_{\underline{X}}[-j-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\pi_{H,*}\Omega^{i-1}_{\underline{X}\times H}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{0\leq j\leq n}\Omega^{i-j}_{\underline{X}}[-j]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\pi_{*}\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

(9.2.7)

where the left vertical morphism
\nu
is given by the (split) inclusion into the direct sum

\bigoplus_{0\leq j\leq n}\Omega^{i-j}_{\underline{X}}[-j]

and the horizontal morphisms are quasi-isomorphisms.
Using (
) and the equality
\pi^{*}(-)=\pi^{*}(-)\cup\xi^{0}
.
We deduce the isomorphism (
) by noting that the cokernel of
\nu
is exactly
\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}}
.

Next, we assume that
\partial X\neq\emptyset
, so that
m\geq 1
.
We proceed following the script of Sato
[
83
, §2]
(a sketch for relative differentials can be found in
[
14
, §6.2]
).

Write
I=\{1,\ldots,m\}
and for
1\leq a\leq m
, we define the disjoint union

\partial X^{[a]}=\coprod_{1\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{a}\leq m}\partial X_{i_{1}}\cap\ldots\cap\partial X_{i_{a}}.

Note that each
\partial X^{[a]}
is regular (since
\partial X
is a strict normal crossing divisor on
\underline{X}
),
and that there is a canonical morphism

\nu_{a}\colon\partial X^{[a]}\to\underline{X}

for each
a\geq 1
.
The log structure on
X
induces the compactifying log structure on each
\partial X^{[a]}
.
More explicitely, define for each
a\geq 1
the divisor
E_{a}
on
\partial X^{[a]}
by the formula

E_{a}=\coprod_{1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{a}\leq m}\big((\partial X_{i_{1}}\cap\ldots\cap\partial X_{i_{a}})\cap\sum_{j\notin\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{a}\}}\partial X_{j}\big).

This is a normal crossing divisor on
\partial X^{[a]}
(with strictly less than
m
components on each irreducible component of
\partial X^{[a]}
).
The
cdh
-covering of
\partial X
given by the
\partial X^{[a]}
’s gives rise to the exact sequence of sheaves

\displaystyle 0\to\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{X}}\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}}(\log\partial X)\to

\displaystyle\nu_{1,*}\Omega^{i-1}_{\partial X^{[1]}}\xrightarrow{\rho_{2}}\nu_{2,*}\Omega^{i-2}_{\partial X^{[2]}}\to\ldots

(9.2.8)

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\to\nu_{a,*}\Omega^{i-a}_{\partial X^{[a]}}\xrightarrow{\rho_{a}}\nu_{a+1,*}\Omega^{i-a-1}_{\partial X^{[a+1]}}\to\ldots,

on
\underline{X}
,
where each morphism
\rho_{a}
is induced by the alternating sum on residues, see e.g.,
[
83
, Proposition 2.2.1]
.

We may repeat the construction above for
X\times(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})
.
First, note that there is an evident canonical morphism of log schemes

X\times(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})\to\underline{X}\times(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1}).

Letting
\nu_{a}
denote the natural map
\partial X^{[a]}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}\to\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}
,
we get the following exact sequence of sheaves on
\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}
(using the above convention that
H
stands for
\underline{X}\times H
as a divisor on
\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}
)

\displaystyle 0\to\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}(\log H)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}}\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}(\log H+\partial X)\to\nu_{1,*}\Omega^{i-1}_{\partial X^{[1]}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}\xrightarrow{\rho_{2}}\ldots

(9.2.9)

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\to\nu_{a,*}\Omega^{i-a}_{\partial X^{[a]}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}\xrightarrow{\rho_{a}}\nu_{a+1,*}\Omega^{i-a-1}_{\partial X^{[a+1]}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}\to\ldots

Here, on each scheme
\partial X^{[a]}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}
we consider the compactifying log structure given by the inclusion of the complement of the normal crossing divisor

E^{[a]}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}+\partial X^{[a]}\times H
.
To simplify the notation,
we set

A_{a}:=\Omega^{i-a}_{\partial X^{[a]}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}\text{ and }B_{a}:=\Omega^{i-a}_{\partial{X}^{[a]}}.

Let
F_{A}
be the cokernel of
\varepsilon_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}
and let
F_{B}
be the cokernel of
\varepsilon_{X}
.
Then using (
9.2.9
) we obtain the spectral sequence

R^{a+q}\pi_{*}(\nu_{a+1,*}A_{a+1})\Rightarrow R^{a+q}\pi_{*}F_{A}.

Now, by induction, there is an isomorphism

\pi^{*}\colon\nu_{a+1,*}B_{a+1}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\pi_{*}(\nu_{a+1,*}A_{a+1}),

and the term
R^{a+q}\pi_{*}(\nu_{a+1,*}A_{a+1})
vanishes for
q\neq-a
, thus we have

F_{B}\xrightarrow{\simeq}R\pi_{*}F_{A}

using the above spectral sequence.
The proposition follows now from the 5-lemma and the case
\partial X=\emptyset
by comparing the two distinguished triangles

\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{X}}\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}}(\log\partial X)\to F_{B}\to\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}}[1]

and

R\pi_{*}\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}(\log H)\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}}R\pi_{*}\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}(\log H+\partial X)\to R\pi_{*}F_{A}\to R\pi_{*}\Omega^{i}_{\underline{X}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}(\log H)[1]

in the bounded derived category
{\bf D}^{b}(\underline{X}_{\rm Zar})
.
∎

Corollary 9.2.2
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme in
SmlSm/k
.
Then for every
i
,
j
, and
n
, there is an isomorphism

H_{Zar}^{i}(X\times(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1}),\Omega^{j})\simeq H_{Zar}^{i}(X,\Omega^{j}).

Démonstration.

This is immediate from Proposition
9.2.1
.
∎

Proposition 9.2.3
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme, and let
\mathcal{F}
be a quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
.
Then for every
i\geq 0
, there is an isomorphism

H_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\simeq}H_{k\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F}).

Démonstration.

The question is strict étale local on
X
.
Hence we may assume that
X
is affine.
Our claim follows now from Kato’s
[
57
, Proposition 6.5]
with a proof given by Niziol in
[
74
, Proposition 3.27]
.
∎

Proposition 9.2.4
.

Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
be a log modification in
lSm/k
, and let
\mathcal{F}
be a locally free coherent sheaf on
X
.
Then for every
i\geq 0
and
j\geq 0
there is an isomorphism

H_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\simeq}H_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(Y,f^{*}\mathcal{F}).

Démonstration.

The question is strict étale local on
X
, so we may assume that
X
has an fs chart
P
.
Owing to Proposition
A.11.5
there is a subdivision
\Sigma
of
\operatorname{Spec}(P)
such that there is an isomorphism

Y\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\xrightarrow{\simeq}X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}.

Let
p:X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\rightarrow X
and
q:Y\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\rightarrow X
be the projections.
There is a naturally induced commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\mathcal{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma},p^{*}\mathcal{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(Y,f^{*}\mathcal{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(Y\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma},q^{*}\mathcal{F}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

The horizontal morphisms are isomorphisms due to
[
86
, Remark 5.19]
,
which is a consequence of
[
56
, Theorem 11.3]
and the projection formula
[
84
, Tag 01E8]
.
Thus also the left vertical morphism is an isomorphism.
∎

Corollary 9.2.5
.

Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
be a log modification in
lSm/k
.
Then for every
i\geq 0
and
j\geq 0
there is an isomorphism

f^{*}\colon H_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\Omega^{j})\xrightarrow{\simeq}H_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(Y,\Omega^{j}).

Démonstration.

Since
f
is log étale we have
f^{*}\Omega_{X}^{j}\simeq\Omega_{Y}
.
Proposition
9.2.4
allows us to conclude the proof.
∎

Proposition 9.2.6
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme in
lSm/k
.
For every
i\geq 0
and
j\geq 0
there exist functorial isomorphisms

\begin{split}H_{Zar}^{i}(X,\Omega^{j})\simeq^{(1)}&H_{sNis}^{i}(X,\Omega^{j})\simeq^{(3)}H_{dNis}^{i}(X,\Omega^{j})\\
\simeq^{(1)}&H_{s\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\Omega^{j})\simeq^{(3)}H_{d\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\Omega^{j})\\
\simeq^{(2)}&H_{k\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\Omega^{j})\simeq^{(3)}H_{l\acute{e}t}^{i}(X,\Omega^{j})\end{split}

(9.2.10)

Démonstration.

The isomorphisms labeled (1) follow from the fact that the cohomology of coherent sheaves with respect to the Zariski, Nisnevich, and étale topologies coincides,
see (
).
Proposition
9.2.3
implies (2).
Finally,
the isomorphisms labeled (3) follow from Theorem
5.1.2
and Corollary
9.2.5
.

∎

Remark 9.2.7
.

The comparison of strict étale, Kummer étale, and log étale Hodge cohomology groups are also carried out in
[
86
, Theorems 6.13, 6.14]
.

Corollary 9.2.8
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme in
lSm/k
.
Suppose that
t
is one of following topologies :
Zar
,
sNis
,
s\acute{e}t
,
k\acute{e}t
,
dNis
,
d\acute{e}t
, and
l\acute{e}t
.
Then for every
i
,
j
, and
n
, there is an isomorphism

H_{t}^{i}(X\times(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1}),\Omega^{j})\simeq H_{t}^{i}(X,\Omega^{j}).

Démonstration.

Owing to Corollary
9.2.5
and Propositions
9.2.6
and
A.10.2
,
we reduce to the case when
X\in SmlSm/k
.
Then apply Corollary
9.2.2
.
∎

9.3.
A recollection on Grothendieck duality

We recall some material from Grothendieck-Verdier-Neeman duality theory for coherent sheaves
[
40
]
.
If
\underline{X}
is a separated scheme of finite type over
k
,
and
\pi_{\underline{X}}\colon\underline{X}\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
is the structural morphism, we have a pair of adjoint functors
(R\pi_{\underline{X},*},\pi_{\underline{X}}^{!})
between

{\bf D}^{b}(k)
and
{\bf D}^{b}(\underline{X}_{\rm Zar})={\bf D}^{b}(\underline{X})
(since we only consider the Zariski topology we often omit the subscript).
More generally,
if
f\colon\underline{X}\to\underline{Y}
is any morphism of schemes,
there is an adjoint pair

Rf_{*}\colon{\bf D}_{qc}(\underline{X})\leftrightarrows{\bf D}_{qc}(\underline{Y})\colon f^{\times}

(9.3.1)

between the derived categories of quasi-coherent sheaves on
\underline{X}
and
\underline{Y}
(see
[
72
, Reminder 3.1.1]
). When
f
is proper, the functor
f^{\times}
agrees with the familiar functor
f^{!}
classically considered
[
40
]
. When moreover
f
is proper and of finite Tor dimension (this is the case which is going to be interesting for us), the adjunction (
) restricts to an adjunction between the bounded derived categories of coherent sheaves (see
[
72
, Application 6.1.3]
)

Rf_{*}\colon{\bf D}^{b}(\underline{X})\leftrightarrows{\bf D}^{b}(\underline{Y})\colon f^{!}.

(9.3.2)

In this situation, it induces a natural transformation of functors

Rf_{*}R\mathscr{H}\!\textit{om}_{\underline{X}}(-,f^{!}(-))\xrightarrow{\simeq}R\mathscr{H}\!\textit{om}_{\underline{Y}}(Rf_{*}(-),-)

called the standard Grothendieck duality isomorphism (
\mathscr{H}\!\textit{om}
refers to the inner Hom of sheaves on
\underline{X}_{\rm Zar}
to avoid confusion with the inner Hom in the categories of motives). See
[
22
, (3.4.10), p.150]
.
We denote by
D_{\underline{X}}
the dualizing functor

D_{\underline{X}}=R\mathscr{H}\!\textit{om}_{\underline{X}}(-,\pi_{\underline{X}}^{!}(k))\colon{\bf D}^{b}(\underline{X})\to{\bf D}^{b}(\underline{X}).

For any separated morphism of finite type,
