Definition A.7.1
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of fine log schemes.
The sheaf of relative logarithmic differentials
\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}
is given by

\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}\colon=\Bigl(\Omega_{X/Y}\oplus(\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}M^{{\mathrm{gp}}}_{X})\Bigr)\big/\mathcal{K},

where
\mathcal{K}
is the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module generated by local sections of the following forms :

(1)

(d\alpha(a),0)-(0,\alpha(a)\otimes a)
with
a\in\mathcal{M}_{X}
;

(2)

(0,1\otimes a)
with
a\in\operatorname{Im}(f^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{X})
.

The universal derivation
(\partial,D)
is given by

\partial\colon\mathcal{O}_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\Omega_{X/Y}\rightarrow\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}

and

D\colon\mathcal{M}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}_{X}^{{\mathrm{gp}}}\rightarrow\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}.

Example A.7.2
.

Let
h:Q\rightarrow P
be a morphism of fine monoids.
For the induced morphism
f:\mathbb{A}_{P}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{Q}
we have

\Omega_{f}^{1}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{Coker}{h^{{\mathrm{gp}}}}.

The generators correspond to the logarithmic differentials
d\log\alpha(p)
for
p\in P
,
which are regular on the torus
\text{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[P^{{\mathrm{gp}}}])
, modulo those coming from
Q
.

Logarithmic differentials behave somewhat analogously to differentials.
Let

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\rightarrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\rightarrow}}Z

be a sequence of morphisms of fine log schemes.

(1)

There is a natural exact sequence

f^{*}\Omega_{Y/Z}^{1}\rightarrow\Omega_{X/Z}^{1}\rightarrow\Omega_{Z/Y}^{1}\rightarrow 0.

(2)

If
f
is log smooth,
then
\Omega_{f}^{1}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module of finite type, and there is an exact sequence

0\rightarrow f^{*}\Omega_{Y/Z}^{1}\rightarrow\Omega_{X/Z}^{1}\rightarrow\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}\rightarrow 0.

In particular, if
f
is log étale, then
\Omega_{f}^{1}=0
.

(3)

If
gf
is log smooth and the sequence in (2) is exact and splits locally, then
f
is log smooth.

We refer to
[
76
, Chapter IV]
for the proof.

Example A.7.3
.

Let
\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}
be the log scheme introduced in Definition
A.3.6
.
We have

\Omega_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}^{1}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}N.

Example A.7.4
.

The normal crossing variety

X:=\operatorname{Spec}(k[x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]/(x_{1}\cdots x_{d}))

defines a log structure of “semistable type” over the standard log point
(\operatorname{Spec}(k),\mathbb{N})
.
This is a special case of Example
A.2.7
with
X=\operatorname{Spec}(k[\mathbb{N}^{n}])
and
D=V(x_{1}\cdots x_{d})
,
corresponding to the submonoid of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
generated by
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times}
and
\{x_{1},\dots,x_{d}\}
.
We have that
\Omega_{X}^{1}
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module generated by the logarithmic differentials

\frac{dx_{1}}{x_{1}}=d\log x_{1},\cdots,\frac{dx_{d}}{x_{d}}=d\log x_{d},dx_{d+1},\cdots,dx_{n}

subject to the relation

d\log x_{1}+\cdots+d\log x_{d}=0.

A.8.
Exact morphisms

Definition A.8.1
.

A homomorphism
\theta:P\rightarrow Q
of monoids is
exact
if the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{\mathrm{gp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{\mathrm{gp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{\mathrm{gp}}}

is cartesian.

Definition A.8.2
.

A morphism
f:Y\rightarrow X
of log schemes is
exact
if for every point
y\in Y
the naturally induced homomorphism

\mathcal{M}_{X,f(x)}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{Y,y}

is exact.

Proposition A.8.3
.

Let
f
be an exact morphism of integral (resp. saturated) log schemes.
Then every pullback of
f
in the category of integral (resp. saturated) log schemes is also exact.

Démonstration.

See
[
76
, Proposition III.2.2.1(2)]
.
This reference states only the integral case.
However, the saturated case can be proved similarly, see
[
76
, Proposition I.4.2.1(6)]
.
∎

Proposition A.8.4
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a log étale morphism of fs log schemes.
Then
f
is Kummer if and only if
f
is exact.

Démonstration.

We refer to
[
47
, 1.6]
.
∎

A.9.
Kato-Nakayama spaces of fs log schemes

If
X
is a fine and saturated log scheme that is locally of finite type over
\text{Spec}({\mathbb{C}})
we are entitled to the log analytic space
X_{\text{an}}
.
The idea of Kato-Nakayama is to associate to
X_{\text{an}}
a topological space
X_{\log}
that embodies the log structure of
X
is a topological way by using points instead of sheaves of monoids.
By construction there exists a continuous map
\tau\colon X_{\log}\to X_{\text{an}}
that is proper and surjective.
Let
U\subseteq X_{\text{an}}
be the trivial locus of the log structure,
i.e.,
the largest open subset over which
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\text{an}}}^{\times}\hookrightarrow M_{X_{\text{an}}}
is an isomorphism.
Then the open immersion
i\colon U\to X_{\text{an}}
factors through
\tau
,
so that
X_{\log}
can be considered as a “relative compactification” of
i
.
We give a short review of Kato–Nakayama spaces and refer to
[
76
, Section V.1]
for more details.

Let
\mathrm{pt}^{\dagger}
be the log analytic point
\mathrm{pt}=\text{Spec}(\mathbb{C})_{\text{an}}
with monoid
M_{\mathrm{pt}}=\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times S^{1}
,
where each factor is given the monoidal multiplicative structure so that
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{{\mathrm{gp}}}=0
,
and map
\alpha\colon M_{\mathrm{pt}}\to\mathbb{C}
given by
(r,a)\mapsto ra
.
As a set,

X_{\log}:=\operatorname{Hom}(\mathrm{pt}^{\dagger},X_{\text{an}})

is comprised of morphisms of log analytic spaces from
\mathrm{pt}^{\dagger}
to
X_{\text{an}}
.
One identifies
X_{\log}
with the set of pairs
(x,\varphi)
,
where
x\in X_{\text{an}}
and
\varphi\colon M_{X_{\text{an}},x}^{{\mathrm{gp}}}\rightarrow S^{1}
is a homomorphism of abelian groups such that
\varphi(f)={f(x)}/{|f(x)|}\in S^{1}

for every
f\in\mathcal{O}_{X_{\text{an}},x}^{\times}
.
With this description,

\tau\colon X_{\log}\to X_{\text{an}}
is the projection to the first coordinate
(x,\varphi)\mapsto x
.
For every open subset
U\subseteq X_{\text{an}}
and section
m\in M_{X_{\text{an}}}(U)
,
we obtain a function
f_{U,m}\colon U_{\log}\rightarrow S^{1}
, defined by
(x,\varphi)\mapsto\varphi(m_{x})\in S^{1}
.
Here
U_{\log}
denotes the subset of
X_{\log}
of points
(x,\varphi)
with
x\in U
,
i.e.,
the Kato–Nakayama space of the log analytic space
(U,M_{X_{\text{an}}}|_{U})
.
The topology on
X_{\log}
is the coarsest topology that makes the projection
\tau\colon X_{\log}\to X_{\text{an}}
and all the functions
f_{U,m}\colon U_{\log}\rightarrow S^{1}
continuous.
For every
x\in X_{\text{an}}
,
the fiber
\tau^{-1}(x)
is a torsor under the space of homomorphisms of abelian groups
\operatorname{Hom}(M_{X_{\text{an}},x}^{{\mathrm{gp}}},S^{1})
.
If
X_{\text{an}}
is fs (or more generally if
M_{X_{\text{an}},x}
is torsion-free),
then
\tau^{-1}(x)
is non-canonically isomorphic to a real torus
(S^{1})^{r}
where
r
is the rank of the free abelian group
\overline{M}^{{\mathrm{gp}}}_{X_{\text{an}},x}=M^{{\mathrm{gp}}}_{X_{\text{an}},x}/\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
.

The construction of Kato-Nakayama spaces is functorial and compatible with strict morphisms of fine saturated log analytic spaces
X_{\text{an}}\rightarrow Y_{\text{an}}
,
i.e.,
there is a naturally induced cartesian square of topological spaces :

\textstyle{X_{\log}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{\log}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{\text{an}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{\text{an}}}

Example A.9.1
.

1.

For
\mathbb{A}^{1}
with its toric log structure
\mathbb{N}\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{N})=\mathbb{A}^{1}
we have
(\mathbb{A}^{1})_{\log}=\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times S^{1}
.
The projection map
\tau\colon\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times S^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
sends
(r,a)
to
ra
.
More generally,
let
P
be a fine monoid and
X
the affine toric scheme
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C}[P])
with its toric log structure.
Then

X_{\log}=\operatorname{Hom}(P,\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times S^{1})=\operatorname{Hom}(P,\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})\times\operatorname{Hom}(P,S^{1})

is locally compact with topology induced by the one on
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times S^{1}
.

2.

If
X
is smooth and
M_{D}
is the compactifying log structure coming from a normal crossing divisor
D\subseteq X
,
then
(X,M_{D})_{\log}
can be identified with the real oriented blow-up of
X
along
D
.
This is a “smooth manifold with corners”.

The last example suggests that
X_{\log}
should be thought of as the complement of an “open tubular neighborhood of the log structure.”
Our next observation is used in Proposition
7.4.4
.

Proposition A.9.2
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme log smooth over
\mathbb{C}
. Then
X_{\log}
is homotopy equivalent to
(X-\partial X)_{\text{an}}
.

Démonstration.

As observed in
[
58
, Remark 1.5.1]
,
X
is a manifold with boundary.
Our assertion follows readily from this.
∎

Example A.9.3
.

Let
D\subseteq X_{\text{an}}
be a simple normal crossings divisor.
Then
(X,M_{D})_{\log}
is a manifold with boundary ; its interior is
X_{\text{an}}-D

[
76
, Theorem V.1.3.1]
.
The inclusion
X_{\text{an}}-D\rightarrow(X_{\text{an}},M_{D})_{\rm log}
is a homotopy equivalence.

A.10.
Log blow-ups

In this section, we briefly recall Niziol’s work on log blow-ups
[
73
]
.
Recall that an ideal of a monoid
P
is a subset
I\subseteq P
such that
P+I\subseteq I
.

Definition A.10.1

(
[
73
, Definition 3.1]
)
.

A subsheaf
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{X}
of ideals of the monoid
\mathcal{M}_{X}
is coherent if locally for the étale topology of
X
there is a chart
P\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{X}
for the log structure and a finitely generated ideal
I\subseteq P
of the monoid
P
, such that
\mathcal{I}
coincides with the subsheaf of
\mathcal{M}_{X}
generated by the image of
I
.

A subsheaf
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{X}
of ideals is invertible if a single element can locally generate it, or equivalently if it is induced locally by a principal ideal of a chart
P\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{X}
.
If
\mathcal{I}
is coherent, then it is invertible if and only if
\mathcal{I}_{x}\subseteq M_{X,x}
is a principal ideal for every
x\in X
.
For a coherent sheaf of ideals
\mathcal{I}
of the log structure on
X
,
the log blow-up of
X
along
\mathcal{I}
is a log scheme
(X_{\mathcal{I}},M_{\mathcal{I}})
equipped with a map
f_{\mathcal{I}}\colon X_{\mathcal{I}}\rightarrow X
satisfying a universal property :
the ideal generated by
f_{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}\mathcal{I}
in
M_{\mathcal{I}}
is invertible and every morphism of log schemes
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
such that the ideal generated by
f^{-1}\mathcal{I}
on
Y
is invertible factors
uniquely through
f_{\mathcal{I}}
.
Locally on
X
, for a chart
P\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{X}
and a finitely generated ideal
I\subseteq P
inducing
\mathcal{I}
,
the log blow-up is given by the pullback along
X\rightarrow\text{Spec}(k[P])
of the blow-up
\text{Proj}(\bigoplus_{n}I^{n})\rightarrow\text{Spec}(k[P])
.

The following result due to Niziol strengthens Kato’s work
[
56
, (10.4)]
.

Proposition A.10.2
.

For
X\in lSm/k
, there is a log blow-up
Y\rightarrow X
such that
Y\in SmlSm/k
.

Démonstration.

This is a particular case of
[
73
, Theorem 5.10]
.
∎

Remark A.10.3
.

When forming triangulated categories of logarithmic motives, for every log blow-ups
Y\rightarrow X
in
lSm/k
, we invert the naturally induced morphism of motives

M(Y)\rightarrow M(X).

Since every fs log scheme
X
admits a log blow-up equipped with a Zariski log structure, see
[
73
, Theorem 5.4]
,
we may assume that the log structure of
X
— and thus of every log blow-up
[
73
, Proposition 4.5]
— is Zariski.

Remark A.10.4
.

Log blow-ups performed in the category of fine log schemes or the category of fs log schemes differ by a “normalization” or “saturation” on the level of monoids.
See
[
73
, Section 4]
for details.

Remark A.10.5
.

Let
X
be a (normal) toric variety equipped with its natural log structure.
Every (saturated) log blow-up of
X
turns out to be a “toric blow-up” given by subdivisions of the fan
\Sigma
of
X
.
Indeed,
by
[
73
, Proposition 4.3]
,
the log blow-up along a coherent sheaf of ideals
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{X}
is the same as the normalization of the blow-up of
X
along the coherent sheaf of ideals

\langle\alpha(\mathcal{I})\rangle\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{X}
for
\alpha\colon\mathcal{M}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
When
X=\text{Spec}(k[P])
,
the global sections of this image form a homogeneous ideal of
k[P]
,
and the blow-up along this ideal affords a description via a subdivision of the corresponding cone.
Patching together the subdivisions on the affine pieces yields a subdivision of
\Sigma
realizing the log blow-up.

Example A.10.6
.

Set
X=\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}\oplus\mathbb{N}}
, and let
K
be the zero ideal of
\mathbb{N}\oplus\mathbb{N}
.
Now let
\mathcal{K}
be the coherent sheaf of ideals in
\mathcal{M}_{X}
associated with
K
,
and let
P_{1}
(resp.
P_{2}
) be the submonoid of
\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}
generated by
(-1,1)
and
(1,0)
(resp.
(1,-1)
and
(0,1)
).
From the construction of log blow-ups in
[
76
, Lemma II.1.7.3, Remark II.1.7.4]
,
we see that the log blow-up
Y
of
X
along
\mathcal{K}
is the gluing of
\mathbb{A}_{P_{1}}
and
\mathbb{A}_{P_{2}}
along
\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}}
.
On the underlying schemes, we note that
\underline{Y}
is the usual blow-up of
\underline{X}=\mathbb{A}^{2}_{k}
along the origin.

A.11.
Log étale monomorphisms

A morphism of schemes is a closed (resp. an open) immersion if and only if it is a proper (resp. an étale) monomorphism by
[
30
, Corollaire IV.18.12.6, Théorème IV.17.9.1]
.
Thus a morphism of schemes is an isomorphism if and only if it is a surjective proper étale monomorphism.

In contrast to this,
a proper (resp. a log étale) monomorphism need not be a closed (resp. an open) immersion,
and a surjective proper log étale monomorphism does not need to be an isomorphism.
However, on the level of log motives, we will turn surjective proper log étale monomorphisms into isomorphisms.
For this purpose, we shall study log étale monomorphisms.

Example A.11.1
.

By
[
76
, Proposition III.2.6.3(3)]
, log blow-ups are universally surjective proper monomorphisms.
Zariski locally on
X
and
Y
,

f
has a chart
P\rightarrow Q
such that
P^{\rm gp}\rightarrow Q^{\rm gp}
is an isomorphism, which can be checked by appealing to
[
76
, Lemma II.1.7.3, Remark II.1.7.4]
.
Thus
f
is log étale by
[
76
, Theorem IV.3.3.1]
. In conclusion,
f
is a universally surjective proper log étale monomorphism.

Lemma A.11.2

(
[
77
]
)
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow S
be a log étale morphism of fs log schemes, and let
i:S\rightarrow X
be a section of
f
. Then
i
is an open immersion.

Démonstration.

According to the assumptions, we can form the commutative diagram of fs log schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{S}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}

where

(i)

a
denotes the diagonal morphism.

(ii)

p_{2}
denotes the projection on the second factor.

(iii)

Each square is cartesian.

It suffices to show that
a:X\rightarrow X\times_{S}X
is an open immersion.
Since the diagonal morphism

\underline{X}\rightarrow\underline{X}\times_{\underline{S}}\underline{X}

is radicial, we reduce to showing that
a
is strict étale by
[
30
, Théorème IV.17.9.1]
.
As in
[
30
, Corollaire IV.17.3.5]
, the morphism
a
is log étale.
Thus it remains to show
a
is strict.
We divide the proof into three steps.

(I)
Locality on
S
.

Let
g:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
be a strict étale cover of fs log schemes, and set
X^{\prime}=X\times_{S}S^{\prime}
.
Then the commutative cartesian diagram of fs log schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\times_{S^{\prime}}X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{S}X}

shows the question is strict étale local on
S
.

(II)
Locality on
X
.
Let
h:X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
be a strict étale cover of fs log schemes.
Then we have the commutative diagram of fs log schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\times_{S}X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{S}X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{S}X,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}

where

(i)

The lower square is cartesian.

(ii)

a^{\prime\prime}
denotes the diagonal morphism.

(iii)

h^{\prime}:={\rm id}\times h
and
h^{\prime\prime}:=h\times{\rm id}
.

Assume that
a^{\prime\prime}
is a strict morphism.
Then
a^{\prime}
is also strict since
h^{\prime\prime}
is strict, while
a
is also strict since
h
is a strict étale cover.
Conversely, if
a
is a strict morphism, then
a^{\prime}
is strict and therefore also
a^{\prime\prime}
is strict.
Thus the question is strict étale local on
X
.

(III)
Final step of the proof.
By
[
76
, Theorem IV.3.3.1]
, Zariski locally on
X
and
S
we have an fs chart
\theta:P\rightarrow Q
of
f
such that the following holds.

(i)

\theta
is injective, and the cokernel of
\theta^{\rm gp}
is finite,

(ii)

The induced morphism
X\rightarrow S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}
is strict étale.

Hence by parts (I) and (II),
we may assume that
(X,S)=(\mathbb{A}_{Q},\mathbb{A}_{P})
.
Then it suffices to show that the diagonal homomorphism

\mathbb{A}_{Q}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{Q}\oplus_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}

is strict.
In effect, it suffices to show
a\oplus(-a)\in(Q\oplus_{P}Q)^{*}
for every
a\in Q
.
Choose
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
such that
na\in P^{\rm gp}
.
Because the summation homomorphism

P^{\rm gp}\oplus_{P^{\rm gp}}P^{\rm gp}\rightarrow P^{\rm gp}

is an isomorphism,
the two elements
(na)\oplus 0
and
0\oplus(na)
of
Q\oplus_{P}Q
are equal.
Thus we have
n(a\oplus(-a))=0
.
Since
Q\oplus_{P}Q
is an fs monoid, it follows that
a\oplus(-a)\in Q\oplus_{P}Q
.
This means that
a\oplus(-a)\in(Q\oplus_{P}Q)^{*}
since
n(a\oplus(-a))=0
.
∎

Lemma A.11.3

(
[
77
]
)
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow S
be a log étale monomorphism of fs log schemes, and let
P
be an fs chart of
S
.
Then Zariski locally on
X
, there exists a chart
\theta:P\rightarrow Q
of
f
with the following properties.

(i)

The induced morphism
X\rightarrow S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}
is an open immersion,

(ii)

\theta^{\rm gp}:P^{\rm gp}\rightarrow Q^{\rm gp}
is an isomorphism.

Démonstration.

The question is Zariski local on
X
.
Let
x
be a point on
X
.
By appealing to
[
76
, Theorem IV.3.3.1]
, we may assume there exists an fs chart
\theta^{\prime}:P\rightarrow Q^{\prime}
with the following properties.

(i)’

The induced morphism
X\rightarrow S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q^{\prime}}
is strict étale.

(ii)’

\theta^{\prime}
is injective, and the cokernel of
\theta^{\prime\rm gp}
is finite of order invertibile on
\mathcal{O}_{S}
.

(iii)’

The chart
Q^{\prime}
of
X
is exact at
x
.

Note that
Q:=P^{\rm gp}\cap Q^{\prime}
is an fs monoid by Gordon’s lemma
[
76
, Theorem I.2.3.19]
.
Then the induced homomorphism
P^{\rm gp}\rightarrow Q^{\rm gp}
is an isomorphism, i.e., we have (ii).
Thus the projection

S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}\rightarrow S

is a log étale monomorphism.
Consider
f
as the composition
X\rightarrow S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}\rightarrow S
.
Then the first arrow is a monomorphism since
f
is a monomorphism.
According to
[
76
, Remark IV.3.1.2]
,
the first arrow is also log étale.
Since the inclusion
Q\rightarrow Q^{\prime}
is Kummer, see Definition
A.1.5
,
replacing
X\rightarrow S
by
X\rightarrow S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}
,
we may also assume that
\theta^{\prime}
is Kummer.

Let
X_{1}
(resp.
S_{1}
) be the strict closed subscheme of
X
(resp.
S
) whose underlying scheme is
\{x\}
(resp.
\{f(x)\}
) with the reduced scheme structure.
Since
f
is a monomorphism, the induced pullback morphism
X_{1}\rightarrow S_{1}
is also a monomorphism.
Since this is a morphism of log points, it can be written as

\operatorname{Spec}(Q^{\prime}\oplus k^{\prime\prime*}\rightarrow k^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P\oplus k^{\prime*}\rightarrow k^{\prime}),

where
\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime\prime})=\underline{X_{1}}
and
\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime})=\underline{S_{1}}
.

Suppose for contradiction that
\theta^{\prime}
is not an isomorphism.
Then the fiber product
X_{1}\times_{S_{1}}X_{1}
has more than one point,
and hence the diagonal
X_{1}\rightarrow X_{1}\times_{S_{1}}X_{1}
is not an isomorphism.
This contradicts the fact that
X_{1}\rightarrow S_{1}
is a monomorphism.
Thus
\theta^{\prime}
is an isomorphism.

Then by (i)’,

f
is strict étale.
Since
f
is a monomorphism,

f
is an open immersion by
[
30
, Théorème IV.17.9.1]
.
Thus we have (i).
∎

Lemma A.11.4
.

Suppose
P
is an fs monoid such that
P^{\mathrm{gp}}
is torsion free and
u:\Sigma\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
is subdivision of fans.
Then the morphism
\mathbb{A}_{u}:\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{P}
is universally surjective.

Démonstration.

Let
p:S\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{P}
be a morphism of fs log schemes, and set
X:=S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}
.
We need to show that the projection morphism
f:X\rightarrow S
is surjective.
This question is Zariski local on
S
, so we may assume that
p
has a factorization

S\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{Q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\theta}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{A}_{P},

where the first morphism is strict and
\theta:P\rightarrow Q
is a homomorphism of fs monoids.
The projection

\Sigma\times_{\operatorname{Spec}(P)}\operatorname{Spec}(Q)\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(Q)

is also a subdivision of fans due to Lemma
A.3.11
.
Hence, by replacing
P
with
Q
, we may assume that
p
is strict.
In this case
\underline{f}
is a pullback of
\underline{\mathbb{A}_{u}}
by Lemma
A.5.6
.
Since
\underline{\mathbb{A}_{u}}
is surjective, and any surjective morphism in the category of schemes is universally surjective,
\underline{f}
is also surjective.
∎

Proposition A.11.5

(cf.
[
77
]
)
.

Let
(f_{i}:X_{i}\rightarrow S)_{i\in I}
be a finite family of log étale monomorphisms where
X_{i}
is quasi-compact for every
i\in I
.
Suppose that there is a strict morphism
S\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}
where
\Sigma
is a fan.
Then there exists a subdivision of fans

u:\Sigma^{\prime}\rightarrow\Sigma

such that for each
i\in I
,
the pullback

f_{i}^{\prime}\colon X_{i}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\rightarrow S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}

is an open immersion.
If
f_{i}
is surjective and proper, then
f_{i}^{\prime}
is an isomorphism.

Démonstration.

Let us start with a log étale monomorphism
f:X\rightarrow S
, where
X
is quasi-compact.
Suppose that
\Sigma
is in a lattice
N
.
Choose fs submonoids
\{P_{j}\}_{j\in J}
of
\mathbb{Z}^{r}
such that the dual monoids
P_{j}^{\vee}
correspond to the maximal cones of
\Sigma
.
Then

S_{j}:=X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{A}_{P_{j}}

affords an fs chart
P_{j}
, and
\{S_{j}\rightarrow S\}_{j\in J}
is a finite Zariski cover.
By Lemma
A.11.3
, there exists a finite Zariski cover
\{U_{jk}\rightarrow S_{j}\}_{k\in K_{j}}
such that for each
k
,
there is a homomorphism
\theta_{k}:P_{j}\rightarrow Q_{jk}
of fs monoids with the properties that
\theta_{k}^{\rm gp}
is an isomorphism and the induced morphism

U_{jk}\rightarrow S_{j}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P_{j}}}\mathbb{A}_{Q_{jk}}

is an open immersion.

Lemma
A.3.16
yields a fan
\Theta_{jk}
in
N
such that
Q_{jk}^{\vee}\in\Theta_{jk}
and
\lvert\Theta_{jk}\rvert=N
.
Let
\Sigma_{0}
be the fan in
N
whose only cone is
N
,
and let
\Sigma^{\prime}
be the fiber product of
\Sigma
and all
\Theta_{jk}
over
\Sigma_{0}
.
Owing to Lemma
A.3.11
we get that
\Sigma^{\prime}
is a subdivision of
\Sigma
.
Moreover, for each cone
\sigma
of
\Sigma^{\prime}
there is
j\in J
and
k\in K_{j}
such that
\sigma\subset Q_{jk}^{\vee}
.
This implies that the induced morphism

u_{j}^{\prime}:U_{jk}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\rightarrow S_{j}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}

is an open immersion.
In particular,
u_{j}^{\prime}
is strict étale.
Thus the pullback

f^{\prime}:X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\rightarrow S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}

is strict étale.
Since
f^{\prime}
is a monomorphism, it follows that
f^{\prime}
is an open immersion by
[
30
, Théorème IV.17.9.1]
.

Assuming that
f
is surjective and proper, we consider the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

(A.11.1)

As a result of Lemma
A.11.4
, we get that
\mathbb{A}_{u}
is universally surjective, and hence
g
, and
g^{\prime}
are universally surjective.
Thus
(fg^{\prime})^{-1}(s)
is nonempty for every point
s\in S
.
The induced open immersion
(fg^{\prime})^{-1}(s)\rightarrow g^{-1}(s)
is proper,
and it is an isomorphism according to Zariski’s main theorem since
g^{-1}(s)
is connected.
Thus
f^{\prime}
is surjective, and hence an isomorphism.

In the general case,
choose a subdivision of fans
\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Sigma
for each
i\in I
such that the pullback

X_{i}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}}\rightarrow S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}}

is an open immersion.
Letting
\Sigma^{\prime}
be the fiber product
\Sigma_{i_{1}}^{\prime}\times_{\Sigma}\cdots\times_{\Sigma}\Sigma_{i_{r}}^{\prime}

where
I=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{r}\}
,
we have that the pullback

X_{i}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\rightarrow S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}

is an open immersion for any
i
.
To conclude we note that the induced morphism
\Sigma^{\prime}\rightarrow\Sigma
is a subdivision of fans owing to Lemma
A.3.11
.
∎

Proposition A.11.6
.

Suppose
f:X\rightarrow S
is a surjective proper log étale monomorphism of fs log schemes.
Then any pullback along
f
is a surjective proper log étale monomorphism.
In particular,
f
is universally surjective.

Démonstration.

The question is Zariski local on
S
, so we may assume that
S
is quasi-compact and has an fs chart
P
.
Then
X
is quasi-compact since
f
is proper.
By Proposition
A.11.5
, there exists a subdivision of fans
u:M\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
such that the morphism
f^{\prime}
in (
A.11.1
) is an isomorphism.
In the proof of Proposition
A.11.5
, we have checked that the morphism
g
in (
A.11.1
) is universally surjective.
Thus
f
is universally surjective.
To conclude, we use that a pullback along a proper log étale monomorphism is a proper log étale monomorphism.
∎

Definition A.11.7
.

Recall from
[
52
]
that a morphism
f:X\rightarrow S
of fs log schemes is called a
log modification
if Zariski locally on
S
,
there exists a log blow-up
g:Y\rightarrow X
such that the composition
fg
is also a log blow-up.

3
3

3

In
[
52
]
, the condition is étale local on
S
. Since we are only interested in fs log schemes with Zariski log structures, we may restrict to the Zariski case.

Example A.11.8
.

As observed in
[
52
]
, a morphism of fs log schemes induced by a subdivision of toric fans in the sense of
[
76
, §I.1.9]
is an example of a log modification,
see also
[
75
, pp. 39-40]
.

Proposition A.11.9
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow S
be a morphism of fs log schemes.
Then
f
is a surjective proper log étale monomorphism if and only if
f
is a log modification.

Démonstration.

Assume that
f
is a log modification.
The question is Zariski local on
S
, so we may assume that there exists a log blow-up
g\colon Y\rightarrow X
such that
fg
is also a log blow-up.
Since
g
and
fg
are surjective proper log étale monomorphisms as observed in Example
A.11.1
,
f
is a surjective proper log étale morphism.
Consider the induced commutative diagram of fs log schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times_{X}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times_{S}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{S}X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}

Since
g
and
fg
are monomorphisms, the diagonal morphisms
Y\rightarrow Y\times_{S}Y
and
Y\rightarrow Y\times_{X}Y
are isomorphisms.
Thus the upper horizontal morphism is an isomorphism.
The right vertical morphism is surjective since
g
is universally surjective.
Thus
\Delta
is surjective.
By Lemma
A.11.2
,
\Delta
is an open immersion, so
\Delta
is an isomorphism.
Thus
f
is a monomorphism, and hence a surjective proper log étale monomorphism.

Conversely, assume that
f
is a surjective proper log étale monomorphism.
The question is Zariski local on
S
, so we may assume that
S
is quasi-compact and has a neat fs chart
P
.
Then
X
is quasi-compact since
f
is proper.
By Proposition
A.11.5
, there exists a subdivision of fans
\Sigma\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
such that the pullback

X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\rightarrow S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}

is an isomorphism.
As observed in Example
A.11.8
, there exists a log blow-up
T\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}
such that the composition
T\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{P}
is also a log blow-up.
Then the morphisms

X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}T\rightarrow X\text{ and }X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}T\rightarrow S

are log blow-ups, so
f
is a log modification.
∎

Proposition A.11.10
.

Any composition of log modifications is a log modification.
Any pullback of a log modification is a log modification.

Démonstration.

See
[
52
, Lemma 3.15(2),(3)]
.
Alternatively, one can use Proposition
A.11.9
.
∎

Lemma A.11.11
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow S
and
g:Y\rightarrow S
be morphisms of fs log schemes.
Suppose that
g
is a log modification.
Then there are at most one morphism
h:X\rightarrow Y
such that
gh=f
.
Moreover, if
f
is a log modification, then
h
is a log modification.

Démonstration.

Suppose that such an
h
exists.
The graph morphism
u:X\rightarrow X\times_{S}Y
is a pullback of the diagonal morphism
d:Y\rightarrow Y\times_{S}Y
, and
d
is an isomorphism since
Y
is a log modification.
Thus
u
is an isomorphism.
This means that
h
is isomorphic to the projection
X\times_{S}Y\rightarrow Y
.
If
f
is a log modification, then the projection is also a modification by Proposition
A.11.10
.
∎

Definition A.11.12
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme.
We denote by
X_{div}
the category of log modifications over
X
, and we denote by
X_{div}^{Sm}
the full subcategory of
X_{div}
consisting of log modifications
Y\rightarrow X
such that
Y\in SmlSm/k
.

Proposition A.11.13
.

Every exact log étale monomorphism
f:X\rightarrow S
of fs log schemes is an open immersion.

Démonstration.

The question is Zariski local on
S
and
X
, so we may assume that
S
has s neat fs chart
P
at
s\in S
.
Moreover, due to Lemma
A.11.3
,
we may assume there exists a chart
\theta:P\rightarrow Q
of
f
such that
X\rightarrow S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}
is an open immersion and

\theta^{\rm gp}:P^{\rm gp}\rightarrow Q^{\rm gp}
is an isomorphism.

Let
x
be a point of
X
mapping to
s
.
Then
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X,x}\simeq Q/F
for some face
F
of
Q
.
By the assumption that
f
is exact, we see that

P\simeq\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{S,s}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X,x}\simeq Q/F

is exact.
Thus
P\rightarrow Q_{F}
is exact owing to
[
76
, Proposition I.4.2.1(3)]
, i.e.,

P\simeq P^{\rm gp}\times_{Q^{\rm gp}}Q_{F}.

Since
\theta^{\rm gp}
is an isomorphism, this implies
P\simeq Q_{F}
.
This shows that the morphism
f
is strict.
Thus
f
is a strict étale monomorphism, and hence an isomorphism according to
[
30
, Théorème IV.17.9.1]
.
∎

Proposition A.11.14
.

Suppose
X
is an fs log scheme.
Then
X_{div}
is a filtered category.

Démonstration.

For any
Y,Y^{\prime}\in X_{div}
, we have that
Y\times_{X}X^{\prime}\in X_{div}
owing to Proposition
A.11.10
.
Thus
X_{div}
is connected.
Owing to Lemma
A.11.11
there exists at most one morphism
Y\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
of fs log schemes over
X
.
Thus
X_{div}
is filtered.
∎

Proposition A.11.15
.

For
X\in lSm/k
, the category
X_{div}^{Sm}
is cofinal in
X_{div}
.

Démonstration.

Follows from Proposition
A.10.2
.
∎

Proposition A.11.16
.

For
X\in lSm/k
, the category
X_{div}^{Sm}
is a filtered category.

Démonstration.

Follows from Propositions
A.11.14
and
A.11.15
.
∎

A.12.
Small Kummer étale and small log étale sites

In this subsection we review the small Kummer étale site
X_{k\acute{e}t}
and the small log étale site
X_{l\acute{e}t}
for a saturated log scheme
X
.
We also prove some basic properties.

Definition A.12.1
.

A saturated log scheme
X
is
log strictly local
(resp. a
log geometric point
) if
\underline{X}
is the spectrum of a strictly henselian local ring
(resp. a separably closed field),
and for every integer
n>0
prime to the characteristic of
k
the multiplication by
n
morphism on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}(X)
is bijective.

A saturated log scheme
X
is
log strictly henselian
if
X
is a finite disjoint union of log strictly local schemes.

Definition A.12.2
.

For a saturated log scheme
X
, let
X_{k\acute{e}t}

(resp.
X_{l\acute{e}t}
) denote the category of Kummer étale (resp. log étale) morphisms of saturated log schemes whose target is
X
.
The topology on
X_{k\acute{e}t}
(resp.
X_{l\acute{e}t}
) is generated by the pretopology with coverings comprised of Kummer étale (resp. log étale) morphisms

\{U_{i}\rightarrow U\}_{i\in I}
for which
\amalg_{i\in I}U_{i}\rightarrow U
is universally surjective in the category of
saturated
log schemes.

Remark A.12.3
.

Suppose that
X
is an fs log scheme.
For
X_{k\acute{e}t}
, a family of morphisms
\{U_{i}\rightarrow U\}_{i\in I}
is a covering if and only if
\amalg_{i\in I}U_{i}\rightarrow U
is surjective, see e.g.,
[
70
, Proposition 3.2]
.
In particular, a family of morphisms
\{U_{i}\rightarrow U\}_{i\in I}
is a covering if and only if
\amalg_{i\in I}U_{i}\rightarrow U
is universally surjective in the category of
fs
log schemes.

However, we need to take the category of
saturated
log schemes in the definition of coverings in
X_{l\acute{e}t}
.
For example,
in the proof
[
70
, Proposition 3.14(1)]
,
a surjectivity of
(\amalg_{i\in I}U_{i})\times_{U}V\rightarrow V
is used for some saturated but non fs log scheme over
U
.

Lemma A.12.4
.

Let
X
be a log strictly henselian scheme.
Then every saturated log scheme
Y
that is finite and strict over
X
is also strictly henselian.

Démonstration.

Since
\underline{X}
is strictly henselian,
\underline{Y}
is strictly henselian too.
Let
y
be a closed point of
Y
with image
x
in
X
.
Then
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,y}\simeq\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X,x}
since
Y
is strict over
X
.
Thus for every integer
n>0
prime to the characteristic of
k
the multiplication by
n
on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,y}
is bijective.
∎

For an integral monoid
P
and
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, we let
P^{1/n}
denote the submonoid of
P^{\rm gp}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
given by

P^{1/n}:=\{p\in P^{\rm gp}\otimes\mathbb{Q}:np\in P\}.

(A.12.1)

Lemma A.12.5
.

Let
S
be an fs log scheme with an fs chart
P
whose underlying scheme
\underline{S}
is strictly local.
Then every Kummer étale cover
f:X\rightarrow S
admits a refinement of the form of a projection

S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{P^{1/n}}\rightarrow S

where
n\in N^{+}
is invertible in
T
.

Démonstration.

Any open subscheme of
X
is again a Kummer étale cover since
\underline{S}
is strictly local.
Hence,
owing to Theorem
A.6.2
,
we may assume that
f
admits an fs chart
\theta:P\rightarrow Q
with the following properties.

(i)

A naturally induced strict étale morphism

p:X\rightarrow S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}.

(ii)

The kernel and cokernel of
\theta^{\rm gp}:P^{\rm gp}\rightarrow Q^{\rm gp}
is finite, and the orders are invertible in
S
.

Since
f
is Kummer, (ii) implies that
Q\subset P^{1/n}
for some
n
invertible in
S
.
Replacing
X
by
X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{Q}}\mathbb{A}_{P^{1/n}}
, we may assume that
Q=P^{1/n}
.
Then
k[Q]
is a locally free
k[P]
-module so that
\mathbb{A}_{Q}
is finite over
\mathbb{A}_{P}
.
Thus
S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}
is finite over
\mathbb{A}_{P}
.
This shows that the underlying scheme of
S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}
is strictly henselian since
\underline{S}
is strictly henselian, and
p
admits a section.
It follows that
S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}
is a refinement of
X
.
∎

Lemma A.12.6
.

Suppose that
S
is an fs log scheme.
Let
f:X\rightarrow S
be a morphism of saturated log schemes and let
g:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
be a Kummer étale morphism of fs log schemes.
If
X
is log strictly henselian, then the projection
X\times_{S}S^{\prime}\rightarrow X
has a section.

Démonstration.

We may assume that
X
is local.
Replacing
\underline{S}
by its strict henselization at the image of the closed point of
X
, we may assume that
\underline{S}
is strictly local.
Then since
S
is local,
f
admits an fs chart
\theta:P\rightarrow Q
.
Owing to Lemma
A.12.5
we may assume that
S^{\prime}=S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{P^{1/n}}
where
n>0
is an integer invertible in
k
.
There is a naturally induced commutative diagram of saturated monoids

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{1/n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{1/n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

By the assumption on
Q
the upper horizontal morphism is an isomorphism.
This means that the homomorphism
Q\rightarrow Q\oplus_{P}P^{1/n}
has a retraction, so the projection
X\times_{S}S^{\prime}\rightarrow X
has a section.
∎

A.13.
Sharpened fans

In this subsection, we review the notion of Kato’s fan in
[
56
]
, which we call a sharpened fan, and we study basic properties.
A sharpened fan contains less information and is more flexible than an fs monoscheme.

Definition A.13.1
.

For a monoidal space
\Sigma=(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}_{\Sigma})
, we set

\overline{\Sigma}:=(\Sigma,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Sigma}).

For a morphism of monoidal spaces
f:\Sigma^{\prime}\rightarrow\Sigma
, let

\overline{f}:\overline{\Sigma^{\prime}}\rightarrow\overline{\Sigma}

denote the naturally induced morphism of sharpened fans.

For an fs monoid
P
, we set

\overline{\rm Spec}(P):=\overline{\operatorname{Spec}(P)}.

Note that
\overline{\rm Spec}(P)\simeq\overline{\rm Spec}(\overline{P})
.

Definition A.13.2
.

A
sharpened fan

\Delta
is a monoidal space such that there exists an open cover
\{U_{i}\}
with an isomorphism

U_{i}\simeq\overline{\rm Spec}(P_{i}),

where
P_{i}
is a sharp fs monoid.
A morphism of sharpened fans is a morphism of monoidal spaces.
A
cone
of
\Delta
is an open subset of
\Delta
that is isomorphic to
\overline{\rm Spec}(P)
for some sharp fs monoid
P
.
Note that there is a one-to-one correspondence between cones of
\Delta
and points of
\Delta
.

We say that
\Delta
is
affine
if there is an isomorphism
\Delta\simeq\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P)
for some sharp fs monoid
P
.
We say that
\Delta
is
smooth
if every cone of
\Delta
is isomorphic to
\overline{\rm Spec}(\mathbb{N}^{r})
for some
r
.

For any property
\mathbf{P}
of maps of topological spaces, we say that a morphism of sharpened fan satisfies
\mathbf{P}
if the underlying map of topological spaces satisfies
\mathbf{P}
.

Remark A.13.3
.

The reader may want to compare the above with the related notions of a conical polyhedral complex with an integral structure, a fan satisfying (
S_{fan}
), and an s-fan,
see
[
59
, Definitions II.5 and II.6]
,
[
56
, 9.4]
, and
[
76
, Remark II.1.9.4]
.

In the following, we establish some basic properties for sharpened fans.
We refer to
[
76
, Sections II.1.2, II.1.3]
for the monoscheme versions.

Proposition A.13.4
.

Let
P
be a sharp fs monoid, and let
\Delta
be a sharpened fan.
Then there is a canonical bijection

\alpha:\operatorname{Hom}(\Delta,\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P))\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}(P,\Gamma(\Delta,\mathcal{M}_{\Delta})).

Démonstration.

We define
\alpha
by the morphism sending
f:\Delta\rightarrow\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P)
to its associated homomorphism on the global sections

P\simeq\Gamma(\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P),\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P)})\rightarrow\Gamma(\Delta,\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}).

Suppose that
\Delta=\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(Q)
for some sharp monoid
Q
.
Then
\alpha
has an inverse

\operatorname{Hom}(P,Q)\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(Q),\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P))

sending
g:P\rightarrow Q
to
\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(g):\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(Q)\rightarrow\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P)
.
Thus
\alpha
is bijective in this case.

In the general case, let
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be the set of cones of
\Delta
, and let
\{\sigma_{ijk}\}_{k\in I_{ij}}
be the set of cones of
\sigma_{i}\cap\sigma_{j}
where
i,j\in I
.
There is a naturally induced diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}(\Delta,\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\amalg_{i}\operatorname{Hom}(\sigma_{i},\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\amalg_{i,j,k}\operatorname{Hom}(\sigma_{ijk},\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}(P,\Gamma(\Delta,\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\amalg_{i}\operatorname{Hom}(P,\Gamma(\sigma_{i},\mathcal{M}_{\sigma_{i}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\amalg_{i,j,k}\operatorname{Hom}(P,\Gamma(\sigma_{i},\mathcal{M}_{\sigma_{i}})).}

Here the horizontal diagrams are equalizers.
The middle and right vertical morphisms are bijections by the above.
Thus the left vertical morphism is a bijection.
∎

On the category of sharpened fans, the
Zariski topology
is the smallest Grothendieck topology containing all open covers.
The Yoneda lemma allows us to identify any sharpened fan with its representable sheaf.

Lemma A.13.5
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a sheaf of sets on the category of sharpened fans,
and suppose that
\{\Delta_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\}_{i\in I}
is a set of morphisms from sharpened fans
\Delta_{i}
satisfying the following conditions for every morphism

\Psi\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
, where
\Psi
is a sharpened fan.

(i)

For every
i\in I
, the sheaf
\Delta_{i}\times_{\mathcal{F}}\Psi
is representable, and the projection

\Delta_{i}\times_{\mathcal{F}}\Psi\rightarrow\Psi

is an open immersion.

(ii)

The set
\{\Delta_{i}\times_{\mathcal{F}}\Psi\rightarrow\Psi\}_{i\in I}
is a Zariski cover.

Then
\mathcal{F}
is representable.

Démonstration.

For
\Delta_{ij}:=\Delta_{i}\times_{\mathcal{F}}\Delta_{j}
for
i,j\in I
,
the projections
\Delta_{ij}\rightarrow\Delta_{i},\Delta_{j}
are open immersions by the condition (i).
Hence we can glue the morphisms
\Delta_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
for
i\in I
to obtain a morphism

f\colon\Delta\rightarrow\mathcal{F}

from a sharpened fan
\Delta
.

It remains to show that
f
is an isomorphism.
For this purpose, we need to show that for every morphism
\Psi\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
from a sharpened fan, the projection

f_{\Psi}\colon\Delta\times_{\mathcal{F}}\Psi\rightarrow\Psi

is an isomorphism.
The sharpened fan
\Delta\times_{\mathcal{F}}\Psi
is again the gluing of
\Delta_{i}\times_{\mathcal{F}}\Psi
for
i\in I
along
\Delta_{ij}\times_{\mathcal{F}}\Psi
for
i,j\in I
.
The condition (ii) shows that
f_{\Psi}
is an isomorphism.
∎

Lemma A.13.6
.

Let
P\rightarrow Q
and
P\rightarrow P^{\prime}
be homomorphisms of sharp, saturated monoids.
Then the fiber product

\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(Q)\times_{\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P)}\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P^{\prime})

is representable by
\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\overline{Q^{\prime}})
, where
Q^{\prime}
is the amalgamated sum
Q\oplus_{P}P^{\prime}
in the category of saturated monoids.

Démonstration.

Since
Q^{\prime}
is the amalgamated sum, for every saturated monoid
M
, there is a bijection

\operatorname{Hom}(Q^{\prime},M)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}(Q,M)\times_{\operatorname{Hom}(P,M)}\operatorname{Hom}(Q,M).

If
M
is sharp,
then
\operatorname{Hom}(Q^{\prime},M)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}(\overline{Q^{\prime}},M)
, and hence there is a bijection

\operatorname{Hom}(\overline{Q^{\prime}},M)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}(Q,M)\times_{\operatorname{Hom}(P,M)}\operatorname{Hom}(Q,M).

Specializing the above to
M=\Gamma(\Delta,\mathcal{M}_{\Delta})
, where
\Delta
is any sharpened fan,
and applying Proposition
A.13.4
concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition A.13.7
.

The category of sharpened fans has fiber products.

Démonstration.

Let
f:\Psi\rightarrow\Delta
and
g:\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
be morphisms of sharpened fans.
Take the fiber product
\Psi^{\prime}:=\Psi\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime}
in the category of sheaves.
We need to show that
\Psi^{\prime}
is representable.
Let
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be the set of cones of
\Delta
.
For every
i\in I
, let

\{\tau_{ij}\}_{j\in J_{i}}\text{ and }\{\sigma_{ik}^{\prime}\}_{k\in K_{i}}

be the set of cones of
\Psi
and
\Delta^{\prime}
.
Owing to Lemma
A.13.6
, we see that

\tau_{ijk}^{\prime}:=\tau_{ij}\times_{\sigma_{i}}\sigma_{ik}^{\prime}

is representable for every
i
,
j
, and
k
.
Moreover, for every morphism
\Theta\rightarrow\Psi^{\prime}
of sheaves, where
\Theta
is a sharpened fan, there is an isomorphism

(\amalg_{i,j,k}\tau_{ijk}^{\prime})\times_{\Psi^{\prime}}\Theta\simeq\amalg_{i,j,k}(\tau_{ij}\times_{\Psi}\Theta)\times_{\sigma_{i}\times_{\Delta}\Theta}(\sigma_{ik}^{\prime}\times_{\Delta^{\prime}}\Theta).

The latter is representable and also a cover of
\Theta
.
Thus Lemma
A.13.5
shows that
\Psi^{\prime}
is representable.
∎

Lemma A.13.8
.

Let
\alpha
be the functor from the category of fs monoschemes to the category of sharpened fans sending any monoscheme
\Sigma
to
\overline{\Sigma}
.
Then
\alpha
preserves fiber products.

Démonstration.

Suppose that
\theta:P\rightarrow Q
and
\varphi:P\rightarrow P^{\prime}
are homomorphisms of fs monoids.
Let
Q^{\prime\prime}
the amalgamated sum
\overline{P^{\prime}}\oplus_{\overline{P}}\overline{Q}
in the category of saturated monoids.
Observe that

\operatorname{Spec}(P^{\prime})\times_{\operatorname{Spec}(P)}\operatorname{Spec}(Q)\simeq\operatorname{Spec}(P^{\prime}\oplus_{P}Q),

and

\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\overline{P})\times_{\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\overline{P})}\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\overline{Q})\simeq\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\overline{Q^{\prime\prime}}).

There is a naturally induced commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{\prime}\oplus_{P}Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{P^{\prime}}\oplus_{\overline{P}}\overline{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{P^{\prime}\oplus_{P}Q},}

which induces a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{P^{\prime}\oplus_{P}Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{Q^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{P^{\prime}\oplus_{P}Q}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rm id}

The homomorphism
\overline{P^{\prime}\oplus_{P}Q}\rightarrow\overline{Q^{\prime\prime}}
is surjective since the homomorphism
P^{\prime}\oplus_{P}Q\rightarrow Q^{\prime\prime}
is surjective.
It follows that the homomorphism
\overline{P^{\prime}\oplus_{P}Q}\rightarrow\overline{Q^{\prime\prime}}
is an isomorphism.
We deduce that there is a canonical isomorphism

\alpha(\operatorname{Spec}(P^{\prime})\times_{\operatorname{Spec}(P)}\operatorname{Spec}(Q))\rightarrow\alpha(\operatorname{Spec}(P^{\prime}))\times_{\alpha(\operatorname{Spec}(P))}\alpha(\operatorname{Spec}(Q)).

(A.13.1)

Suppose that
\Theta\rightarrow\Sigma
and
\Sigma^{\prime}\rightarrow\Sigma
be morphisms of fs monoschemes.
There is an open cover
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
\Sigma
, an open cover
\{\tau_{ij}\}_{j\in J_{i}}
for every
i\in I
, and an open cover
\{\sigma_{ik}^{\prime}\}_{k\in K_{i}}
for every
i\in I
such that
\sigma_{i}
,
\tau_{ij}
, and
\sigma_{ik}^{\prime}
are affine for every
i
,
j
, and
k
.
Due to (
A.13.1
), we have isomorphisms

\alpha(\tau_{ij}\times_{\sigma_{i}}\sigma_{ik}^{\prime})\simeq\alpha(\tau_{ij})\times_{\alpha(\sigma_{i})}\alpha(\sigma_{ik}^{\prime}).

Gluing these isomorphisms concludes the proof.
∎

Next, we explain how to construct an fs monoscheme from the data of a sharpened fan.
We begin by proving the following lemma.

Lemma A.13.9
.

Let
\Sigma
be an fs monoscheme.
Then the map

\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{N}),\Sigma)\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\mathbb{N}),\overline{\Sigma})

(A.13.2)

sending
f
to
\overline{f}
is bijective.

Démonstration.

If
g:\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\mathbb{N})\rightarrow\overline{\Sigma}
is a morphism, then the image of
g
lies in a cone of
\overline{\Sigma}
containing the image of the closed point of
\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\mathbb{N})
.
Hence we are reduced to the case
\Sigma=\operatorname{Spec}(P)
, where
P
is an fs monoid.

There is a commutative diagram of sets with vertical bijections

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{N}),\operatorname{Spec}(P))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\mathbb{N}),\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\overline{P}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}(P,\mathbb{N})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}(\overline{P},\mathbb{N}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

where
\mu
sends
g:P\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
to
\overline{g}:\overline{P}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
.
Every homomorphism
g:P\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
maps the unit group
P^{*}
maps to
0
.
This shows that
\mu
is bijective.
∎

Construction A.13.10
.

Let
\Delta=(\Delta,\mathcal{M}_{\Delta})
be a sharpened fan.
Suppose that
N^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}^{\rm gp}
is a surjective morphism of sheaves,
where
N
is a lattice with dual
N^{\vee}
.
Then

\Delta_{N}:=(\Delta,N^{\vee}\times_{\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}^{\rm gp}}\mathcal{M}_{\Delta})

is a monoidal space.
Moreover,
\Delta_{N}
is toric, and there is an isomorphism
\overline{\Delta_{N}}\simeq\Delta
.

Let us check that
\Delta_{N}
is an fs monoscheme.
For this purpose, we may assume that

\Delta=\overline{\rm Spec}(P),

where
P
is a sharp fs monoid.
Let
x
be a point of
\Delta
corresponding to a face
F
of
P
.
Since
\mathcal{M}_{\Delta,x}\simeq P/F
, there is an isomorphism

\mathcal{M}_{\Delta_{N},x}\simeq N^{\vee}\times_{(P/F)^{\rm gp}}(P/F).

Applying
[
76
, Proposition I.4.2.1(1)]
to
P_{F}
we obtain the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P/F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(P_{F})^{\mathrm{gp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(P/F)^{\mathrm{gp}}.}

It follows that

\mathcal{M}_{\Delta_{N},x}\simeq N^{\vee}\times_{(P_{F})^{\mathrm{gp}}}P_{F}\simeq N^{\vee}\times_{P^{\mathrm{gp}}}P_{F},

which is a localization of the monoid

P_{N}:=N^{\vee}\times_{P^{\rm gp}}P

with respect to the face
F_{N}:=N^{\vee}\times_{P^{\rm gp}}F
.
Thus there is an isomorphism

\Delta_{N}\simeq\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P_{N}).

This proves the claim.

Suppose that
\Delta
is connected.
Then
\Delta_{N}
is also connected so that it has a unique generic point
\xi
, see
[
76
, p. 198]
.
It follows that

\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}),\Delta_{N})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{M}_{\Delta_{N},\xi}^{\mathrm{gp}},\mathbb{Z})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}(N^{\vee},\mathbb{Z})\simeq N.

We obtain the composite map

\varphi_{\Delta,N}:\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\mathbb{N}),\Delta)\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{N}),\Delta_{N})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}),\Delta_{N})\simeq N,

(A.13.3)

where the first map is the inverse of (
A.13.2
), and the second map is induced by the homomorphism
\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
.

Proposition A.13.11

(
[
56
, Section 9.5]
)
.

Let
\Delta
be a connected sharpened fan whose underlying topological space is a finite set.
Suppose that
N^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{gp}}
is a surjective morphism of sheaves, where
N
is a lattice with dual
N^{\vee}
.
If the morphism

\varphi_{\Delta,N}:\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\mathbb{N}),\Delta)\rightarrow N

is injective, then the associated fs monoscheme
\Delta_{N}
is a fan.

Démonstration.

Owing to Theorem
A.3.9
we need to show that
\Delta_{N}
is toric and separated.
In Construction
A.13.10
we noted that
\Delta_{N}
is toric.
If
\varphi_{\Delta,N}
is injective, then the homomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{N}),\Delta_{N})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}),\Delta_{N})

is injective.
This means that
\Delta_{N}
is separated.
∎

Example A.13.12
.

Suppose that
\Sigma
is an fs monoscheme.
Then
\Delta:=\overline{\Sigma}
is a sharpened fan.
If
\Sigma
is a fan in a lattice
N
, then
\mathcal{M}_{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{gp}}\simeq N^{\vee}
.
Thus the naturally induced morphism of sheaves
\mathcal{M}_{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{gp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{gp}}
gives a surjective morphism of sheaves

N^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{gp}}.

Applying
[
76
, Proposition I.4.2.1]
to
\mathcal{M}_{\Sigma}
we deduce the isomorphism

\mathcal{M}_{\Sigma}\simeq N^{\vee}\times_{\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{gp}}}\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}.

This shows
\Sigma\simeq\Delta_{N}
, where
\Delta_{N}
is the fs monoscheme in Construction
A.13.10
.

As in the case of fans, there are notions of subdivision and partial subdivision of sharpened fans as follows.

Definition A.13.13
.

Let
f:\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
be a morphism of sharpened fans.
We say that
f
is a
subdivision
(resp.
partial subdivision
) if the following conditions are satisfied.

(i)

For any
x^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{M}_{\Delta,f(x^{\prime})}^{\rm gp}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\Delta^{\prime},x^{\prime}}^{\rm gp}
is surjective.

(ii)

For any
x\in\Delta
,
f^{-1}(x)
is a finite set.

(iii)

The map

\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\mathbb{N}),\Delta^{\prime})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\mathbb{N}),\Delta)

is bijective (resp. injective).

Remark A.13.14
.

In
[
56
, Definition 9.7]
a subdivision in our sense is called a proper subdivision.

We note that compositions of subdivisions (resp. proper subdivisions) are again subdivisions (resp. proper subdivisions).

Lemma A.13.15
.

Any subdivision
f:\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
of sharpened fans is surjective.

Démonstration.

Let
x
be a point of
\Delta
, which is the generic point of a cone
\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P)
of
\Delta
,
where
P
is a sharp fs monoid.
By appeal to
[
76
, Proposition I.2.2.1]
there exists a homomorphism
p:P\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
such that
p^{-1}(0)=0
.
Let
y
be the generic point of
\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\mathbb{N})
, and set
h:=\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(p)
.
Then we have that
h(y)=x
.
By condition (iii) in Definition
A.13.13
there is a morphism
g:\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\mathbb{N})\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
such that
f\circ g=h
.
It follows that
f(g(y))=h(y)=x
, and hence
f
is surjective.
∎

Lemma A.13.16
.

Let
f:\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
and
g:\Delta^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
be morphisms of sharpened fans.
If
g
and
f\circ g
are subdivisions, then
f
is a subdivision.

Démonstration.

Since
g
is surjective by Lemma
A.13.15
, the condition (i) (resp. (ii)) in Definition
A.13.13
for
f\circ g
implies the condition (i) (resp. (ii)) for
f
.
Condition (iii) for
g
and
f\circ g
implies the condition (iii) for
f
.
∎

Lemma A.13.17
.

If
f\colon\Sigma^{\prime}\rightarrow\Sigma
is a subdivision (resp. partial subdivision) of fans,
then
\overline{f}:\overline{\Sigma^{\prime}}\rightarrow\overline{\Sigma}
is a subdivision (resp. partial subdivision) of sharpened fans.

Démonstration.

Suppose that
f
is a
\Sigma
is in a lattice
N
.
Let
x
be a point of
\Sigma
corresponding to a cone
\sigma
.
If
x^{\prime}
is a point of
\Sigma^{\prime}
such that
f(x^{\prime})=x
, then there are isomorphisms

\mathcal{M}_{\Sigma^{\prime},x^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{gp}}\simeq\mathcal{M}_{\Sigma,x}^{\mathrm{gp}}\simeq N^{\vee}.

It follows that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Sigma^{\prime},x^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{gp}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Sigma,x}^{\mathrm{gp}}
is surjective, which is condition (i) in Definition
A.13.13
.
There are only finitely many cones in
\Sigma^{\prime}
.
This shows that condition (ii) holds.
Since
\lvert\Delta^{\prime}\rvert\rightarrow\lvert\Delta\rvert
is bijective (resp. injective), we deduce condition (iii).
∎

Proposition A.13.18
.

Let
\Sigma
be a fan.
Then for every subdivision (resp. partial subdivision)
f:\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta:=\overline{\Sigma}
of sharpened fans,
there exists a subdivision (resp. partial subdivision) of fans
g:\Sigma^{\prime}\rightarrow\Sigma
such that
\overline{g}\simeq f
.

Démonstration.

Suppose that
f
is a partial subdivision.
We may suppose that
\Sigma
lies in a lattice
N
.
As observed in Example
A.13.12
, there is a surjective morphism of sheaves
N^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{gp}}
such that
\Sigma\simeq\Delta_{N}
.
Then we have a composite morphism of sheaves

N^{\vee}\simeq f^{*}N^{\vee}\rightarrow f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}^{\rm gp}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\Delta^{\prime}}^{\rm gp},

which is surjective because of condition (i).
This gives a morphism of fs monoschemes
g:\Delta_{N}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta_{N}
such that
\overline{g}\simeq f
.

Let us check that
\Delta_{N}^{\prime}
is a fan.
Condition (ii) implies that the underlying topological space of
\Delta^{\prime}
is a finite set.
Moreover, condition (iii) implies that the map in (
A.13.3
)

\varphi_{\Delta^{\prime},N}:\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\mathbb{N}),\Delta^{\prime})\rightarrow N

is injective.
Proposition
A.13.11
shows that
\Delta_{N}^{\prime}
is a fan.
Since
\Delta
and
\Delta^{\prime}
are in the same lattice
N
, we deduce that
g
is a partial subdivision.

If
f
is a subdivision, then condition (iii) implies that the map

\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{N}),\Delta_{N}^{\prime})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{N}),\Delta_{N})

is bijective by Lemma
A.13.9
.
Proposition
A.3.13
shows that
g
is a subdivision.
∎

Definition A.13.19
.

There is a notion of star subdivision for smooth sharpened fans as in the case of toric fans, see Definition
A.3.14
.
Let
\tau=\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(Q)
be a cone of a smooth sharpened fan
\Delta
where
Q
is a sharp fs monoid.
For any cone
\sigma=\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P)
of
\Delta
containing
\tau
where
P
is a sharp fs monoid, consider the star subdivision
\Theta
of
\operatorname{Spec}(P)
relative to
\operatorname{Spec}(Q)
.
Replace
\sigma
by
\overline{\Theta}
for every
\sigma
, then we obtain a new smooth sharpened fan
\Delta^{*}(\tau)
.
This is called the
star subdivision
of
\Delta
relative to
\tau
.

Lemma A.13.20
.

Every partial subdivision
f\colon\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
of sharpened fans is a monomorphism in the category of sharpened fans.

Démonstration.

We show that the diagonal morphism
\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime}
is an isomorphism.
To that end, it suffices to check that for every cone
\sigma
of
\Delta
, the induced morphism

\Delta^{\prime}\times_{\Delta}\sigma\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime}\times_{\Delta}\sigma

is an isomorphism.
Hence, we reduce to the case when
\Delta=\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P)
for some sharp fs monoid
P
.
In this case, due to Proposition
A.13.18
, there exists a partial subdivision
g\colon\Sigma^{\prime}\rightarrow\Sigma:=\operatorname{Spec}(P)
of fans together with an isomorphism
\overline{g}\simeq f
.
Lemma
A.3.12
implies
g
is a monomorphism.
By appeal to Lemma
A.13.8
we deduce that
f
is a monomorphism.
∎

Lemma A.13.21
.

Let
f\colon\Psi\rightarrow\Delta
be a subdivision (resp. partial subdivision) of sharpened fans, and
g\colon\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
a partial subdivision of sharpened fans.
Then the projection
f^{\prime}\colon\Psi^{\prime}:=\Psi\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
is a subdivision (resp. partial subdivision).

Démonstration.

The question is local on
\Delta
, so we may assume that
\Delta
is affine.
Owing to Proposition
A.13.18
there exists a subdivision (resp. partial subdivision) of sharpened fans
u\colon\Theta\rightarrow\Sigma
and a partial subdivision

v\colon\Sigma^{\prime}\rightarrow\Sigma
of sharpened fans such that
\overline{u}\simeq f
and
\overline{v}\simeq g
.
Let
u^{\prime}\colon\Theta^{\prime}:=\Theta\times_{\Sigma}\Sigma^{\prime}\rightarrow\Sigma^{\prime}
be the projection.
Lemma
A.3.11
shows that
u^{\prime}
is a subdivision (resp. partial subdivision).
By Lemma
A.13.8
we have
\overline{u^{\prime}}\simeq f^{\prime}
.
Due to Lemma
A.13.17
we can conclude that
f^{\prime}
is a subdivision (resp. partial subdivision).
∎

Lemma A.13.22
.

Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
and
g\colon Z\rightarrow Y
be monomorphisms in a category
\mathcal{C}
with fiber products.
Then the projection

f^{\prime}\colon Y\times_{X}Z\rightarrow Z

is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{C}
.

Démonstration.

There is a naturally induced commutative diagram with cartesian squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\times_{X}Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times_{X}Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces fg}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

Since the composite
fg
is a monomorphism, it follows that
f^{\prime}g^{\prime}
is an isomorphism.
This shows that
f^{\prime}
is an epimorphism.
Similarly, again since the pullback of a monomorphism is a monomorphism, it follows that
f^{\prime}
is a monomorphism.
Thus
f^{\prime}
is an isomorphism.
∎

Lemma A.13.23
.

Let
p\colon\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
be a subdivision of smooth sharpened fans.
If the underlying topological space of
\Delta
is a finite set, then there exists a sharpened fan
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
obtained by a finite succession of star subdivisions from
\Delta
such that the morphism
\Delta^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
factors through
p
.

Démonstration.

Let
\{\sigma_{1}=\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P_{1}),\ldots,\sigma_{n}=\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(P_{n})\}
be all the cones of
\Delta
, where
P_{1},\ldots,P_{n}
are sharp fs monoids.
We will inductively construct subdivisions

\Delta_{n}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\Delta_{0}:=\Delta

such that for every
1\leq i\leq n
the projection

\Delta^{\prime}\times_{\Delta}\Delta_{i}\times_{\Delta}\sigma_{i}\rightarrow\Delta_{i}\times_{\Delta}\sigma_{i}

(A.13.4)

is an isomorphism.

Suppose that we have constructed
\Delta_{i-1}
.
By Lemma
A.13.21
the projection

\Delta^{\prime}\times_{\Delta}\Delta_{i-1}\times_{\Delta}\sigma_{i}\rightarrow\Delta_{i-1}\times_{\Delta}\sigma_{i}

is a subdivision of sharpened fans.
Proposition
A.13.11
shows there exist subdivisions
g:\Sigma\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P_{i})
and
h:\Sigma^{\prime}\rightarrow\Sigma
of fans such that
\overline{g}
and
\overline{h}

are isomorphic to the projections

\Delta_{i-1}\times_{\Delta}\sigma_{i}\rightarrow\sigma_{i}\text{ and }\Delta^{\prime}\times_{\Delta}\Delta_{i-1}\times_{\Delta}\sigma_{i}\rightarrow\Delta_{i-1}\times_{\Delta}\sigma_{i}.

Due to Lemma
A.3.15
there is a subdivision
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
of
\Sigma
obtained by a finite succession of star subdivisions from
\Sigma
such that
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow\Sigma

factors through
\Sigma^{\prime}
.
Since the underlying topological space of
\Sigma
is an open subset of the underlying topological space of
\Delta_{i-1}
,
we can take the corresponding finite succession of star subdivisions from
\Delta_{i-1}
to obtain a subdivision
\Delta_{i}
of
\Delta_{i-1}
.
Then the morphism

\Delta_{i}\times_{\Delta}\sigma_{i}\rightarrow\Delta_{i-1}\times_{\Delta}\sigma_{i}

is a subdivision and factors through
\Delta^{\prime}\times_{\Delta}\Delta_{i-1}\times_{\Delta}\sigma_{i}
.
The projection

\Delta^{\prime}\times_{\Delta}\Delta_{i-1}\times_{\Delta}\sigma_{i}\rightarrow\Delta_{i-1}\times_{\Delta}\sigma_{i}

is a subdivision by Lemma
A.13.21
.
Now use Lemmas
A.13.20
and
A.13.22
to deduce that the morphism (
A.13.4
) is an isomorphism.
This completes the construction of
\Delta_{i}
and hence, by induction,
\Delta_{n}
.

From (
A.13.4
) we see that for every
1\leq i\leq n
the projection

\Delta_{n}\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime}\times_{\Delta}\sigma_{i}\rightarrow\Delta_{n}\times_{\Delta}\sigma_{i}

is an isomorphism.
It follows that the projection
\Delta_{n}\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta_{n}
is an isomorphism since
\{\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}\}
comprises all the cones of
\Delta
.
Hence the morphism
\Delta_{n}\rightarrow\Delta
factors through
\Delta^{\prime}
.
∎

A.14.
Frames of fs log schemes

Let
X
be an fs log scheme.
If there exists a fan
\Sigma
with a strict morphism

X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma},

then any partial subdivision
\Sigma^{\prime}
of
\Sigma
produces a new fs log scheme
X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
.
Alas, it is unclear whether such a fan
\Sigma
always exists in the category of fs log schemes.
For fs log schemes in
lSm/k
, we shall proceed with employing sharpened fans.
As an application, we will show that every log modification
Y\rightarrow X
in
SmlSm/k
admits a factorization into log modifications

Y_{n}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow Y_{1}\rightarrow Y_{0}:=X

such that for every
1\leq i\leq n
, the morphism

\underline{Y_{i}}\rightarrow\underline{Y_{i-1}}

is a blow-up along a smooth center.

Definition A.14.1
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme.
A
preframe
of
X
is a sharpened fan
\Delta
with a morphism of monoidal spaces
s\colon(X,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})\rightarrow\Delta
.

A preframe
\Delta
of
X
is called a
frame
of
X
if the following condition is satisfied :
Locally on
X
and
\Delta
,
there exists a chart
t\colon(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
with an fs monoid
P
such that
\overline{t}
is isomorphic to
s\colon(X,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})\rightarrow\Delta
.
Such a frame is an
s
-frame in the sense of
[
76
, Definition III.1.12.1]
.

Proposition A.14.2
.

For every
X\in lSm/k
we can associate a frame

s_{X}:(X,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})\rightarrow\Delta_{X}

satisfying the following properties.

(i)

Every preframe of
X
uniquely factors through
s_{X}
.

(ii)

The morphism of monoidal spaces
s_{X}
is open and surjective.

(iii)

For every morphism
f:X\rightarrow Y
in
lSm/k
,
there exists a unique morphism of sharpened fans
\Delta_{f}:\Delta_{X}\rightarrow\Delta_{Y}
such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{f}}

commutes.

Démonstration.

Owing to
[
76
, Theorems III.1.11.1, IV.3.5.1]
,
X
is very solid in the sense of
[
76
, Definition III.1.10.1]
.
Then apply
[
76
, Proposition III.1.12.3]
and its following paragraph.
∎

Lemma A.14.3
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact fs log scheme in
lSm/k
.
Then the underlying space of the associated sharpened fan
\Delta_{X}
is a finite set.

Démonstration.

There exists a finite Zariski cover
\{U_{i}\}
of
X
with the property that there exists a chart
t_{i}\colon(U_{i},\mathcal{M}_{U_{i}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P_{i})
such that the composite morphism

(U_{i},\underline{\mathcal{M}}_{U_{i}})\rightarrow(X,\underline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s_{X}}}{{\rightarrow}}\Delta_{X}

factors through
\overline{t_{i}}
.
Since the underlying topological space of
\operatorname{Spec}(P_{i})
is a finite set, we can use property (ii) of Proposition
A.14.2
to conclude.
∎

Construction A.14.4
.

Suppose
X
is an fs log scheme with a frame

\pi:(X,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})\rightarrow\Delta,

and let
p:\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
be a partial subdivision of sharpened fans.
Let
\mathcal{C}_{X,\Delta^{\prime},\Delta}
be the category of pairs
(f,\pi^{\prime})
, where
f\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
is a morphism of fs log schemes and

\pi^{\prime}:(X^{\prime},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{\prime}})\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}

is a morphism of monoidal spaces such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

commutes.
In
[
56
, Proposition 9.9]
, it is shown that
\mathcal{C}_{X,\Delta^{\prime},\Delta}
admits a final object

X\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime}.

The fiber product notation is used for convenience ; technically, it is not a fiber product.
Moreover,
the morphism
\pi^{\prime}
gives a framing of
X^{\prime}
.

Let us explain the local description of
X^{\prime}
in
[
56
, Proposition 9.9]
.
Suppose that
P
is an fs chart of
X
with a homomorphism
\theta:P\rightarrow Q
of fs monoids such that
\theta^{\rm gp}
is an isomorphism,

\Delta=\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\overline{P})
,
and
\Delta^{\prime}=\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\overline{Q})
.
In this case, we have

X\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime}\simeq X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}.

More generally,
suppose that
\Delta=\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\overline{P})
and
\Delta^{\prime}=\overline{\Sigma}
,
where
P
is an fs chart of
X
and
\Sigma
is a partial subdivision of
\operatorname{Spec}(P)
.
Then the local description gives an isomorphism

X\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime}\simeq X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}.

(A.14.1)

Lemma A.14.5
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme with a frame
\Delta
, and let
\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta
and
\Delta^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
be partial subdivisions of sharpened fans.
Then there is a canonical isomorphism

(X\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime})\times_{\Delta^{\prime}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}\simeq X\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime\prime}.

Démonstration.

We need to show that
(X\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime})\times_{\Delta^{\prime}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is a final object in the category
\mathcal{C}_{X,\Delta^{\prime\prime},\Delta}
.
Suppose that
f:X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
is a morphism of fs log schemes with a morphism of monoidal spaces
(X^{\prime},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{\prime}})\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime\prime}
such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}

commutes.
The universal property of
X\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime}
means that there is a unique morphism
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime}
such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{\prime}}

commutes.
Similarly,
the universal property of
(X\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime})\times_{\Delta^{\prime}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
means that there is a unique morphism

X^{\prime}\rightarrow(X\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime})\times_{\Delta^{\prime}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}

in
\mathcal{C}_{X,\Delta^{\prime\prime},\Delta}
.
This shows that
(X\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\prime})\times_{\Delta^{\prime}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is a final object in
\mathcal{C}_{X,\Delta^{\prime\prime},\Delta}
.
∎

Lemma A.14.6
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme in
lSm/k
.
Then for every subdivision of fans
p:\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta_{X}
,
the natural morphism

f:X\times_{\Delta_{X}}\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow X

is a log modification.

Démonstration.

The question is Zariski local on
X
, so we may assume that
X
admits an fs chart
t\colon X\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
such that
\overline{t}
is isomorphic to
s_{X}\colon(X,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})\rightarrow\Delta_{X}
.
Owing to Proposition
A.13.18
there exists a subdivision of fans
q:\Sigma^{\prime}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
such that
\overline{q}\simeq p
.
From the construction of
X\times_{\Delta_{X}}\Delta^{\prime}
we see that

X\times_{\Delta_{X}}\Delta^{\prime}\simeq X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\operatorname{Spec}(P)}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}=X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}.

This concludes the proof since the projection
X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\rightarrow X
is a log modification due to Example
A.11.8
.
∎

Lemma A.14.7
.

Suppose
X
is an fs log scheme in
lSm/k
with an fs chart
t\colon X\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
where
P
is an fs monoid.
For every subdivision
\Sigma\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
of fans, the associated morphism of sharpened fans

\Delta_{X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\rightarrow\Delta_{X}

is a subdivision.

Démonstration.

The question is Zariski local on
X
, so we may assume that
\overline{t}
is isomorphic to
s_{X}\colon(X,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})\rightarrow\Delta_{X}
.
Due to (
A.14.1
) there is an isomorphism

X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\simeq X\times_{\Delta_{X}}\overline{\Sigma}.

It follows that
\Delta_{X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\simeq\overline{\Sigma}
.
Since
\Sigma\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
is a subdivision,
\overline{\Sigma}\rightarrow\Delta_{X}
is a subdivision by Lemma
A.13.17
.
∎

Lemma A.14.8
.

For any log modification
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
in
lSm/k
,
the associated morphism
u\colon\Delta_{Y}\rightarrow\Delta_{X}
of sharpened fans is a subdivision.

Démonstration.

The question is Zariski local on
X
, so we may assume that
X
is quasi-compact and admits an fs chart
t\colon X\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
such that
\overline{t}
is isomorphic to

s_{X}\colon(X,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})\rightarrow\Delta_{X}
.
Owing to Proposition
A.11.5
there is a subdivision of fans
p:\Sigma\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
such that the pullback

Y\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\rightarrow X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}=:X^{\prime}

is an isomorphism.
Therefore we have associated morphisms
\Delta_{X^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle v}}{{\rightarrow}}\Delta_{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u}}{{\rightarrow}}\Delta_{X}
of sharpened fans.

We claim that
v
and
u\circ v
are subdivisions.
Owing to Lemma
A.11.3
there is a Zariski cover
\{U_{i}\}
of
Y
such that for every
i
the morphism
U_{i}\rightarrow X
admits an fs chart
\theta\colon P\rightarrow Q_{i}

such that
\theta^{\mathrm{gp}}\colon P^{\mathrm{gp}}\rightarrow Q_{i}^{\mathrm{gp}}
is an isomorphism.
This means that
\operatorname{Spec}(Q_{i})
is a partial subdivision of
\operatorname{Spec}(P)
.
Lemma
A.3.12
shows that the projection

\Sigma_{i}:=\operatorname{Spec}(Q_{i})\times_{\operatorname{Spec}(P)}\Sigma\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(Q_{i})

is a subdivision of fans.
Thus by Lemma
A.14.7
the associated morphism of sharpened fans

\Delta_{U_{i}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}}\simeq\Delta_{U_{i}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{Q_{i}}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma_{i}}}\rightarrow\Delta_{U_{i}}

is a subdivision.
It follows that
v
is a subdivision.
Moreover, Lemma
A.14.7
also shows that
u\circ v
is a subdivision.
As a consequence,
u
is a subdivision by Lemma
A.13.16
.
∎

Lemma A.14.9
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme in
SmlSm/k
.
Then the associated sharpened fan
\Delta_{X}
is smooth.

Démonstration.

The question is Zariski local on
X
,
so we may assume that
X
admits an fs chart
t\colon X\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
such that
\overline{t}
is isomorphic to
s_{X}\colon(X,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})\rightarrow\Delta_{X}
.
Lemma
A.5.9
shows that
P\simeq\mathbb{N}^{r}
for some integer
r\geq 0
.
Thus
\Delta_{X}\simeq\operatorname{\overline{\mathrm{Spec}}}(\mathbb{N}^{r})
is smooth.
∎

Definition A.14.10
.

For
X\in SmlSm/k
,
a
log modification along a smooth center
is a log modification
p:Y\rightarrow X
such that Zariski locally on
X
there exists a chart
X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}^{r}}

such that
p
is isomorphic to the projection

X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}^{r}}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}\to X.

Here
\Sigma
is the star subdivision of the dual of
\mathbb{N}^{r}
.

Lemma A.14.11
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme in
SmlSm/k
.
Then for every star subdivision of fans
p:\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Delta_{X}
, the natural morphism

f\colon X\times_{\Delta_{X}}\Delta^{\prime}\rightarrow X

is a log modification along a smooth center.

Démonstration.

The question is Zariski local on
X
,
so we may assume that
X
admits an fs chart
t\colon X\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(P)
such that
\overline{t}
is isomorphic to
s_{X}\colon(X,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})\rightarrow\Delta_{X}
.
Due to Lemma
A.14.9
,
P\simeq\mathbb{N}^{r}
for some integer
r\geq 0
.
By the definition of star subdivisions of sharpened fans, there exists a face
Q
of
P
such that
\Delta^{\prime}\simeq\overline{\Sigma}
,
where
\Sigma
is the star subdivision of
\operatorname{Spec}(P)
relative to
\operatorname{Spec}(Q)
.
Then (
A.14.1
) gives an isomorphism

X\times_{\Delta_{X}}\Delta^{\prime}\simeq X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{\Sigma}.

This shows that
f
is a log modification along a smooth center.
∎

Theorem A.14.12
.

Let
f:Y\rightarrow X
be a log modification of quasi-compact fs log schemes in
lSm/k
. If
X\in SmlSm/k
,
then there exists a sequence of log modifications along a smooth center

X_{n}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow X_{1}\rightarrow X

such that the projection
X_{n}\times_{X}Y\rightarrow X_{n}
is an isomorphism.

Démonstration.

Lemma
A.14.8
shows that the associated morphism
\Delta_{Y}\rightarrow\Delta_{X}
is a subdivision of sharpened fans.
By Lemma
A.14.11
the associated sharpened fan
\Sigma_{X}
is smooth.
Lemma
A.13.23
yields star subdivisions of smooth sharpened fans

\Delta_{n}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\Delta_{1}\rightarrow\Delta_{X}

such that the subdivision
\Delta_{n}\rightarrow\Delta_{X}
factors through
\Delta_{Y}\rightarrow\Delta_{X}
.
We set

X_{i}:=X\times_{\Delta_{X}}\Delta_{i}

for every
1\leq i\leq n
.
By definition
X_{n}\rightarrow X
factors through
f
,
and by Lemma
A.14.5
there is an isomorphism

X_{n}\simeq X_{n-1}\times_{\Delta_{n-1}}\Delta_{n}.

Lemma
A.14.11
shows that the morphism
X_{i}\rightarrow X_{i-1}
is a log modification along a smooth center for every
1\leq i\leq n
.
To show that the projection
X_{n}\times_{X}Y\rightarrow X_{n}
is an isomorphism, use Lemma
A.13.22
.
∎

This is used in the proof of the following result.
